## 10. Wave-25 day-two friction (DX8)

Implementer: Codex / Charlie Cheever, 2026-10-23. Commission: wave-25 fix list
8b #1–#13, building on DX7 and Integration 23/24. This amendment is a reviewed
implementation commission, not author acceptance or a publication claim.
The no-JavaScript migration, atomic activation, job-meter and row-lifetime
custody laws above still apply. Integration 23 owns rollback-view selection and
its trigger; SEC9 owns column-write authority, security gates and adversarial
campaign reach. DX8 changes neither of those lanes' policy.

### Migration interpretation and current-value renames

Backfill arguments and lookups read the assembled pre-patch snapshot, before
any simultaneous rename/backfill writes outputs. Historical argument names
therefore name predecessor fields. A declaration's immutable meaning is its
normalized typed-IR digest. Every changed-meaning refusal, both continuously
carried and after a historical gap, prints old and new full digests. A new name
is a new declaration; pruning does not reset names or immutable definitions.
Prune previews name known down projections whose native/reverse or overlapping
forward-preimages they discard. This warning is privileged operator output;
prune remains explicit and destructive, and unavailable evidence still refuses
subsequent reversal/re-entry. Preview never mutates live history.

For the generated `$rename:down` with identical declared old/new scalar types,
the current renamed cell outranks its non-null retained predecessor cell. This
is an identity spelling change, not a historical computation: v1 must see the
edited v2 quote, not the original quote. Stage it on the existing newer snapshot
and preserve native/reverse evidence in the same transaction. Repeated
rollback/forward cycles retain that current value. Different-type renames still
refuse even when sample values happen to fit. Authored down projections retain
their exact forward-preimage/non-null-native precedence; a generated rename
does not grant another declaration permission to replace protected cells.
Conflicting edits under an older view still require an explicit bridge where
existing receipt custody cannot reconcile them. The change stays isolated in
backfills.rs; it does not alter rollback views or release bridges.

### Operator processes, development clock and real accounts

`snapback4 stop <deployment-dir>` uses the existing same-owner authenticated
local release-control channel, then awaits the kernel lock-release barrier.
Already stopped is success. An unverifiable/live foreign owner refuses: never
unlink a lock or signal a guessed PID. Proof hosts/frontends/tool commands own
an explicit process group, including npm wrappers and children. Stop, failure
and parent exit release that entire owned group through live keeper/pipe
custody; cleanup is bounded and cannot silently leave a serving descendant.
Processes deliberately leaving custody are outside that promise. Scripts whose
stdout is one JSON line use `npm run --silent`; logs remain separate.

A password-authenticated development host exposes clock status/advance with
fixture/persona doors closed. It requires an exact full `service:operator`
bearer token minted through the existing local store authority. Ordinary users,
other seats, read-scoped sessions, unauthenticated requests and production
hosts cannot use it. Staging keeps its existing full-service-seat authorization;
explicit unsafe development retains its existing local fixture behavior.
Authenticated development jumps preserve live full/read session families and
worker leases by default in the same store transaction as the jump's durable
authority effects; expired/revoked families never revive. Explicit
`sessions: 'expire'` requests expiry testing. Header/SDK reconciliation keeps
current clients alive across the jump. The development offset is process-local
and returns to wall time on restart; staging retains DX7's persistent checkpoint
and paused-drain behavior. A failed/ambiguous jump is never automatically
replayed. `startDev` exposes operator-backed now/advance/drain without opening
fixture doors. Production uses wall time and refuses clock control.

`provisionAccounts` shares seedAccounts plan validation, generated credentials,
identity retry/deadline behavior, result shape, selected seat and provision
mutation. It accepts production and staging through real signup/login and
ordinary authorized mutations; it refuses development and uses no seed/system
bypass. Custom per-user args retain seedAccounts' existing merge precedence.
A minted service seat is only that exact principal, not a superuser. Warn when
its identity matches no schema-authorized identity; this is diagnostic, never
a promise of authority. The mutation's actual read and write rules decide.

### Inspection and saved artifacts

Default active sql/data/releases announce the retained dormant row count when
nonzero. Missing active-table errors identify a retained table and its row count
and name a concrete dormant inspection command. Inspection remains a privileged
read-only consistent snapshot, outside application rules. Only direct,
schema-proven media cells in `sql --json-objects` decode to their ordinary wire
JSON values; positional `--json` remains compatible. Truncation and dormancy
notices go to stderr, preserving JSON stdout; existing limit/more fields stay.

An identical-source deploy with --save-version can refresh an existing saved
version only if the complete Quarry source set and exact bytes match. Differing
source refuses. Generated client artifacts are emitted from that saved source's
Bound, independently of the working tree; fixtures/source evidence stay intact.
Refresh follows successful adoption/no-op and records the correct retained
release identity. Replaying an old source never silently emits the current API.

### Paging, proof assertions and contract advice

Board/roster starters and list recipes use newest-first `last N by .id`, while
explicit oldest-first examples remain labelled. The language's ordering default
does not change. Cursors are opaque evaluator-issued positions: send next back
unchanged; a row id/projection is not a cursor. `exhaustPages(readPage)` begins
with null, gathers array pages, follows next, and refuses denial/loading,
malformed/repeated cursors or its explicit page bound. It never turns a capped
or partial read into complete evidence. Projections change fields, not cursor
meaning/order/completeness.

Day-two recipes compare by stable id across stages and declared common fields,
not list position or whole differently-shaped row images. IDs created by a
frozen v1-client replay are recorded and excluded from preservation baselines.
V2-native IDs may be absent from an exact v1 view; they must reappear unchanged
on forward. Domain-specific synthesized old values need an authored projection
assertion, not an equality claim for incompatible representations.

renderedText(..., null) equals omitted options. Whitespace normalization,
case-sensitive exact matching by default, explicit substring matching and DX7's
legal scroll reachability remain unchanged. The lane-031 full-line regression
must match mounted reachable `Pump logged.` text, without widening hidden clips.
Write-authority hints explain that a mutation require and the caller's table
rule are separate gates. The captured-vs-live-parent card describes existing
lifetime/audience semantics; it grants no new authority.

Old-client INPUT BREAK advice follows accepted wire identities, including
references through declared table renames and lowered aliases/defaults. Real
removed/required/narrowed inputs still warn. Pure object-result field additions
are RESULT NOTE, not RESULT REVIEW or an adoption hazard; changed/removed fields
and domains retain review. These remain conservative advice, not a proof of
unchanged business behavior. No runtime input validation is weakened.

### Required evidence

Tests cover edited historical/native renames over repeated cycles, identical
and incompatible types, authored-down precedence, continuous/gapped digest
refusals and read-only prune warnings; authenticated dev clock with fixture
doors closed, real SDK sessions and expiry mode; graceful/foreign/idempotent
stop and npm descendant cleanup; production real-account provisioning and seat
warnings; media/truncation/dormancy inspection; saved-source client refresh;
additive results and renamed-reference input advice; paging progress/failures,
null/full-line rendering and newest-first starter execution. Every changed
guide/starter snippet runs as written against fresh packed artifacts.

Before completion, replay the relevant wave-25 commands on copied stores
without owner/token files and delete scratch. Run the requested five-crate,
client, difftest, packed Linux, wasm/docs/reference and root guide/inventory
checks. Report original failures and scoped reruns independently. Mac is skipped
per the coordinator; integration owns it. Both required independent families
must review the same frozen amendment as READY, with verbatim provenance.

# Snapback 4 — agent command sheet

Read this, then [LLP 3000](../llp/3000-snapback4.explainer.md) (the root and
the bakeoff protocol), [LLP 2000.000](../llp/2000.000-keep-the-core-flip-two-substrates.rfc.md)

adopted here as written; LLPs for Snapback 4 are numbered **3000 and up**
(`llp-create --under 3000` for a child; extend LLP 3000 or LLP 2000.000
before minting a new number).


Crates: `snapback4-core` (value model, schema with `sync`, IR, key encoding,
SQLite store, rules with hoisting, the meter, the interpreter, the partition
log, at-most-once, the day-two classifier, wire forms), `snapback4-lang`
(Quarry lexer, parser, binder, diagnostics, the TypeScript emitter),
`snapback4` (verbs and the HTTP wire), `snapback4-sim` (the deterministic
world hosting the engine and a replica device), `snapback4-difftest` (seeded
Quarry source generator, rewrite and meter oracles, optional Postgres store
oracles with prefetch on/off, the server-against-device oracle on both device stores, the engine-against-Lean-model oracle, structural shrinking and replay; not published or linked by shipped crates), `snapback4-device` (the
interpreter over a `DeviceStore`: the core's SQLite under the native
module for Expo and Exact2 hosts, memory under wasm on the web; one
dispatcher, one suite on both)). The guide (`snapback4-lang/src/guide.rs`) is
the language card and the keyword cards; packages: `snapback4` (the client
over the device and the CLI shim), `snapback4-darwin-arm64` and `snapback4-linux-x64` (the binaries), `snapback4-expo`
(the Swift module over the device's C ABI).

Gotchas you cannot derive from the code:

- Own Cargo and Bun workspaces; the repository root excludes them. Run cargo/bun from `snapback4/`. Node 24.x (`engines`): run the suite with the pinned runtime on PATH, not the ambient Homebrew node.
- Web builds require `bun install --frozen-lockfile` first. Binaryen 132.0.0 is
  an exact workspace devDependency; build/measurement invoke its portable
  `node_modules/binaryen/bin/wasm-opt` through Node, never a PATH optimizer.
  Missing/mismatched dependencies and optimizer failures refuse the build.
  See README's web-device build instructions and HV round 4 for host parity.
- The suite schedules client files in one pool: `max(1, min(24,
  floor(os.availableParallelism() / 3)))` slots (3 on 10 CPUs, 24 on 96).
  This counts Linux CPU affinity and leaves room for each file's servers,
  browser renderers and workers. Acquisition and server-e2e share that pool;
  there are no extra shard processes outside it. Real-timer files listed in
  `TIMER_CLIENT_FILES` drain that pool and run individually afterward with
  unchanged timing bounds. The quote-jump browser witness remains pooled.
  The two full-count model
  files retain a separate serial slot. `SNAPBACK4_TEST_SERIAL_CLIENT_FILES=1`
  still forces one client file at a time. The external file watchdog measures
  time since a subtest started or finished: max(900 seconds, largest single
  declared guard + max(60 seconds, 25%)). There is no absolute duration cap.
  Console output does not count as progress. Serial mode uses the same bounds.
  See `scripts/run-client-tests.mjs` and the QF round-2 guard census; update
  named work counts and recheck the census when declarations change. Every
  file runs as native `node --test` with default isolation, in a process group
  whose first member is a tiny Node keeper. The runner closes the keeper's stdin
  on completion, stall or abort; only the keeper calls `kill(0, SIGKILL)`.
  Runner death also closes that pipe. An unexpectedly exited keeper fails the
  file and never authorizes a runner signal. Cleanup requires two consecutive
  completed inspections (50 ms apart) with no living group member. Observed leftovers
  fail as TEST_FILE_LEAK. Failed listings and unclassifiable PID/PPID/PGID
  prefixes remain fail-closed. A ps timeout at two seconds retries once with a
  60-second bound; all other command errors refuse immediately. Listings use
  asynchronous spawn so other files' timers and keeper EOF delivery keep running.
  File watchdogs and cleanup's five-second window use per-file monotonic time.
  Expiry requires another completed snapshot: a live group after the deadline
  refuses; two consecutive empty snapshots pass even when the second is late.
  Precisely
  recognized exit-time routing anomalies
  discard the whole snapshot and retry at most twice, 50 ms apart; persistent
  anomalies refuse and never confer ownership (LLP 3000 §7, QF2).
  Metadata is validated for group members and
  their visible descendants; unrelated rows only increment diagnostics.
  Signed Darwin UIDs, variable lstart spacing and comm spaces are accepted.
  Vanished members count as exited. Processes leaving the group (setsid,
  setpgid or detached spawns) are outside cleanup custody; visible descendants
  receive metadata validation but are never signalled by the runner. Native Node's exit
  status alone decides test success; reporter counts are diagnostic only.
  The reporter observes progress as Node delivers it: synchronous work that
  does not yield may buffer events in Node's child until that work finishes.
  Such an unreported interval is subject to the same stall bound. See QF Round 7.
  The M5 MacBook is the publication host; the office M1 is calibration-only.
- Client tests and their helpers must gate Linux APIs (`/proc`, `strace`, `ss`,
  `taskset`, namespace/network tools) at the test or call, or implement a
  portable fallback. Keep portable assertions running on Darwin and give each
  skipped witness an explicit reason. The client pool runs
  `test-platform-audit.test.mjs`: a cheap static census follows static named/namespace `.mjs`
  imports and named helpers and checks scoped Linux skips/branches/early returns.
  A gate in another test does not count. This conservative common-case guard
  does not prove dynamic imports, generated scripts or arbitrary control flow;
  review those paths and run affected tests on the publication Mac.
  Listener snapshots and syscall tracing are Linux qualification capabilities,
  never dependencies of the published `snapback4/proof` browser helper.
- Before a publication dry run, warm the test artifacts **in that publication
  worktree**, while the host is free of calibration work:
  `LIBSQLITE3_FLAGS=-USQLITE_ENABLE_MEMORY_MANAGEMENT cargo test --workspace --no-run`.
  Use the dry run's toolchain, target directory, profile/features, `RUSTFLAGS`
  and Cargo profile environment. Do not add `--release` or `--profile production`:
  the suite uses the default test profile. The suite still asks Cargo for the
  complete test inventory, runs every binary and runs doctests separately.
  Warming compiles without executing tests and supplies no qualification receipt.
- One owner on the core (LLP 3000 decision 4): the engine, the store and the client contract are written by the lead session; Codex lanes take the fixture generator, the reference apps, the device witnesses and the periphery. Do not reshape `contract.ts` from a lane; propose the change in the journal.
- No ceiling is raised in slice one (LLP 2000.000 §12). A thing that does not fit changes the design or waits.
- The journal is append-only and dated; never rewrite an entry. LLP 3000 §7 carries decisions that must outlive it.
- Nothing here depends on `snapback2/` at runtime. Copy source deliberately, split under 1,500 lines on the way in, and say so in the journal.
- Named lane: `~/projects/snapback-sb4` on `sb4/*` branches. Never `git stash`; commit checkpoints.
- The conformance corpus (`corpus/`) is Snapback 2's serialized IR and value
  model; the core keeps those serializations so it binds. A case the slice
  cannot run is skipped with a named reason, never counted as a pass.
- `snapback/generated/api.ts` is built by `check` and `dev`, never committed;
  the app's typecheck needs a `check` first (the suite does one).
- Point rule probes and `exists` require a unique key. A fully pinned superset
  of a required unique key is unique too (for example an explicit role index
  containing the unique conversation/principal pair). Nullable unique key
  arguments need non-null proof. Other existence checks use bounded populations
  in code. Named equalities covering a total unique key check extra columns on
  that one row without an additional index.
- The packaged CLI uses `LIBSQLITE3_FLAGS=-USQLITE_ENABLE_MEMORY_MANAGEMENT` during both publication tests and its production build (LLP 3000 §7). A plain production Cargo build retains the dependency's ordinary SQLite policy; use the same build input when comparing with the package. The variable is unnecessary at runtime.
- When creating a stage, `scripts/publish.mjs` runs the suite and refuses on red, builds the production binary, packs `snapback4-darwin-arm64` and verifies the installed digest, then publishes both packages. Bump `version` in `packages/snapback4/package.json` (and its `optionalDependencies`), `packages/snapback4-darwin-arm64/package.json`,
  `packages/snapback4-linux-x64/package.json` and `Cargo.toml` together; a test refuses a mismatch.
- Publication retains exact qualified tarballs under `target/publication/`. If a
  stage exists, use `node scripts/publish.mjs --resume` to validate its recorded
  suite qualification, requalify those exact bytes through the installed-consumer
  proof, and finish missing registry artifacts; an existing version must match
  its staged integrity. This also applies after `--dry-run`. An ordinary invocation
  refuses an existing stage so changed source cannot silently publish old bytes.
  Bump the version to build a new pair; preserve a partially published stage.
- Cross-host publication (LLP 3000.021 §3.3): prepare the common client once
  from clean release source, qualify that tarball on Linux, and import the
  Linux tarball plus its receipt on the Mac. Source commit, client SRI and
  Linux SRI must agree. Linux prefers working Docker/Podman, otherwise uses checksum-pinned proot
  with clean Ubuntu 24.04/Alpine root filesystems in a user cache, and refuses
  `NPM_TOKEN` or `NODE_AUTH_TOKEN`, even when empty. It never signs or packs
  the client. The Mac never rebuilds or executes Linux; `--resume` rechecks
  its copied receipt. No receipt packs a Darwin-only client; an existing
  two-platform preparation is refused on that path. Packed dependencies must
  match the staged set. Uploads use verified private snapshots, with each
  digest checked again immediately before publish.
  Commands and current qualification limits:
  [`reports/20260927-lx-linux-package/REPORT.md`](./reports/20260927-lx-linux-package/REPORT.md).
- The guide is tested text. Every ```quarry block in `crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide.rs` and in `packages/snapback4/README.md` is compiled by `crates/snapback4-lang/tests/guide.rs` with no diagnostic and no note (the language card is also run against the engine); every refusal code the sources emit must be in `guide::CODES`; every `guide("..")` a diagnostic carries must name a card. Add a form to the language card when you add it to the parser, fence README Quarry as ```quarry, and never fence Quarry as plain text to dodge the test.
- Native jobs (LLP 3000.005) need `rustup target add wasm32-unknown-unknown`; the suite's fixture build fails loudly without it. The guest SDK `crates/snapback4-native` is a path dependency until it is published; an app's `snapback/native/Cargo.toml` names it by path.

Quarry store testing (LLP 3000.035 §5.1): `SNAPBACK4_EVAL_POSTGRES` is an
explicit evaluation connection. The factory owns one physical schema per worker,
restores its rows and bookkeeping, installs the compiled fixture, then opens the
ordinary engine. Mutation attempts restore a seeded snapshot; shrinking reuses
that same factory. `Database::connect_fresh()` is an opt-in test mode. Fresh and
restored outcomes must serialize to identical bytes. The local wire observer
retains SQLSTATE, message, detail and SQL statement before the public query API
converts a driver error to a refusal; it never changes the traffic.

Reads sweep every public boundary. Mutations compare every discovered position
twice on SQLite, and compare up to eight seeded positions per dimension on
Postgres (including first/last), plus the case's original budget. Sampling
counters are in `store_stats`; each selected position runs prefetch on and off.
Physical prefetch counters are separate from the exact public outcome record.
The widened generator (`src/widen.rs`) emits prefetch-reachable reads: pages
projecting `tags[.tag]` and `tags[name = .label]` (a `Get` and a unique
`Lookup` whose keys vary across rows) and a loop reading `tags[row.tag]`, so
`store_stats` reports marked reads, transferred rows, cases with a transfer
and `prefetch_consumption_witness_probes` (fewer database calls with prefetch
on: cached answers were consumed). A planted cached-hit-to-miss fault is caught
by the random corpus (LLP 3000.035 §5.1; the closed
[coverage ticket](../issues/closed/20261007-quarry-store-oracle-prefetch-coverage-gap.md)).
Heap is charged identically on every store and the device (LLP 3000.007
§20.24.5); any disagreement is a ticket candidate, never normalized into a pass.
The cluster regression asserts it finds no signature and refuses any new one.

`run-stores --seconds N` stops each worker between cases after N seconds; a case
and any shrinking already underway finish. `cases` remains the requested count,
`generated` is actual completed generation, and `time_limited_workers` reports
the early stop. Slowest-case timing includes generation, comparisons, shrinking
and artifact saving. It does not silently claim the requested million. The scheduled
runner rejects incomplete reports; its own `--hours` option instead chooses a
case count from a 256-case calibration of both campaigns, with 20% headroom and
a million-case cap. This is a sizing estimate, not a hard deadline.

Generator widening (`src/widen.rs`, LLP 3000.035 §5.1): from a stream
independent of the base generator (`generate::base`; a seed it leaves alone is
the base case byte for byte), cases gain prefetch-reachable reads, mutation
blocks (lets, `require`, `if`, several writes to `key` and `k2`, reads after
writes), a prelude of mutations `prep0..` run in order on the same store before
`run` (`Case::steps`, sometimes as the other viewer; `observe` then reads both
keys), and expiry (`expire at .until`, a clock advancing from `seeded_at`
through each step to `clock`, sweeps, `until: now + ttl`). Every oracle runs the
prelude (`execute::world`) and compares its outcomes (`prelude-*` fields;
`store-postgres-prelude`); a device acquires at the seed clock and catches up by
one sync round after the server's prelude. The prelude is emitted first so no
rewrite moves its sites. `lean::generate` narrows half the cases outside the
model (projections, expiry) back into its fragment; the rest stay named
unsupported (`shape`, `loop`, `table-expiry`). The report's `coverage` counts
each new feature (`block/*`, `prelude/*`, `projection/*`, `expire/*`, `clock/*`).

Quarry device testing (`src/devices.rs`, `src/device_writes.rs`, LLP 3000.035
§5.1 oracle 4): each case runs on a server engine and on a device of each store
(native SQLite at a file path, and memory), both brought up through the client's
own path: the `/schema` backend as JSON text, then `local.ts::syncRound` (capture
`sync_state`, `/sync` snapshot pages built as `serve_sync.rs` builds them,
`observe_store`, `apply` through `call_text`, catch-up stream). Never seed a
device store directly. Reads the device publishes without the server (`complete`
with `unknown` false, or a known refusal) compare the whole outcome record
except `seq` (and server-only `delivery`); every other read counts under a named
reason (`online-only-*`). Low ceilings reach the device only through `call`'s
`spent` ledger (`default - n`), compared at the server's boundary positions.
Writes go through `admit`: a device refusal must equal the server's, a
predicted row must equal the committed one (less its `pending` marker), and
after `settle` plus a sync round the device must answer `observe` as the server
does. `run-devices` accepts `--seconds`. Known findings are retained under
`cases/found/device-*` with a `DIAGNOSIS.md`; `tests/devices.rs` replays them
and `tests/smoke.rs` pins the fixed-seed signature set.

The Lean oracle (`run-lean`, LLP 3000.035 §5.2): `snapback4_lang::emit::lean`
translates each generated program's surface tree (never the binder's IR) to
the model's case JSON; `quarry-oracle` (`lean/Oracle.lean`, one process per
worker) answers one tagged outcome per line; the engine runs on SQLite at
default ceilings. Compared: kind, refusal code, data (tagged: an id is not a
text), `complete`, `next` presence, each side's own continuation, and a
mutation's `observe` reread. Not compared: meter fields and sites. Programs
outside the model's fragment are named unsupported reasons, never passes.
`lean::generate` adds Lean-only variants (per-effect rules, a hidden probe
target, nullable/id orders, residual filters) aimed at `lean/OPEN.md`. A
disagreement is a Rust defect, a model defect (only when a document
contradicts the model: fix the model, rerun `lean/check.sh`), or an open
point (record the engine's behaviour in `OPEN.md`); never change the model to
match the engine without a document. Findings live in
`cases/found/lean--*` with `DIAGNOSIS.md`; `tests/lean.rs` replays them and
pins the open signature set. Lean-dependent tests skip loudly without the
built model; `SNAPBACK4_REQUIRE_LEAN=1` makes that a failure.

Quarry scheduled testing: prepare a separate clean clone whose current branch
can fast-forward to `origin/main`. The runner fetches/fast-forwards it and starts
its own socket-only evaluation cluster with `fsync=off`,
`synchronous_commit=off`, `full_page_writes=off`, `shared_buffers=1GB`, and
`max_connections=200`. It builds the Lean model's `quarry-oracle` first
(`lake build` in `lean/`; a host without `lake` fails the run), then runs the
SQLite, store, device and Lean campaigns on the selected
seed window, replays shrunk findings twice, and files/appends open `issues/*.md`
tickets. Only ticket changes are committed/pushed, with three fetch/rebase
retries. State and reports live outside Git under `--state-dir`; seeds advance
only after completed work and successful owned-cluster cleanup. Dirty/diverged
trees, overlap, setup failures, partial reports and failed publication refuse.
A crash can retain the lock and cluster directory; inspect `lock/owner.json`
and the run report. Never stop a cluster by process name.

`--dry-run --cases 32` writes previews and a separate `next-seed.dry-run.json`,
never commits or pushes. Dry runs may explicitly supply `--external-postgres
'host=... user=... dbname=...'`; that cluster is neither started nor stopped by
the runner. Ambient connections do not override normal private-cluster ownership.
The installer remains an operator action; do not invoke it during development.

The Lean oracle on a scheduled host (one-time, per user, no sudo): install
elan with `curl https://raw.githubusercontent.com/leanprover/elan/master/elan-init.sh -sSf | sh -s -- -y --default-toolchain none`.
The runner finds `lake` on `PATH` or in `~/.elan/bin`; the first `lake build`
downloads the toolchain pinned in `lean/lean-toolchain` (Lean 4.34.1, no
Mathlib), so the first scheduled run needs network. Check by hand with
`(cd lean && ./check.sh)` in the runner's clone. `run.json` records the pinned
toolchain and the oracle binary's digest.

## 19. Dormant visibility and migration (DORM)

Implementer: Codex for Charlie Cheever, 2026-10-19; target 0.4.35. This is a
proposal until its implementation and two independent READY reviews are
recorded. It amends §9.1's prohibition on value substitution only for an
explicit, previewed, reversible projection. No inferred coercion is added.
The wave-19 day-two evidence distinguishes 21 affected old-client records
from retained facts in ten newer-only tables; no row deletion was found.

### 19.1 Declared old views and renames

Wave-21 EVOL extends this initial surface in [LLP 3000.000.000.002 §8](./3000.000.000.002-declarative-backfills.rfc.md#8-wave-21-release-evolution): active-input refresh, truthful causes, loosened-target bridges, table/asset identity renames and bounded file-text inputs supersede the corresponding initial restrictions below. SEC6 owns security-review resurfacing.

A reverse projection belongs to the newer source and runs when that
declaration is left. It reads the assembled newer source under activation's
system authority and ordinary job meters, before projection or narrowed
visibility closure can hide its inputs. Typed inputs and deterministic point
reads follow LLP 3000.000.000.002; simultaneous projections read one snapshot
and cannot consume each other's new results. A reverse-only declaration is
first-class: moving a legacy field to a related table must not require a
dummy forward transformation. Output fields are validated against the
selected target schema. Identity, reference lifetime, captured authority,
asset and engine-owned fields retain their existing safety boundaries.

The spelling is `down extensions legacyReason(id: id):` followed by
`{reason: (explanations[extension = id] ! else MISSING_REASON).explanation}`.
Typed inputs describe the current newer row; outputs describe the selected
older view, so an old output field need not remain in the newer table.
`explanations.extension` must have a unique point index. A declaration is
self-contained and immutable by table/name across retained history.
Point-read syntax is inherited from LLP 3000.000.000.002 §2: a bare unique
`explanations[extension = id]` returns a nullable row, not a scalar field
or a silent field fallback. Field access requires the normal non-null
proof or `! else CODE`. An explicitly nullable scalar projection can use
`{reason: (explanations[extension = id] ! else MISSING_REASON).explanation
if exists explanations where .extension = id else null}`. The existence
probe is metered on the same source snapshot; its cut refuses instead of
selecting the null branch. No new nullable lookup syntax is introduced.
This version has no per-release selector for a down declaration: applicability
is solely whether the selected same table has the output field. Two writers
for that table/field therefore co-apply whenever either can apply; different
intended release numbers do not disambiguate them. Reverse output sets on
a table are disjoint, including implicit rename writers. Reject overlapping declarations before any adoption work, naming
the declarations and field; types do not establish target applicability.
Each surviving target cell has at most one reverse writer after saved
forward-preimage/non-null-retained precedence. A declaration applies only when the selected target
contains its named output; absent target outputs remain retained, not
physically introduced into that release.
Both raw-schema admission and every activation recheck this condition for
the actual candidate/target pair. Computed down outputs are scalar old-view
cells only: no reference, asset, collection, identity, captured/maintained,
engine-owned or source lifetime output is admitted. A column rename may
only move existing lifetime evidence unchanged; it cannot create a binding.

The saved native preimage has the same scalar restriction as the old-view
output. A scalar substitution must not archive a newer reference, asset,
collection or lifetime-owned cell without custody. A separate scalar column
or typed bridge is the repair. Typed reference inputs keep private parent
lifetime bindings even when their argument is annotated `id` or an older
schema exposes an ordinary id; deleting/reusing that parent cannot authorize
recapture on forward or on the next rollback with unchanged inputs. Raw schema
validation applies down admission, including unique names, disjoint outputs
and sequence ordinals as engine-owned cells. Existence fragments require a
declared plain index and a valid prefix/range shape at raw admission;
malformed source lookups refuse rather than panic. Every evaluated point/existence lookup sees indexes
rebuilt for the assembled source snapshot, including restored rows when no
expiry is due. A declaration with no applicable projected cells does not
rebuild or validate indexes solely for an expression that will not run.

Rows with saved forward-backfill predecessor preimages use those exact
preimages. An entering inactive down receipt likewise restores its exact native
cells ahead of another leaving declaration's substitution. Non-null native
legacy cells also win over a standalone `down`.
A retained value synthesized by an earlier `down` remains owned by its
receipt, not a native predecessor preimage. On each later rollback it must
be recomputed from current declared newer inputs (including changed point
dependencies), with a fresh P receipt. A prior synthesized image never
suppresses that recomputation or becomes current truth by precedence.
The old synthesized image must still match its receipt before replacement;
a conflicting edit aborts rather than silently keeping a stale image.
A row whose native restoration never completed still owns its inactive
receipt: leaving another narrowed view preserves that receipt and projected
image rather than recording its projection as a fresh native preimage.
A standalone `down` explicitly authorizes replacing missing/null old-view
cells, including an intentionally authored null. This is a declared old-view
policy rather than an inference that null is missing: an operator who needs
an intentionally null old view must author a nullable expression preserving
it. Every physical pre-projection value, including null, is retained as the
exact newer-cell preimage in P; physical absence has a distinct marker.
Forward restores that original null/absence exactly, so synthesis does not
discard its authoritative preimage. No provenance claim distinguishes
intentional null from historical manufactured null. Thus nulls
materialized and retained by earlier old-source rollback do not suppress it.
This is declared synthesis, not a claim about a legacy row's birth release.
An asserted lookup for an intentionally null old cell can therefore refuse;
authors use an explicitly nullable expression when null is the truthful
old view and newer source data is absent. Rows born
with the newer representation may use the declared reverse projection.
Retain the exact newer projected cells and their reverse result in the same
transaction. Compatible forward restores those cells rather than deriving
an approximation of their authored value. Unrelated edits under the older
source survive. A changed projected cell or a changed dependency that would
make its restoration ambiguous refuses atomically with a bridge repair.
Deletion/expiry ends the lifetime and clears the corresponding evidence;
id reuse never inherits it. Prune previews these private images and leaves
reversal-unavailable identities when values are explicitly discarded.
Missing lookups, meter cuts and invalid reference/authority evidence refuse,
never become false absence. A value with no truthful old representation
retains ordinary narrowed dormancy; zero observations do not silently become
one observation. The declaration is not a claim that different product
meanings are equivalent. All indexes, constraints, read-through validation,
maintained data, generation and release selection commit with the projection.

The outcomes are disjoint. A saved forward predecessor preimage wins. Otherwise an
eligible row evaluates the declared expression: an asserted missing point
executes its authored refusal and aborts activation; an ordinary nullable
point may return null only where the expression explicitly allows it.
There is no implicit missing-lookup fallback. A successfully computed value
that does not fit the old type follows narrowed dormancy. Invalid/corrupt
evidence and authority/lifetime failures abort. A meter cut aborts the entire
transaction with the ordinary bounded-work refusal; retrying unchanged
inputs is not promised to succeed, and an indexed bridge is the repair.
Exact retained native cells win on re-entry; an older-source edit to their
declared dependencies that conflicts with that native image refuses rather
than deriving over it. An out-of-domain older dependency edit is still a
conflict; hiding that row under the narrower newer type does not authorize
recapture. Pruned evidence requiring discarded values refuses
explicitly, including in later previews; it never fabricates null/default.
Carrying an active pruned declaration through an unrelated schema change
does not need discarded values while its native cells remain available.
Reintroducing a retired native cell still requires actual retained evidence.
Dependency verification, projection and native eligibility marking also visit
already narrowed row images. Adding a declaration while a native row is
hidden must not skip its later projection. Retired inputs use their retained
source definition, including the default interpretation of physical absence.
Hiding a row or retiring an input column cannot bypass a conflict.
For each row, let P be exactly the applicable fields computed by down after
preimage precedence, including outputs which fail the old target domain
and therefore make the row dormant. Their native preimages and computed
results remain receipt-owned even while the row is narrowed. Re-entry
assembles that retained projected row, verifies the same receipt, restores
its native P cells, then applies destination narrowing/validation. It must
not discard P merely because its old-view value is not visible. Its receipt stores each field's newer value or physical
absence and its old-view result. Forward first verifies every field in P
still has exactly the physical presence and value of that old-view result
(absent, present-null and present-default are distinct; interpretation
through a default cannot make them equal),
then restores only P. Receipt-owned physical absence survives later
narrowing, column retirement and whole-row restoration; an older literal
default must not become its native value. Candidate validation interprets
that absence through the native destination definition while the retained
image keeps it physically absent. Individual dormant cells and whole-row
archives must agree on each receipt-owned patch/inverse before either can
be overlaid into a source snapshot. Fields outside P keep the current
old-source values.
Any conflicting field aborts the entire activation; a disjoint field edit
does not authorize overwriting a conflicting projected field.

A column rename is declared, never inferred from similar names or types.
The spelling `newName: Type renamed from oldName` names its old column;
both directions preserve the
authoritative cell and lifetime evidence through the same reversible
adoption. A conflicting already-present target value refuses rather than
choosing a winner. Required fields do not acquire invented defaults. Client
argument compatibility is distinct from result shape compatibility: an old
query's result field cannot be promised merely because stored data moved.
Old operation wrappers/projections and the compatibility report must reflect
the actual exposed result shape. Devices execute no migration metadata;
semantic capability/store fences refuse runtimes that cannot interpret the
new declarations or retained evidence.

The rename is strictly within the same table and row lifetime; its grammar
names a column, never another table/row/parent. Cross-parent and cross-table
moves are not renames. Identical cell types means the full structural type
including numeric/text bounds, enum members, nullability and nominal target,
not merely its base kind. Existing default/constraint validation still
applies to the selected schema; the rename never invents a missing cell
from a new default, truncates or coerces it. A type conversion needs
a separately declared transformation; the rename itself never converts.
The paired rename form uses the same closed scalar/lifetime boundary as
computed down: references, assets, collections and engine-owned/maintained
columns reject during compilation, before either direction can activate.
This initial surface never accepts a forward-only protected rename.
A missing required source cell without saved evidence/default is E_ADOPT,
not an invented optional value. Old and new target schemas validate their
respective view in each direction.

### 19.2 Forward and captured read-through authority

Selecting a newer retained source after older-source writes re-enters its
declared backfills. Newly written old-source rows receive required outputs;
changed old-source inputs or lookup dependencies catch up under the existing
cohort/preimage conflict rules. Native newer outputs remain authored values.
This is not a continuously running trigger and does not synthesize a missing
application side-table fanout job. A schema that needs such reconciliation
must declare/prove it or retain a compatible bridge; it cannot claim required
labels have been supplied merely because retained rows returned.

Changing only a read predicate, including narrowing it then selecting the
older predicate, must not itself make captured read-through rollback refuse.
The reference identity, parent lifetime and authority provenance remain
bound. Reusing another parent, resurrecting an expired capture, or changing
capture structure does not qualify as a predicate-only change. Preserve old
captures; do not recapture a broader grant as a side effect of rollback.
Actual target read rules still authorize every read and the preview warns
when selecting older rules restores broader confidentiality exposure.
The operator is the existing same-UID filesystem owner, already capable of
editing the deployment database directly; activation is not a lesser data
role. This amendment adds no new capability/confirmation for selecting old
rules. Predicate-only means unchanged reference/capture structure and
bound parent lifetime/provenance; only the parent read predicate differs.
Writing the down declaration explicitly authorizes its old-view synthesis;
it is not inferred from a type match. Previews list transformed reverse
cells/rows separately from restoration and display lost confidentiality
clauses. Target rules are the selected source's rules: choosing old source
can intentionally broaden access, as before this amendment. The author's
commission does not introduce a second acknowledgment flag or an audience
containment proof for an explicitly authored operator migration.

### 19.3 Exact visibility before mechanical diffs

Rollback preview leads with `N records visible under release C will be
hidden from release T users`. Count only before-active authoritative rows
that become retained dormant in a table T has; show each table and bounded
ids in JSON. Separately count surviving target-visible cells that become
null/default or newly lack a retained legacy cell, and rows in tables T
does not have. The empty-cell category separates newly empty/defaulted cells
from restored empty legacy cells; the latter are not claimed as lost text.
Legacy stores cannot distinguish intentionally null old cells from nulls
materialized by an earlier rollback. Report the exact view without inventing
that provenance. This category counts cells, not distinct records across columns.
Actual expiry/deletion and rebuilt private caches are excluded from dormant
loss. Counts precede read rules and are table populations, never a promise
about a particular viewer's query results. Legacy stores lack reliable
row-birth release provenance, so the preview says `visible under`, not
`created under`. An old column newly shown as null is a factual view change;
the tool does not infer which related business text the application wanted.
Hidden-row counts and surviving-cell counts are disjoint by row presence;
newer-only rows have a table absent from T and cannot also be old-table
losses. No jobs or expiry run on dormant rows (§4). On reactivation the
original source deadline still applies: an expired lifetime can retire then,
and retention never promises that expired records revive indefinitely.

Each affected class names reverse projection or an expand/contract bridge
as the repair. Rule/guard/operation diffs follow it. Deploy preflight also
warns before future writes to new tables, widened domains or missing old
fields can make a later rollback hide rows or show empty/defaulted cells.
For these three syntactic risk classes the warning is conservative: no
false negatives for new tables, widened value domains and removed old fields;
a fitting authored projection may make a warning a false positive.
Exact reverse adoption on the post-deploy snapshot reports current effects
separately from conservative future-write risks. Previews use MAIN tables
in their private disk snapshot: read-through's connection-local temp-store
configuration cannot erase their before image. All scratch disappears on
success or refusal. Nothing changes live rows, pointers, history or time.

### 19.4 Operator inspection and retained identity

`sql --dormant --release DIR` and `sql --all-releases --release DIR` are
mutually exclusive read-only operator views under one pinned SQLite read
snapshot. The first contains retained whole/narrowed rows and retained cells;
the second includes active and retained images. Removed table names and
retained column names are queryable through generated logical application
relations; internal namespaces remain unavailable to submitted SQL. Factual
state/source metadata distinguish active rows, dormant rows and dormant
cells. A retained-cell record contains its id and retained cells, with
unprovided columns null. Conflicting images are separate labelled records,
never an arbitrary winning value. Inspection-mode reserved-name collisions
refuse instead of shadowing application columns. These modes bypass read
rules as existing owner-only SQL does; they add no HTTP/client namespace.
`all-releases` means every image still retained, not time-travel snapshots
or one invented version of every row per historical release.
`_snapback_columns` is the JSON array of actually provided application cells
on each inspection record, so a retained null is distinguishable from an
unprovided SQL NULL. This is provenance, not a missing-value substitution.

Equivalent retained executable source remains selectable across compiler
upgrades. Compiler reports or equivalent old/new packaging metadata do not
change source identity; actual source, native and worker behavior remain
identity-bearing and full control-request integrity still covers all files.
Ordinary refused deploys leave neither numbered candidates nor stages.
This is a source-bundle identity, not a frozen compiler-binary identity or
a proof of behavior equivalence between compiler versions. Activation
recompiles retained source with the selected runtime and revalidates every
data/authority constraint. Actual compiler semantic fixes can change
execution without minting a new source id; version negotiation and the
normal upgrade/publication qualification remain independent fences.
Backfill/reverse definition identity still includes its normalized typed
IR. Recompilation that changes that historical definition cannot reuse its
old receipts: immutable-name checks refuse and require an explicit new-name
bridge. Selecting the source id across an upgrade is supported; success of
an incompatible historical migration after an arbitrary semantic compiler
change is not promised. Removing the source-identity trap does not bypass
these admission checks.

### 19.5 Argument aliases and review evidence

`explanation: text 1..500 alias reason` declares one canonical argument and
one accepted old spelling on a Quarry query/mutation. Normalize the old
spelling to the canonical value before typed execution, including local
device execution. Both spellings supplied at once refuse E_INPUT even if
equal. Alias names must be unique and cannot collide with canonical args;
deprecated/aliased combinations refuse. Canonical default/null validation
semantics are unchanged. Generated TypeScript accepts either spelling and
rejects both; capability comparison recognizes the old shape accepted by
the alias. Compatibility advice for renamed same-typed inputs recommends
mapping with an alias. It never recommends accepting and dropping the old
value with `deprecated` as a rename repair.

Every changed guide fence/starter runs as written against a freshly packed
tarball. Qualification includes regression stores, actual old callers,
reverse/native/preimage conflicts, forward old-source writes, inspection,
hot/cold/check parity and copied wave-19 dormant lanes. Full test and
differential results and any external qualification limits belong in
WORKSTREAM-REPORT.md. Reviews are mutually blind Muse and fresh Codex
sessions on one immutable semantic revision, retained verbatim with
provenance. A READY verdict records review readiness, not author acceptance
or a publication receipt. No version bump or push is authorized.

### 19.6 Review record

Six mutually blind design rounds are preserved under `llp/reviews/3000.000.000-dorm-r*`. Round 6 received READY from Muse `muse-spark-1.3-contributor` (high, wrapper fallback) and a fresh Codex `gpt-6.1-sol` (high) on the same immutable snapshot; provenance includes its SHA-256 and both exact response digests. Earlier NOT READY findings drove disjoint writer admission, closed scalar/lifetime boundaries, per-cell restoration, explicit null/presence semantics and repeated-cycle recomputation. These are design readiness verdicts, not an implementation test result, author acceptance or a publication receipt. The commissioned implementation and qualification results are recorded in the workstream report.

A final narrowly scoped confirmation (r7) also returned READY from both providers: protected rename kinds reject during compilation, preventing a forward-only protected transition. The unchanged full amendment remains covered by the r6 pair. Both exact outputs and delta provenance are preserved.

## 20. Visible rollback views (ROLL)

Implementer: Codex for Charlie Cheever, 2026-10-22; commissioned for 0.4.39.
This amendment replaces opt-in bridging for declared domain widenings and
relaxed unique keys. Existing retention, metering, authority, immutable source,
and atomic activation laws continue to apply. SEC7 owns ordinary check gates
and ranking; ROLL owns deploy presentation and release selection.

### 20.1 Prepared source and selection

Deploy compares every selectable ordinary predecessor, even without sampled
widened rows. A widening without an applicable authored down requires a compiled
rollback view. All needed views must prepare successfully or deploy refuses.
View admission compares structural source domains (including text minima and
formats), nominal aliases, unique keys and caps; it never parses human advice
strings to decide whether a view is needed. A fitting authored scalar down
removes its covered value-domain risk; extraction/renames retain their typed
identity laws.
The compatible join is LLP 3000.000.000.002 §8: optional union, integer and text
bound union with identical format, enum union, recursively joined bounded lists,
and asset size-bound union with identical nominal kind, MIME and once policy.
Nullability and list cardinality join separately. This differs from extraction,
which requires full type equality, including size, optionality and cardinality.
References join only across explicit nominal identity renames. A mixed change
cannot enlarge any current shared-column domain with predecessor-only values:
current read/write predicates may rely on their schema-level impossibility.
The ordinary union view refuses even if that column is not the widening trigger.
A closed alternative may retain the complete current authored source closure
(schema, defaults, programs, helpers, jobs and authority), rather than synthesize
a union. It requires every predecessor table and column under the same spelling,
every predecessor operation name/kind, and current argument domains accepting
every predecessor input under the same closed compatibility rule below. It
retains the exact current domains and values, with no resurrection, translation
or old-only writes. Missing fields/names or incompatible inputs refuse. Current
result semantics are disclosed for old-UI review; current extra operations
remain current-authorized. Native/worker bundle qualification remains mandatory.
Otherwise author a compatible source and truthful migration before deploying. Enum order
and nominal rename spelling do not change this domain comparison. Domain
includes nullability, recursive list-element/cardinality bounds, text formats,
integer bounds, asset kind/size/MIME/once constraints; unsupported or incompatible
domain changes refuse rather than truncate or coerce. No conversion,
zero-money fallback, implicit business default or arbitrary row choice is inferred.

The view retains predecessor operation names and argument contracts, including
existing authored operation aliases under their compilation law (same-module,
no alias chains). Every exported predecessor alias counts as an old name; a
current alias resolves to its actual authored target for input, guard, helper
and authority comparison. Renaming an export never drops its current guards. A current
require can be copied only with identical argument and entire transitive local
binding/helper meanings, lookup predicates and control-flow paths. A changed
unmodeled closure or guard depending on writes refuses. Canonical expression
comparison ignores source locations, never semantics. When a relaxed unique key invalidates an old lookup, or current guards require
an added field on a shared table, retain that field and its authored current
backfill. An affected operation whose current guard/workflow cannot be transported
unchanged may likewise reuse its current authored endpoint under the same closed
input-compatibility rule. Every current added field is retained on a shared table, even without a guard
mention; required fields retain their authored default/backfill and every old
write path must supply them through a compatible current authored endpoint or
refuse compilation. No missing-required-field write is permitted. All helpers in a reused workflow, including write,
return and system-scope dependencies, need identical transitive meanings. An already-authored current endpoint of the same name/kind whose
arguments accept every predecessor input may replace the affected old endpoint;
the initial comparison permits identical argument types, optional widening and
integer-bound widening and enum-superset widening, never narrowing or new required arguments. Retained
current backfill identity keeps its output custody active. This supplies authored
writes for the added required fields instead of inventing defaults; the generator never invents first-row
selection. The replacement's point/limited selection must retain the language's
deterministic total ordering, including its stable id tie-break; a source outside
that deterministic language fragment refuses. Missing or incompatible authored
endpoints refuse compilation.

A generated visible view retains the current forward-migration declaration set
for shared output columns. It does not resurrect a retired predecessor transform
whose outputs already remain current columns in the compiled union. Historical
source and receipts remain intact. This preserves current native values and
migration custody instead of reinterpreting them with a retired input domain.
Transforms needed for fields removed from current source remain subject to the
ordinary truthful-projection and complete-adoption checks.

A generated visible view omits a predecessor-only historical down declaration
when all its outputs remain current columns whose generated domains admit the
current native values. Its immutable original declaration and receipt evidence
remain historical; the view does not reenter that retired projection or restore
stale preimages over native values. Downs needed for removed fields remain.
Exact original-source selection retains its projection/conflict behavior. Writer
adoption still enforces full domains, lifetimes and zero-unacknowledged hiding.

Shared tables retain current caps, immutability, final-existence constraints
and lifecycle guards. Generated source carries authored current constraints,
unique keys, expiry and opening declarations; the compiled view must preserve
current hard constraints, reference-deletion/lifetime shape and sync audience
or preparation refuses. Text constraints follow the explicit domain join, not
a separate implicit relaxation. Schema quotas cannot become weaker merely
because their current rule IR is unchanged. Unsupported structural transport
requires an authored compatible workflow; no arbitrary population choice or
new business default is generated.

Shared tables retain the **current authored authority**, including transition
bindings, read rules, payload guards and field ownership. The compiled bound IR
must equal current authority after declared identity alignment. A release switch
is a view over the same current authoritative rows, never restoration of a
historical database snapshot: deleted rows stay deleted and current values
stay current except declared truthful migrations. No resurrection or replay of
historical preimages over authored current images is authorized. With no
hiding and no domain enlarging beyond current, those row populations remain
the current populations; changing read visibility still follows exact current
authority.
For intentional hiding, IR equality alone is insufficient: §20.2 additionally
checks population-sensitive authority/guards under the actual writer. Removed old immutability/uniqueness
constraints follow explicitly relaxed current constraints. Newer-only tables
retain current rules without promising old clients expose new concepts. Native
or worker dependencies without a qualified authored bundle refuse. Undefined
fields, changed helper closures and incompatible workflows refuse rather than
weakening guards. Nullable result fields and carried/reused guards appear in
an action-focused checklist; successful mechanical adaptations are facts, not
11–42 mandatory decisions. The commissioning author's chosen visible-rollback
contract explicitly permits nullable/wider result domains; these are disclosed
as RESULT compatibility changes, never claimed to retain a nonnull v1 result
contract. Requiring nonnull results would contradict that requested end game.
Names and accepted input contracts remain preserved. Dormant restoration advice
names --restore-dormant.

Removing a defaulted predecessor column requires an authored truthful down.
The initial grammar has no acknowledged-absence escape for removed defaults.
Preview may suggest a cross-table expression from declared references/columns,
but it is advisory source for the author to review, never an inferred business
mapping. A false default is not a reversible representation.

Prepared source is immutable. Each parent manifest maps original target id to
numeric view id, executable digest, changed columns, carried guards and hiding
reason. The parent's full control digest includes the manifest; the view's full
control digest includes its metadata. Prepared views are usable only after the
parent's CAS activation commits and never count as ordinary latest or equal
forward source. Failed parents remove stages/views. Unknown admitted outcomes
retain them for history-authoritative recovery; uncommitted parents cannot
authorize selection. Before either equal-source no-op or retained-source reactivation, prepare the
complete desired view set privately and compare target/executable digest/hiding
reasons with that source's own committed manifest, verifying its entries. A
matching set permits no-op or identity reuse. A missing, stale or newly needed
mapping creates an ordinary registration parent using the same source and full
adoption/CAS validation; no view is selectable before that commit. Output and
JSON identify the equivalent retained source as sourceRelease. Source and old
manifests remain immutable. Temporary desired views are removed on no-op/reuse,
refusal and preview. This also upgrades unchanged legacy deployments without
needed registrations. Later equal source is a no-op once its desired set matches.
Retained-source reactivation and rollback preserve their own registry context.

Resolve the manifest belonging to the active ordinary release, or the
registered parent of the active compiled view, under the command lock. Never
borrow a later deployment's view across source branches: it may carry weaker
guards than the active source. If that context has no mapping, default selection
uses original source with the same writer visibility checks. Bind actual source
digest, expected release and activation revision to the existing CAS. ABA/equal-content switches change revision. Cold, hot,
preview and restart use that actual immutable source. Selection restores dormant
names implicitly, with full data/constraint/lifetime validation. Output and
releases identify original and actual source. --exact selects the original and
reports dormant hiding; it bypasses only default visible-view selection, never
applicable security/strict refusals, authority checks or migration validation.

### 20.2 Writer-enforced visibility

Default rollback, even without a registered view, admits zero unacknowledged
hidden predecessor-table rows. Candidate preview also refuses uncovered hiding
on reverse snapshots. Future bad projections refuse selection under the writer.
Preview populations are privileged retained-row counts, not viewer guarantees.

The initial intentional-hiding spelling is:
`rollback N hides TABLE where COLUMN = null because 'REASON'`.
The candidate column must be nullable, the predecessor column nonnullable, and
table/column spellings identical. N is the immutable original. No arbitrary
predicate, blanket exemption or rename combination is admitted. Its view keeps
the null restriction while joining the nonnull domain: nullable 0..1000 versus
required 0..100 becomes required 0..1000, preserving widened nonnull values.
The nonempty reason travels in immutable metadata and repeats on selection.

Under the actual writer transaction, capture the before image, run full adoption,
then compare every retained hidden predecessor row. Only acknowledged null rows
and the actual hidden-reference closure rooted in them are allowed; unrelated
hidden rows refuse atomically. The closure graph contains only scalar/optional
nominal Ref cells in before-image active rows, as typed by the source schema;
ids are aligned through the catalog identity. An edge qualifies only when its
parent was also active before, is actually retained-hidden after adoption, and
has no invalid reference-lifetime marker. No list, synthetic extraction anchor,
asset, dormant overlay, cascade or set-null declaration invents an edge. A
renamed identity uses its same catalog number. Before-hidden rows are outside
this newly-hidden population; newer-only tables are distinguished. The snapshot
preview is advisory; concurrent writes cannot bypass activation revalidation.
Acknowledgment never changes physical preservation or permits broader read/write
authority. After collecting the actual newly hidden table set, the writer rejects
negative existence authority, reference comparisons that can admit absence, or
population-tainted requirements/control flow. Positive existence requirements
may only become denials. A direct point assertion may discharge absence taint:
if its row is hidden it aborts before subsequent work; its surviving field values
remain unchanged. Writes to affected existing targets require that same exact
point-presence guard. Transitive bindings and compiler-expanded helper bodies
are followed; unknown paths refuse. Tainted branch/loop/conditional-write keys AND persisted write values,
negative/count-based guard dependencies, nested job-query scope and maintained
populations depending on hidden sources are conservatively outside this initial
acknowledgment form. This is a closed syntactic admission check, not an inferred
semantic implication or a finite-snapshot security proof. Explicit reasons do
not bypass it. Source may retain the safety witness in a visible bridge instead.
Ordinary hidden-row occupancy/retention laws remain explicit in the preview;
intentional hiding supplies no promise of automatic forward conflict resolution.

Point-presence proofs discharge taint only for subsequent work in program
order on that exact table/key. A guard cannot justify any earlier tainted read
or write. Mutations/jobs retain their existing atomic transaction law: a later
refusal rolls back all prior writes in the operation, including work before a
point assertion. The population checker does not weaken that transaction law.

### 20.3 Edited transformation outputs

An edited in-place migration output reverses through an applicable down on its
assembled current image, or stays exact if the compiled selected view's full
type/constraints accept that physical value. This never widens a schema per row,
coerces, truncates, null-fills or invents defaults. Otherwise selection refuses.
Original preimage, actual authored newer output, prior computation and physical
old-view presence are separate evidence; edited output never gains native birth.

Forward validates old-view field presence/null/default/lifetime evidence first.
Preserving authored output requires unchanged explicit arguments AND freshly
unchanged computation, including point/existence lookups. Changed inputs require
exact deterministic agreement with authored output or a real conflict printing
competing values. Unedited outputs retain preimage precedence; receipts state
when older views read stale until forward. Repeated and partially retired cycles,
invalid lifetimes, pruned evidence and meter cuts obey the same atomic laws.

### 20.4 Reference renames and asset extraction

Reference renamed-from transports the existing physical cell and reference
lifetime bindings, including retained/narrowed images. It never recaptures a
deleted/reused parent or captured/engine-owned field. Both aliases at once and
unequal destination cells refuse. After identity alignment, nominal target,
nullability, bounds, on-delete and capture shape must be exactly equal; widening
is a separate migration, not part of this rename.

`extract CHILD from PARENT via REFERENCE: {newField: oldField}` is a declarative
migration, not an operation or cross-table rename. It creates one receipt-owned
initial child per source row; child's deterministic id equals parent id in the
child namespace. The child has a required reference to parent with cascade or
restrict deletion; keep/set-null are outside the initial form. Independent active
or dormant destination history refuses. At least one mapped asset is required,
with identical full source/child type. Parent must remove that source asset
column. Other copied fields must be explicitly immutable scalar source fields;
mutable, captured, maintained and nominal-reference copies refuse. Copies must
continue to agree both directions. Other required child columns need authored
defaults. Receipt identity uses numeric parent/child catalog identities and declared column
origins, so table, asset-column and uncaptured reference-column aliases do not
create a new migration or recapture an anchor. Typed schema normalization updates
extraction table/reference slots and preserves source/destination column strings.
Destination admission inspects active rows, dormant rows/cells and competing
receipts. Reentry requires its existing receipt-owned child and valid anchor;
pruned/deleted child history is never recreated from metadata.

Future independent children with distinct ids are allowed; extraction is not
unique-parent. Existing durable id reservation and duplicate-key admission apply
to receipt-owned/dormant children exactly as to ordinary rows. The normal mint
never overwrites an occupied id; an explicitly forced collision refuses. Neither
rollback nor deleting receipt metadata frees a still-retained row id.

Asset ledger home and physical asset cell move together, without reuploading or
changing bytes. The former active cell and equal dormant overlay are removed;
a distinct overlay refuses rather than being discarded. Private displaced-cell
receipt evidence persists separately from active cells. Reverse removes the
child asset cell while retaining identity/scalars and newer-only children. Later
forward uses the current old-view asset, including replacement/optional clearing,
never reviving released receipt bytes or creating a second ledger home.

A native child without a receipt reverses only when exactly one total candidate child
exists for that parent, and that child has a valid private parent-lifetime anchor. Anchors are captured while
extraction is active, including absent-parent invalid anchors; unchanged writes
never recapture invalid evidence. Older independent relations cannot qualify
retroactively. Deletion/id reuse, reparenting and ambiguity refuse; no arbitrary
photo selection. Receipt-owned children likewise require valid lifetime evidence.

For row-dependent audiences the child must read through its parent with identical
parent policy; otherwise source/child read policies must be identical and row-
independent, or destination denies reads. Existing payload gates must match.
Reverse must preserve the current effective child/parent/column audience or
refuse with guarded-bridge advice. These are closed admission rules, not a
policy implication prover. Native/ref compatibility does not confer byte access.

Actual activation revalidates active AND dormant destination emptiness, regular
byte presence/ledger length, full types, scalar copies, parent binding, anchor
validity and reverse ambiguity inside adoption under command locking and CAS.
Preview runs this transaction on a private copy; its counts cannot authorize a
later activation. Failure rolls back evidence, format, homes, selection and
generation together. Devices execute no migration metadata or home authority:
authenticated serving resolves the current server ledger home and current row/
column policy. Existing device generation and media-retention receipts remain;
this introduces no retroactive cache-erasure claim.

Store format 9 fences edited-output/reference/extraction AND compiled-view
registry custody from older runtimes. Every successful release adoption upgrades
to 9 transactionally, before prepared registry entries become selectable. Older
CLIs must not mistake view ids for ordinary latest or skip visibility policy.
Every hot activation request from this CLI carries the explicit registry-custody
feature field, including exact/original-source selection, an empty registry and
an existing format-8 store. The field covers all format-9 adoption laws, including
edited outputs; it is not conditional on a visible view. Updated hosts reject
activation clients omitting it. Older hosts reject that unknown field before
admission; restart serve with the updated runtime. Updated hosts always require
it before activation admission, with no conditional old-wire-shape exception.
This prevents an older engine from executing any adoption without the new laws.
Unchanged no-op deployments without new custody can stay at 8. Format 8 opens;
failed adoption leaves its old format unchanged; 9 survives
old-source selection. Capabilities release-child-extraction-v1 and
release-reference-renames-v1 fence schema admission. Device projections omit
migration metadata while retaining ordinary current tables and through policies.

### 20.5 Presentation and qualification

Human deploy output starts with verdict/security FIX/test status, followed by
adoption/rollback facts. Result advice groups a changed source column with its
affected operations once. Security review is last, scoped to changed declarations,
ranked and capped at five advisory notes, with omitted count. Reachable FIX
witnesses are never capped/suppressed. A trailing verdict supports tail readers.
JSON carries every compatibility detail, report, test status and view mapping.
Campaign matching/stale/missing/incomplete state and age remain explicit. Finite
tests never certify universal security. SEC7 owns ordinary check ranking/gates.

Readiness requires blind Muse and fresh Codex READY reviews of the same revision,
retained verbatim with provenance. Qualification covers cycles/conflicts, optional
money, image/file homes, deleted/reused references, view failure/CAS/restart,
bakeoff copies and packed guide cases. This record claims no unrun test or
publication and authorizes no push/version bump.
