## 11. Wave-22 loop repairs (DX6)

Commission: Charlie Cheever's wave-22 fixes #5, #6 and #8 (2026-10-22).
Implementer: Codex in sb4-b22-dx6. This amends §10, the staging clock of
LLP 3000.000.000.002 §4, supplied sessions in LLP 3000.036.004 §15.4,
and the starter application of LLP 3000.013.002 §2. SEC7 owns security advice,
intent, campaigns, upload recipes and the password/roles recipe.
This proposal is subject to the requested fresh Muse and Codex reviews.

### Session input and compatibility

Seeded records preserve the actual signup session's kind alongside its
principal, token and numeric expiresAt. They can be passed directly as Session.
Online createClient accepts a legacy incomplete session literal as token-only
transport credentials: its token must be nonempty; partial principal, kind and
expiry metadata never establishes identity, refresh timing or authority. The
server resolves the bearer; session() is null and there is no refresh timer,
as for the existing token option. Complete shapes remain strictly validated
and privately snapshotted. Local/device session input remains complete-only.
E_SESSION lists every missing/invalid field without values and gives the exact
one-line online repair createClient({ url, token }). An ISO expiry in a complete
shape refuses instead of creating a NaN timer. Restored full sessions retain
the existing restore validation and server expiry semantics.

### Advances, maintenance and retained boundaries

ClockAdvance remains an object reporting now/fired/held; valueOf returns now
for arithmetic, ordering and new Date(result). This is JavaScript numeric
coercion, not strict-equality compatibility; typed callers use result.now.

For classified staging, advance(duration) preserves remaining ordinary session
lifetimes by default. The core advances valid full/read session created/expiry
fields, read caps and recoverable mint receipts by the same checked delta,
in the same transaction as existing worker-lease preservation, before publishing
the new process clock. Expired, capped, revoked or superseded authority cannot
become live. Selection uses the authority kernel and bounded pages; overflow
refuses atomically. Existing action delegated sessions and supervised service
families remain owned by the worker-lease path, without double shifting.
Historical admission timestamps are evidence, not an ongoing cap; application
deadlines/backoffs remain business time.

Preservation includes SDK custody, not just raw HTTP credentials. A staging
response for the exact request bearer can publish authoritative session expiry
and read cap beside the same locked business-time/revision snapshot, obtained
through a detached store-identity/liveness-checked authority read. No bearer,
new authority, mint, rotation or refresh is exposed. Production publishes no
such metadata. A failed detached timing read suppresses the clock sample,
including streaming-final samples, instead of publishing advanced business
time with stale credential metadata. A client reconciles only its current
bearer's matching request URL/header and monotonically increases its live
expiry/cap before clock listeners run. Local/device clients await durable
credential custody before publishing or persisting their business clock.
Online clients keep a volatile clock; their existing session listeners may
persist custody asynchronously. Authenticated restoration still validates
expired metadata with the staging probe; no online durability barrier is implied.
Heartbeat final frames carry the same matching-bearer timing or unavailable
evidence as ordinary response headers. Reconciliation and the corresponding
local/device clock publication are serialized: durable credential saves are awaited
before publishing or persisting the advanced clock. A failed save leaves the
clock unchanged, retains dirty credential timing for retry, and surfaces as a
transport failure; rotation/close fences also suppress stale clock publication.
App preparation retains its existing private credential staging: prepared local
clock offsets remain non-durable, including on discard/close, until the actual
App credential commit succeeds. Only then may clock persistence activate before
the scope is published. A later clock-save failure reports a signed-in warning
and retries through ordinary device work; it cannot roll back durable identity.
The existing per-invocation response cutoff (LLP 3000.013 §6 and §12.7) also
spans implicit scope retirement's local credential cleanup. A scope-owned
reference count retains capture before teardown and through each scheduled
local credential task, releasing on rollback or close without waiting for an
auth operation or remote revocation queue.
Capture occurs synchronously when the response decoder validates a body,
before caller Promise continuations can cross that cutoff.
An invocation retains a body validated while live through settlement; a
completed capture owner cannot reopen its cutoff through another begin or hold.
Retention preserves a decoded body, not a stale authentication classification.
An E_AUTH refusal remains scoped to the bearer revision actually dispatched:
rotation supersedes prior and intermediate retiring-bearer revisions, including
when the response was decoded before rotation or arrives after cancellation or
a retryable refresh failure. Recheck that authority when consuming retained
responses. A successor already dispatched during credential persistence remains
current; its own refusal may sign it out. Sign-out alone does not supersede a
same-bearer refusal, and sent outcomes and business refusals retain their ordinary
invocation custody. Retryable refresh failure still permits a same-id retry under
the current credential revision.
Rotated/logged-out credentials cannot be restored by a late response. Client
clocks are isolated, so one bearer's response cannot expire another before its
own timing is reconciled. Read startup verifies before scheduling expiry;
an expired restored metadata image requires a successful authenticated read-only
staging probe with matching viewer/scope and live timing. Production/offline
without this evidence keeps the existing expiry refusal. The local constructor
reconciles bootstrap timing before applying a restored device clock's expiry
check. Tests retain actual read clients and restore them across their old
absolute expiry, compare two independent bearers, and exercise explicit-expiry
and production controls. These headers are staging timing evidence only;
ordinary authority is still checked on every server admission. Real elapsed time still consumes
credentials. sessions:'expire' explicitly tests expiry with the existing
business clock. Dev retains its existing expiry behavior. Production rejects
all clock controls and never calls preservation. Restart keeps the persisted credential timestamps. Staging clock persistence
now follows §12; ordinary dev keeps its process-local offset.
This intentional staging-only simulated jump also shifts a read family's cap;
ordinary production read-cap immutability is unchanged.

advance(duration, {drain:false}) advances time and pauses background maintenance
before releasing the writer. This covers expiry sweeps, scheduled enqueue,
queued job chunks, asset collection, view recount and delivery maintenance,
so a mutation can exercise its own deadline check. Authorization and logical
row expiry still apply to ordinary queries/mutations. The pause lasts until
host.drain(), another draining advance, or process restart. drain() uses the
same authenticated endpoint with duration:'0ms', drain:true, advancing no time
and resuming normal coalesced maintenance. eachBoundary requires drain:true.
The HTTP report includes maintenancePaused; native CLI spells these controls
clock advance D --no-drain and clock drain. Failed/ambiguous requests are never
automatically replayed. A failed drain leaves its advanced time intact, its
report explicitly incomplete, and maintenance paused. Only a successful explicit
drain resumes it. drain() advances no time and safely continues existing queued
work/checkpoints; never repeat a lost advance to obtain that recovery.

A saved boundary is future-held only when it is strictly greater than the
boundary of the requested effective schedule window. Equality means that the
current window was already processed: report not-due, without consulting wall
time. Repeated advances inside that window and zero-time drains succeed without
allowHeld, including recovery of queued work after a failed drain completion.
Never enqueue the processed window again.
A draining advance whose saved future boundary blocks requested work refuses
E_CLOCK_HELD before clock, session or lease effects. The refusal names the
schedule, saved boundary, next unprocessed window and recovery duration to
reach the saved boundary from the unchanged process clock, not from the rejected
requested time. Relative recovery hints in accepted reports likewise use the
actual process clock, including when a disclosure floor is ahead of it.
A held report sets heldUntil to that saved boundary and
nextBoundary to the next unprocessed boundary. Catching up to heldUntil
reports not-due and never re-enqueues the processed window; reaching nextBoundary
fires once. Fresh staging storage and continuing with the original offset are
recovery choices; saved markers never rewind. allowHeld:true (CLI --allow-held) explicitly accepts a
retained boundary. Intentional drain:false pauses and dev --hold-schedules are
explicit holds. not-due/initialization remains a successful reported outcome.
Control authorization/classification is checked again under the actual writer.

### Upgrade advice and future formats

Advice is scoped to actual version transitions and each behavior's introduction
version. A fresh init/check is silent. Delivered stderr is acknowledged durably
for non-TTY callers too; baseline checks neither emit nor acknowledge advice.
The new upgrade helper retains warning stderr even under --quiet. Published
older quiet CLIs discard their check child's stderr without signalling this;
that historical capture cannot be distinguished from normal non-TTY delivery.
Advice from that old quiet path may therefore be acknowledged without display;
guide changes remains available. No ancestor
process inspection or script mutation is introduced.

The bounded candidate scan covers partial session literals and numeric advance
uses, explicit persistent startDev calls and data --json consumers. Explicit
nearby refusal assertions do not become dev-door warnings. Candidate paths,
never source or credentials, are printed; incomplete scans remain disclosed.
Future known-family store formats refuse with 'a newer Snapback (FORMAT)'.
Read-only SQL inspection validates the format before exposing rows and exits
nonzero on refusal. Already published old binaries cannot be repaired.

### Starter and proof rendering

Both list starters render through the packaged ReadState. Proven absence and
partial empty results retain distinct text, with existing freshness and
pagination semantics unchanged. Rename the docs' local ReadState example to
ReadNotice so it does not shadow the export. Password clients omit persona
selection and explicit web witness/generated API imports. The proof driver installs the
existing witness bridge before app creation; createApp calls an immutable Symbol observer only under explicit witness=web.
This is a public same-origin test channel, not a secret or an authentication
boundary. A witness URL alone installs no observer and exposes no App global.
The driver installs its nonconfigurable/nonwritable observer before application
scripts and locks/freezes the selected witness bridge; the first App remains
selected for that page. Same-App explicit API registration can enrich it;
a different explicit App refuses E_WITNESS_APP_SELECTION and requires a separate
page. Later automatic Apps cannot silently redirect the proof. Application JS
remains trusted to implement the application: same-origin code can forge its
own test observer outside a driven page, just as it can read web session storage.
The observer grants no new credentials or backend authority. Ordinary bundles
carry only the creation dispatch, not the witness implementation. No-API
registration captures only durable non-ephemeral mutation handles; direct
witness mutate still requires generated API.

Proof text locators match normalized entire descendant text, including composed
React Native spans and wrapping labels. Choose smallest matching visible,
non-editable elements; independent matches retain strict Playwright ambiguity.
Whitespace normalization collapses runs and trims both ends; case is preserved,
with no Unicode normalization or input value matching. Exact strings are case-sensitive;
explicit substring strings are case-insensitive and regex follows its pattern/flags.
String matching stays exact by default; substring is explicit, never a silent
fallback. A composed label cannot make an unrelated sibling count as success.
Text ranges outside overflow clips do not contribute evidence to a visible
ancestor. Layoutless wrappers may forward their visible descendants; a clipped
suffix cannot manufacture a complete status label.

## 12. Wave-24 loop repairs (DX7)

Commission: Charlie Cheever, 2026-10-22; implementer: Codex in sb4-b24-dx7.
This amends §11, LLP 3000.036 §22.8 and the ordinary rule-helper exclusion.
SEC8 owns security questionnaire/dispositions, presence intent, invariant test
coverage and lifecycle advice; DX7 only reports their existing verdicts.
Implementation and tests accompany this amendment; review readiness is distinct
from author acceptance.

### Durable staging time

A staging store owns its cumulative offset and latest explicitly checkpointed
business time. The additive typed metadata record also retains a pending
maintenance pause. Production ignores this record and rejects clock controls;
ordinary dev retains its process-local clock/reset contract. No release-format
change or filesystem sidecar is needed. Store adapters use their existing
metadata transaction and fencing, including Postgres.

Startup resumes at the maximum of wall plus saved offset, checkpointed time,
and the persisted time_audience_clock disclosure floor (LLP 3000.000.000.002
§4), plus the maximum retained commit stamp and schedule boundary even when a
checkpoint exists. A fresh store with no evidence starts at wall time. Unknown
metadata is preserved by ordinary old-binary adoptions; if an older binary or
manual restore removes it, recover time from these retained floors, with no
claim to recover a deleted pause. Explicit startup
offsets (serve --clock-offset and startServe({clockOffset})) are absolute
offsets from wall time, clamped to the resumed floor;
restarting never adds the same offset twice or rewinds saved boundaries.
The resulting effective offset is persisted at startup, preserving the saved
paused flag exactly. This is the maximum of five named evidence values: wall
plus offset, checkpointed now, time_audience_clock, recent commit maximum and
latest schedule boundary (plus an explicitly requested offset floor). Removing
all time evidence by restoring/recreating a store can return it to wall time;
this is the intentional independent-test reset path, never an in-place rewind.
Old binaries do not enforce the pause even when they preserve its record;
downgrade is not a way to run a paused proof under these guarantees. Malformed checkpoints
refuse startup rather than silently resetting. Downgrades to older binaries
ignore this additive record and retain their historical process-local behavior.

Each accepted staging advance checkpoints offset, time and paused=true in the
same transaction that preserves eligible sessions and worker leases, before
publishing the process clock or doing maintenance. E_CLOCK_HELD and input validation precede this transaction. A definite
storage abort rolls back all those effects. Postgres executes the existing
fenced writer transaction containing offset/time/pause and session/lease edits.
An uncertain COMMIT may already have committed: E_COMMIT_OUTCOME_UNKNOWN fences
the host from further admission/maintenance, then shuts it down. Restart reads
the durable checkpoint before serving; never replay a credential shift to
reconcile it. An uncertain pause-clear may already have cleared it, so only
definite failure guarantees retention of the durable pause. Fault injection
covers both write boundaries. A lost reply is still ambiguous and never automatically
replayed. drain:false, a failed drain and an interrupted drain retain the pause
across restart. A successful drain durably clears it only after due work has
finished. If that final write fails, report an incomplete drain and retain the
pause. Startup writes a checkpoint with zero delta and never preserves credentials.
Each advance computes its delta from the resumed effective clock and writes
credential timestamps and checkpoint in one commit: the checkpoint itself is
the durable completion evidence; there is no separate applied marker. A restart
does not shift already-preserved credentials again. Clock
status/advance reports say the offset belongs to this staging store. Coalescing,
eachBoundary, current-window equality and explicit allowHeld keep §11 semantics.

### Rendered and scrollable text

Mounted text inside overflow:auto/scroll ancestors counts even when outside
the scrollport: it can be reached by scrolling. The selector does not scroll
or change application layout. Overflow:hidden/clip remains a real clip, with
per-axis character geometry retaining only reachable characters. Starting at
each text rectangle, compose legal scroll shifts [current-max,current-min]
and each ancestor clip from inner to outer, independently for x and y; this
excludes unreachable negative-origin text and admits an inner scrollport
inside an outer hidden clip without scrolling the page. Zero-sized scrollports
remain clips. visibility:visible descendants may override visibility:hidden;
display:none and opacity:0 suppress every descendant. content-visibility:auto
counts when browser range geometry exists (unrendered skipped content supplies
no geometry); content-visibility:hidden excludes. Use computed CSS overflow
values, including the browser's visible-to-auto cross-axis conversion. Fixed
and sticky descendants use current geometry only, without hypothetical ancestor
scroll movement; the clip model is conservative for their special containing
blocks and does not claim they can be revealed by scrolling. display:none,
visibility:hidden/collapse, opacity:0 and content-visibility:hidden remain
excluded, as do editable/input/script text and layoutless text. This does not
claim occlusion detection or virtualized content that is not mounted. Existing
normalization, smallest matching element and strict ambiguity remain unchanged.

### Upgrade and gate presentation

Upgrade snapshots count security notes alongside compiler notes and report
pending review gates separately, with unavailable previous gate data stated
honestly for old snapshots. The gate status reflects acknowledgments without
writing them during reporting. A refuted invariant cannot be acknowledged:
check explicitly reports that --ack-security was not applied, leaves review
records untouched and explains that the policy/intent must be repaired.
The JSON field acknowledgmentNotApplied carries the same explanation. The
existing Report::refuted predicate determines refutations (LLP 3000.036.004);
ordinary advisory notes never fail check. Only check acknowledgment paths
write dispositions, and delta reporting only reads them. The CLI takes one
--ack-security ID per invocation; when refuted, that entire single-ID write is
suppressed and check exits nonzero. Old upgrade CLIs invoke the newly installed
check and cannot acknowledge/bypass its gates. The advance scan is bounded
heuristic migration advice, never a security gate; indirect aliases or arbitrary
use(t) dataflow can be missed. Mixed failures show
schema/device remediation before the gate repair/ack command, explicitly
subject to repairing any refutation first. Gate-only upgrade failures name
check --ack-security <ID> --reason '<spec rationale>'; other failures retain
schema/device remediation. An already published old CLI may still print its
old E_UPGRADE line; the new check child must carry the actionable explanation.
The release card records the 0.4.37 CLI's obsolete closed-door/startDev advice.
The numeric-advance candidate scan requires an actual advance call in the same
local expression/assignment as numeric consumption; unrelated operators or
new Date/assert calls elsewhere in a file do not qualify.

### Protected immutable payloads

A column-read-protected payload may be immutable. On insert its supplied value
is validated normally. On every update/existing-upsert, a supplied protected
immutable field is ignored without comparing it with stored data, for every
viewer, including its readers and the system. Its retained value and asset
placement remain unchanged; no upload ownership or placement check runs for
the ignored field because no asset is adopted (amending LLP 3000.036 §22.9).
An ignored newly uploaded asset remains unplaced under the existing uploader
ownership, accounting and collection lifetime; no extra adoption or extension
is created. Immutability constrains update/existing-upsert within one row
lifetime; delete is independently authorized by the table delete rule. It
does not add an undeletable identity policy or prohibit a newly authorized
insert after deletion. The operation still validates supplied input types/bounds; ignoring occurs
before merge/placement, including explicit null for an optional field. Invalid
operation input still fails using schema/site facts alone. An
existing upsert differing only in these fields succeeds with them retained;
absent-upsert still inserts. Compiler advice on an
explicit patch names that behavior using schema/site facts only. Device
prediction retains the old cell or its omission and never predicts the supplied
cell. Ordinary jobs/system updates follow the same rule; schema-evolution
backfills remain the explicit replacement path. Ordinary unprotected immutability retains
its existing contract. Cap/unique/index/authority/derived dependencies on
protected fields remain refused. This input-independent policy preserves
LLP 3000.036 §22.8's noninterference oracle and needs no new device protocol:
the same interpreter runs it on the server and device.

### Audience helpers and hidden probes

Expression-bodied helpers, or a block containing one return, may appear in
table/column rules when expansion is exactly the existing rule grammar.
Arguments are ordinary typed rule terms, substituted simultaneously without
caller capture. Only parameters, viewer and rule constants may be free in the
helper; a helper's focal dot cannot capture the caller's row. Nested helpers
are bounded to eight levels and 4096 visited syntax nodes per rule (including
arguments and each expanded body), refused as E_QUARRY_RULE_OPERATOR on
exhaustion. Direct and indirect cycles are detected on the expansion stack.
Parameters cannot shadow viewer or rule constants. exists is explicitly
allowed and pays the same probes after expansion/inversion as handwritten
rules; ordinary reads are excluded. Simultaneous substitution prevents capture;
cycles,
wrong arity/types, block effects/lets, arithmetic, clock and ordinary reads
refuse with E_QUARRY_RULE_OPERATOR and an actionable repair. No new runtime
instruction, probe semantics, authority or resource allowance is introduced.
Expansion precedes read-through, index inference, audience inversion and
security analysis so every consumer sees the same canonical predicate.

Program exists remains a caller-visible read; rule exists retains its existing
policy probe semantics. N_UNREADABLE_PROBE and N_LOOKUP_FILTERED name the exact
hiding read/read-through rule and source site, including exists directly in a
require. No runtime refusal confirms whether a hidden row physically exists.
Runnable guide recipes demonstrate protected immutable payloads, a role helper
and a hidden parent probe alongside a deliberately minimal readable projection.

### Required evidence

Regression coverage includes repeated startServe/advance/drain runs on one
store, drain:false restart and legacy saved boundaries; no double session
shift; production controls and failed-checkpoint atomicity; mounted below-fold
RN-web text and hidden/clip controls; gate-only and refuted upgrade diagnostics;
real and unrelated advance scan candidates; protected immutable equal/different
patches, upserts, assets and server/device observation equality; helper types,
recursion, capture and authority equivalence; hidden program exists source
advice. Every changed guide snippet executes against the fresh pack. Full
five-crate Rust tests, affected client pool, Mac Studio affected platform tests,
qualify-linux --consumer-only (test.sh is prohibited), published 0.4.35/0.4.37
old-CLI upgrades, refs/docs budget and repository-root checks are recorded in
WORKSTREAM-REPORT.md. No version bump, publication or push.

# LLP 3000.000.000.002: Declarative release backfills

**Type:** RFC
**Status:** Review
**Systems:** Snapback 4 language, storage, release adoption, staging clock, proof kit
**Author:** Codex for Charlie Cheever (MIG)
**Date:** 2026-10-19
**Revised:** 2026-10-23 (DX8, §9 day-two migration and operator semantics); 2026-10-21 (EVOL, wave-21 adoption, previews, bridges and migration expressiveness); 2026-10-19
**Related:** [Release activation](./3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md), [Staging](./3000.000.000.001-staging-with-real-users.rfc.md)

## 1. Commission

Implementer: MIG, 2026-10-19. Wave 17's fifty changes require constant,
conditional, derived, enum, string-split and point-lookup transformations.
Declare these in Quarry, with no public migration mutation, job or JavaScript.
REL5 owns release selection/restoration and old-client compatibility; SEC3 owns
security checks. No new version, publication or push authority is implied.

## 2. Language

A top-level declaration describes one table's historical input fields and its
simultaneous output patch:

```quarry
backfill bookings vehicleClass(weight: int, length: int):
  { vehicleClass: 'commercial' if weight > 3500 else 'van' if length >= 500 else 'car' }
```

Here length is stored in centimetres. Arguments are typed historical fields;
they remain in the declaration after the candidate drops those fields. The
compiler binds arguments against their explicit types, not today's table.
The runtime validates them against each interpreted historical row. Output
names belong to candidate authoritative columns and may equal input names:
read-old/write-new. Outputs of multiple declarations on a table must be
disjoint. Names are unique per table. Durable store history keeps one
normalized definition per table/name, including gaps; changing it refuses with a
bridge/new-name repair. A new name denotes a new transformation.

The expression body is an object patch. It uses Quarry's existing arithmetic,
comparisons, null handling, conditional expressions, immutable helper expansion,
and bounded deterministic scalar builtins. Supported text additions are
`before(text, delimiter)`, `after`, `beforeLast`, `afterLast`, `startsWith`,
and `allDigits`. Splits remove the first/last exact delimiter; absent delimiter
means whole text before, empty text after. An empty split delimiter refuses.
Digits means nonempty ASCII digits. Text is Unicode, comparisons and splits
are locale independent. Existing `lower`, `trim`, `slice`, `count`, `sha256`,
`first`, `last`, `at`, `min`, `max`, exact `divide`, calendar/civil transforms
and concatenation retain their normal checked semantics. No randomness,
viewer, clock, minted ids, writes, mutation helpers or loops are admitted.
Identity `id` is never an output. Only scalar outputs are admitted, including new references. Assets, lists,
engine-owned/captured/maintained and lifetime outputs refuse, including fields
owned by a historical source lifetime even when the candidate removes that rule.
Restored narrowed rows retain their individual output ownership, captures and
reference types. Columns added by later predecessor schemas contribute their
own types/defaults only when absent from that archived definition. Overwriting an
existing reference/asset is refused: derive a new reference column so the
existing dormant reference-lifetime ledger owns retirement/restoration.

Point reads use the ordinary `members[principal = owner] ! else NOT_FOUND`
spelling. They require a unique point. A missing asserted point refuses;
ordinary nullable point reads and `exists` retain their explicit semantics.
Historical existence predicates may traverse a metered source range, needed
for wave-17 cross-tower references; they return no row collection. A meter
cut is a refusal, never false absence or an incomplete historical value.
Before evaluating any patch, each unique lookup index validates the historical
population's cardinality with bounded privileged duplicate samples. A different
simultaneous patch making candidate keys unique cannot choose an arbitrary old
match. All reads run as the trusted activation system principal, bypassing application
read rules, against the assembled historical snapshot. Dormant namespaces
remain unnameable. Candidate read indexes are built over that source snapshot
only when their component fields exist there. No JavaScript or app migration
operation receives this authority. All declarations are self-contained: they
cannot read another declaration's outputs in the same activation.

## 3. Adoption and reversibility

The existing activation writer transaction owns this sequence:

1. Assemble retained source rows/cells and their source definitions using the
   existing restoration intent. This includes narrowed rows eligible to return:
   restore their full historical images before evaluation, deferring only the
   backfill output columns' candidate-type checks. Retained reference invalidation,
   source lifetime and audience checks still apply before restoration. Retire
   source-expired lifetimes before any backfill; a transform cannot extend an
   expiry or opening boundary.
2. When leaving a declaration, restore its predecessor-visible output fields
   from saved **output preimages**, separately from its argument inputs. Save
   interpreted old values, including literal defaults/nulls, and absence.
   Added output columns use ordinary dormant retention instead. A different
   current value from both recorded output and preimage refuses with a conflict;
   untouched same-column enum renames restore their exact original value.
   Membership is per row lifetime, with normalized declaration definitions kept
   beside evidence. Removing a whole table suspends its membership rather than
   feeding transformed values back as inputs. Re-entering that declaration reads
   its original overwritten inputs and preserves its current result; selecting
   original source instead reverses it even when the immediately previous schema
   lacked the table/declaration. Ordinary active deletion clears membership.
3. Enter declarations absent from the immediately active schema. Unchanged
   carry-forward and reopening run none for ordinary active rows: candidate-native
   writes are not historical rows. Newly restored narrowed rows receive carried
   declarations too, including a declaration introduced while they were dormant. On re-entry, restored cells/rows are eligible along with
   older active rows. A native v2 row whose absent historical inputs have a
   complete valid restored output image keeps that image. Older v1 rows lacking
   outputs are filled. Declarations must accompany skipped-release source;
   no unseen intermediate transformation is implicitly inferred.
4. Evaluate every entering patch against one assembled source snapshot, with
   ordinary per-row job meters and a paged source walk. Stage output/evidence
   in SQLite temp storage before applying any patch. Recompute the patch on
   re-entry, so changed lookup values count as changed dependencies even when
   declared arguments are unchanged. Compare both saved argument values and
   computed outputs. If unchanged, preserve edited added-column cells. If
   changed, recompute only when current outputs equal the saved result or the
   reversed preimage; otherwise refuse the conflict. Never overwrite a manual
   output edit because another input happened to change. A fully reversed
   same-column output edited during absence also refuses re-entry when its
   current interpreted value equals neither preimage nor saved result, even
   with unchanged arguments. Sparse defaults count by interpreted value.
5. Apply patches and evidence together. Run ordinary narrowing/reference
   closure, projection, complete type/asset validation, indexes, uniqueness,
   caps and maintained-data rebuilds. Transformed dependencies force rebuilds
   even when their declarations did not change. Publish programs, generation
   and release in the same commit. Any error rolls everything back.

Evidence is keyed by table number, row lifetime and the SHA-256 digest of the
normalized declaration/typed IR (no diagnostic locations or whitespace).
It records declared input values, each overwritten output preimage and computed
output. Ordinary authoritative deletion/expiry deletes that row's evidence in
its transaction; id reuse cannot inherit it. Row moves to dormant storage
preserve lifetime. Backfill definitions enter schema/activation identity;
changed rows also set activation population, forcing a fresh generation and
device reacquisition. Public/device schema projections omit all backfill
metadata; devices never execute migrations. A successful evidence installation
sets `snapback4-release-store-6`, transactionally. Unaware binaries refuse that
format even when an old source is selected. Failed installation retains the
previous format. Legacy stores without backfills remain unchanged. Prune previews
backfill preimages separately, with counts, bytes and bounded ids. Explicit
prune deletes their values and keeps only per-lifetime reversal-unavailable
identities. Leaving an overwritten column then refuses with a pruned-preimage
reason: select a bridge retaining present meaning or restore a backup. Active
row deletion clears these identities too. Pruning added-column evidence does
not prevent rollback when no old-visible field needs a preimage.

Dropping a declaration has explicit semantic meaning: leave it by restoring
preimages or refusing conflicts. Keep declarations in every later source
whose view should retain their meaning. Refusals include exact affected counts
and up to five bounded privileged samples. Missing inputs/lookups, output/type
errors, arithmetic/meter errors and uniqueness/cap conflicts refuse atomically;
a widened/optional non-unique bridge plus an authorized repair mutation is the
repair path. A post-backfill-edit conflict requires keeping the current/bridge
source, deciding a truthful old representation, then repairing it before
selecting old source. No automatic fallback turns a new enum value into an old
meaning. Native v2 rows with no preimage retain the parent's existing law:
forward selection may make incompatible present values dormant; rollback
requires representability and refuses with its existing reason. This amendment
does not change REL5's rollback or restoration selection policy.

`deploy --check` uses the existing private disk snapshot, deploy direction and
restoration intent; rollback previews use rollback direction. Later live writes
can change its plan. `backfills` in both cold/hot JSON receipts and exact preview
text reports `table name transformed N rows` or `table name reversed on N rows`.
These are transformed row visits, separate from the parent's disjoint dormant
rows/cells/index entries. They are not additive counts of distinct rows across
different transformations, and do not claim a frozen production population.

Consumed historical references must retain valid parent-lifetime evidence even
when the input column is dropped. Expired predecessor/restored images are retired
before any transform, including ordinary expiry without read-through. Pruning
keeps membership-only tombstones solely for surviving lifetimes; such a tombstone
refuses re-entry requiring lost values rather than treating the row as fresh.

Re-entry retains an adoption-cohort identity with each active row receipt.
All declarations first applied in one activation, including continuously carried
declarations on tables that were never removed, read one reconstructed source
snapshot, including point/range indexes; this overlays that cohort's complete
input evidence and output preimages (including original absence) on current
images. Distinct original cohorts are evaluated separately against their
respective historical overlays, so a declaration introduced later can read an
earlier declaration's already-transformed values. Computed and retained/native output values must fit candidate scalar types and
text minimum/format constraints before narrowing; invalid generated outputs
refuse the entire adoption with affected-row samples. Patches remain staged until
all cohorts succeed, and current row images are restored before applying them.
New declarations use the current assembled predecessor snapshot. Inactive
cohorts restore their own still-omitted input interpretations, independently of
new declarations in that activation, and catch up changed authored inputs. Declared historical arguments consume retained
cells and source definitions even when the candidate omits those columns;
retained parent-lifetime bindings still apply. A native-image exception requires a birth marker for this exact declaration,
a matching fingerprint of the original non-output inputs, and valid, complete outputs;
a new declaration never skips valid old inputs merely because an older
transformation left a retained output. Receipt reference inputs use private namespaced bindings in the existing
dormant parent-lifetime ledger, independently of the live input column. A
retargeted/dropped input cannot substitute its current binding for the saved
reference. Every unchanged saved argument consumed during active or inactive
re-entry validates its private lifetime binding before program evaluation.
Bindings use each restored narrowed row's actual archived reference type
(rather than the table-wide predecessor), even when an
argument is declared as generic `id`. Existing private bindings survive
re-entry with unchanged saved inputs even after the visible column becomes
generic `id`; schema type erasure cannot recapture the same parent id. Reconstruction validates any consumed saved binding before restoring
its value. Original absence is recorded separately from the predecessor's interpreted
value. A physically missing field consumes its source default when present,
otherwise null; receipts retain that interpretation explicitly, preventing
later defaults from changing argument or lookup/index evaluation. Expired sources retire when entering or leaving the
last declaration, including restored table images.

When transformed rows become dormant, candidate output reference types supply
parent bindings even when the predecessor had no such column. Existing invalid
bindings are preserved. If a restored row becomes dormant again, only its
explicitly backfilled authoritative cells update the retained image; original
source definition, other cells and authority provenance stay intact. Forward
selection after rollback reconstructs absent added outputs for that same

# What Snapback 4 Does Not Do

Longer than the doing-list on purpose. Moving something off it means writing one
line naming what it unblocks and taking something off the doing-list in the same
change. Nothing comes off during slice one (LLP 3000 §3: the clock is four weeks).

## The bars

The bakeoff gates in [LLP 3000 §3](../llp/3000-snapback4.explainer.md) are the
bars: the loop bar, hub acquisition within 2× the measured floor, client bytes
under the competitor-anchored budgets, the six scenarios green in the simulator
on the hub fixture. Everything not required to pass them does not exist yet.

## Not building

- **A second authoring language.** Quarry, one spelling, copied from Snapback 2 (LLP 1004, LLP 1007 §4b). TypeScript is for seeds, journeys, effect bodies and the client.
- **Byte- and step-priced certification.** No closure, heap, output or step ceilings at `check`; the certificate certifies shape, uniformity, completeness class and prediction eligibility. Resources are metered at runtime (LLP 2000.000 §6).
- **A per-query result cache.** No range receipts, validators, halos, absence evidence, coverage spans, extent facts, held directories, fact integrity or swap journals. The client replicates declared partitions with one watermark each (LLP 2000.000 §7).
- **Engine-minted ids for client inserts, grants, ID slices.** Ids are client-minted, time-ordered, 128-bit; the engine mints for its own writes.
- **The `drain` protocol, follower-tab inbox rows, `defineItems`, `withHistory`, `mutationTarget`, `receipt.source`, `local.caughtUp`, `partial` reason codes.** The card (`packages/snapback4/src/contract.ts`) is the whole client vocabulary; `why` carries the rest.
- **`retain` as a receipt rule.** `until-revoked` and `delivered-history` survive as the client's leave policy on a scope tombstone.
- **JavaScript in the data plane.** Queries and mutations run in the Rust interpreter inside the SQLite transaction, and in the same interpreter on the client (host decided at week 3 by measurement, LLP 2000.000 §9). A *job* may be the app's own Rust, hosted as wasm through the interpreter's operations under the job's meter (LLP 3000.005, the Moves line below); a query or mutation may not.
- **An embedded JS engine for effects.** The data plane never calls out. `emit` writes the effect durably in the mutation's commit and an external worker holding a service token claims, performs and settles it over the wire (`snapback4/worker`); the four reference effects run on the builder's Node (LLP 2000.000 §10, decided 2026-09-13).
- **Committed generated code; an auth mode; a supervised bundler.** As Snapback 2 (`rules/NOT-DOING.md`): types by inference, personas not modes, `SNAPBACK_URL` is the client contract.
- **A TypeScript twin of the interpreter.** Retired by the author's ruling of 2026-09-13 (LLP 3000 §7): one implementation, in Rust, on every host — the core's SQLite under the native module, memory under wasm on the web with the host persisting each commit's changes. The web pays 312,924 B gzip before the diet where the twin's tier paid 16,842; the conformance obligation between two interpreters ends. Expo Go runs the online tier.
- **More than one hosted target, an admin console, media transcoding or thumbnails, push delivery itself (a worker's), cron syntax beyond `every N at HH:MM`, vector or relevance-ranked search (word-prefix search is in: `words(..)` and `matching`), federation, multi-region, device-to-device sync, at-rest encryption claims.** After the clock, on evidence.
- **General observability beyond `why`, `trace`, `logs`, `data`, `sql`, server operation latency, and bounded client subscription inspection.** The `sql` verb is read-only inspection of the developer's own store (LLP 3000.007 §8.8), never an application surface. Separately declared application SQL reads are admitted for increment D (LLP 3000.009 §6.2, §14.4; the 2026-09-16 Moves line below), not implemented by this inspection verb.
- **A guide corpus.** The compiler's facts, one language card and keyword cards, as Snapback 2; no freshness dates. Every card's Quarry is compiled by the suite and the language card is run, so a card cannot promise a form the parser refuses.

- **Partial union delivery, two group audiences, a public branch.** A union syncs principal-column branches and at most one group branch, each a whole disjunct of the read rule (LLP 3000.036.000.000); a branch with an extra condition, a second group or a `public` branch is refused with the rewrite, never partially delivered; service grants add no device audience (LLP 3000.007 §20.6). A union does not combine with delivered history, read-through children, synced or row-keyed views, owner keys, timelines, private field owners, acquisition windows or direct/inherited expiry on content or membership scopes.
- **Exact horizon maintenance.** The horizon bounds acquisition (a snapshot, a bootstrap); the log never evicts or back-fills to hold a device's group at exactly N rows. A local read past the acquired horizon by key says `complete: false`.

- **Clock expressions in rules.** A read rule compares with `=` and `!=` only; `.expiresAt > now` remains a mutation `require`. Stored lifetimes use `not before .col` for server-controlled opening and `expire at .col` for expiry. The engine publishes opening commits; devices receive no unopened payload. The supported compositions and explicit exclusions are in LLP 3000.036.003.
- **A jobs framework.** No priorities, worker pools or dead letters: one FIFO of runs, one chunk at a time, a refused chunk parked with its refusal. Actions, effects and scheduled actions carry per-operation retries, deadlines and cancellation, and a record per external step (LLP 3000.009 §5.2; the Moves line below). Missed period boundaries while a server is down fire once on return, not once per missed period.
- **Asset processing.** No transcoding, thumbnails, rungs or HEIC: the bytes are served as uploaded; JPEG, PNG, WebP and MP4 only; image dimensions and optional MP4 duration read from the header; MP4 dimensions are not extracted.
- **Search beyond word prefixes.** One tokenizer (NFC, lowercase, alphanumeric words), one index shape (`scope, words(cols), order.., id`), one read (`matching`): every term a prefix of some word, the first term's words merged as lanes, bounded by the meter in a uniform scope, at most 32 words in an admitted non-uniform scope. No stemming, no ranking by relevance, no phrase or mid-word matching, no per-language folding, no intersection index for the second term (it filters the page).
- **An identity provider.** Guests, password accounts, service seats, and sessions a service mints for accounts the application authenticated itself. No phone, OAuth, magic links or MFA in the engine: an application's worker verifies those and mints the session.
- **No multi-server presence, no presence history or last-seen storage, no broadcast event stream, no connection-bound rows.** `connected[p]` stays server-wide, counting held polls and their 35 s grace; a live query that may read it re-runs in the background about every 5 seconds to stay current (LLP 3000.018). Typing and viewing use short-lived, authorized ephemeral tables in one server process (LLP 3000.017); they never sync or decide durable invariants. A device does not know presence and says so.

## Process not doing

- **2026-09-28 — ordinary mixed search** (LLP 3000.028 §3.3, lane OS round 1).
  Admit the SDK's bounded merge of current server rows and certified Left images
  for the proved single time-ordered word page. This removes application-owned
  online/held selection for that shape. One server call per requested page;
  recomputation pays again from consumed positions. The coordinator shares the
  existing device read allowance. No durable search session, extra synced rows,
  new authority or additional allowance is admitted.

- **2026-09-28 — inferred current-scope word search** (LLP 3000.028 §3.3,
  lane OS). A single bare ordinary word page may derive the exact non-expiring
  membership audience for the existing compact server plan. This removes the
  requirement to repeat that audience predicate in this narrow server query.
  The compiler supplies a scoped words index when absent; it is an ordinary
  schema index, maintained on writes and on devices, with no extra synced rows
  or new authority. The device keeps its original bare scan. Round 0 left ordered composition
  and combined accounting outside its independent subset; round 1 above admits
  that bounded SDK composition. Durable search sessions remain excluded.
- **2026-10-12 — union sync audiences** (LLP 3000.036.000.000, workstream UNION, two blind review rounds). A table syncs to the union of principal-column audiences and one group audience; a device holds a row while any branch admits its viewer. What it unblocks: owner-or-team, buyer-or-seller and owner-or-staff tables offline for every reader, the shape three app farms asked for. Taken off: "one `sync` audience per table". Partial delivery, two groups and a public branch stay excluded (above).
- **2026-09-28 — explicit owner-key coverage** (LLP 3000.028 §3.4, Lane OK commission). A public window may opt into a compiler-owned, horizon-free principal key partition to answer ordinary own-key existence offline. Taken off: the exclusion of narrower platform key distribution beside one public window. General union audiences, full own-row replication and per-query absence caches remain excluded. The projection uses existing metered maintains, adoption and whole-partition publication; existing windows do not expand.
- **2026-09-26 — a live query keeps presence current** (LLP 3000.018). A 0.4.2 probe found an observed `connected[p]` reporting `fresh: true` while stale for over a minute, and a comparative builder polled around it. What it unblocks: online marks and presence-aware lists that stay current. Taken off: "a presence stream" as an exclusion for live queries; a presence API, last-seen storage and multi-server presence stay out. Under the author's standing authorization for reworks that make queries excellent (2026-09-25).

- **No drift registries, inventories, line-pinned diagnostics registries, claim digests or frozen-record gates.** Generated projections are built, not committed.
- **No gate bound to an exact commit; no push gated on a battery.** Five blocking checks in sixty seconds, run before push; everything slower on a schedule with bounded tickets.
- **No ceiling raised in slice one.** Budgets are anchored to a competitor's measured number and recorded once in the journal.
- **No standing panel** except the two blind reviews LLP 3000 decision 4 names (partition inversion; the meter's cut on non-uniform scans). The author explicitly commissioned the one-off LLP 3000.008 panel and disjoint Astra implementation lanes on 2026-09-14; its bounded disposition is recorded in that umbrella and LLP 3000 §7.
- **No new LLP number** without first extending LLP 3000 or LLP 2000.000; no spec without running code (`rules/RULES.md`: only implemented specs bind).
- **No second session on the core** during slice one; lanes take the periphery.
- **No test matrix as the primary harness.** The simulator is; real-browser and device witnesses cover only what it cannot reach.
- **No files over 1,500 lines**, including ones copied from Snapback 2. Split on
  the way in. The one exception is the package's published documents
  (`packages/snapback4/README.md` and its linked references): the rule exists to
  keep a *source* file reviewable, and the 0.2.25 calibration measured the cost of
  applying it to a document — splitting the README was that run's single largest
  scoring loss, because the builder then had four places to look instead of one
  (LLP 3000.007 §20.19). A published document is bounded by whether a builder can
  find one way in, not by a line count; the census that enforces this rule covers
  source, and says so.

## Deliberately worse

- No compatibility with Snapback 1 or Snapback 2 apps, packages or on-disk formats.
- No API stability before the clock; every publish is `0.0.x`.
- Sparse prose documentation; the diagnostics and the recipes carry the load.
- One hosted target, one platform binary at a time. SQLite remains the default store; Postgres deploy/serve admits the initial Unix-socket HTTP profile under LLP 3000.009.001 (Moves, 2026-10-20; `guide release-json`). Provider HA, full parity and publication remain outside that profile.

## Moves

- **2026-10-20 — Snapback 4 on Postgres moves onto the doing-list** (author: "commit and push these things, then do your \"what i'd do next\" things, then get this working on postgres and measure it"; LLP 3000.009.001 and its children). Unblocks: the store-port extraction on SQLite (P0, including the `Committer` seam with the serial batch); the Postgres adapters for every durable-state family and `serve`/`deploy`/`doctor` over Postgres (P1), with store-held fencing, the declared HA mode and `BlobStore`; the optimistic committer, commissioned on 2026-10-19 and built after its execution stage is sized; the scale program of LLP 3000.009.001.002 with its raw-Postgres, Supabase and Convex controls on fleet machines; and a research note comparing replicated SQLite with Postgres for applications that outgrow one SQLite file. Taken off in the same change: the evaluation-only framing of the Postgres adapter. Stands: no publication or managed-service deployment authority, no lowering of Quarry to SQL, no SQL writes to managed data, one core owner. Several serving processes (P2), log retention, live graduation (LLP 3000.009.001.001) and the async-safe external anchor each wait for their own Moves line.

# Rules

**Snapback 2 optimizes the edit-to-verified loop — the agent's building an app on it, and ours building it.**

Speed is the constraint. Everything below exists to keep the edit-to-verified loop short
and to stop this repository accreting apparatus faster than anyone can remove it. These
rules bind `snapback2/` and the LLPs numbered 1000 and up; Snapback 1 is baselined, not
exempt, and its numbers may only fall.

Each rule is **[check]** (enforced by `caps`) or **[review]** (enforced by a human).

## Budgets — every budget is a trade, not a limit

- **5 blocking checks, 60s total.** A sixth requires deleting one, in the same PR. **[check]**
- **59 documents in the working set** (`llp/current/`). Today's count. A 60th requires
  archiving one — which is just removing the link. Snapback 2 aims to leave this at 15;
  Snapback 1's links are archived at promotion. **[check]**
- **12 documents in the foundation** (`llp/foundation/`). **[check]**
- **This file: 700 words.** If it grows, something becomes a check or stops being a rule. **[check]**
- **1,500 lines per source file, baseline 204.** Generated files exempt — and generated
  files are built, not committed; so are benchmark run records under `benchmarks/runs/`,
  which are evidence, not source. The baseline is Snapback 1's oversize count on the day
  the rule landed; it may not grow, and `snapback2/` contributes zero to it. **[check]**

## Blocking checks

`caps` · `ref-check` · `snapback2/scripts/test.sh` · `snapback2/scripts/measure.mjs --check` · `check-inventories`

## Loop shape

- **Every check reports all failures in one run.** Fail-fast is banned. **[check]**
- **Nothing blocks on anything slower than 60 seconds.** Slow verification runs on a
  schedule, attributed to the breaking commit, and files a bounded set of issues. **[review]**
- **Fix loops get 3 rounds.** Then stop: escalate to a human or descope. **[review]**

## Time budgets

Tracked every run of `snapback2/scripts/measure.mjs` (appends `snapback2/metrics/loop.jsonl`).
A regression is a P0 with a name on it.

| | budget |
|---|---|
| Incremental rebuild after a one-line edit | 15s |
| Test what you just changed | 30s |
| Blocking gate suite | 60s |
| Full build, warm cache | 5 min |
| **`snapback2 check` on a program (the agent's product latency)** | **1s** |
| **`snapback2 doctor` boot-to-ready** | **0.5s** |

## Scope

- **`NOT-DOING.md` is binding.** Moving something onto the doing-list means writing why
  and taking something off. **[review]**
- **Delete; don't deprecate.** No compat shims or migration paths before promotion. **[review]**
- **Nothing is compiled, transpiled, or generated at runtime that could be built ahead of
  time.** **[review]**
- **A spec needs an implementer and a date, or it isn't written.** **[review]**
- **Only implemented specs bind.** A document with no running code behind it is a
  proposal, and a proposal cannot block a PR or justify a check. **[review]**

## Agents

- **Agents remove apparatus freely and add none.** An agent PR that adds a check, script,
  registry, config file, or design doc needs explicit human approval saying so. **[review]**
- **2026-09-06 session exception:** the user delegated the search implementation
  decisions; one `snapback2-core/build.rs` may generate pinned Unicode tables at
  build time, alongside the existing package build. No new gate, registry,
  runtime generator, or Unicode dependency is authorized by this exception. **[review]**
- **Snapback 2 LLPs are numbered 1000 and up**; `llp-create` must be told. **[review]**
- **Snapback 4 LLPs are numbered 3000 and up** (`snapback4/`, LLP 3000); it adopts these budgets
  and loop shape as its own and keeps its scope in `snapback4/NOT-DOING.md`. **[review]**

## Shared machines

- Warm, named lanes, one per stream of work. Never clone fresh; reuse the worktree.
- **Never `git stash`.** Use a temporary commit.
- Kill only PIDs you recorded at launch. Never by name or pattern.

## 11. Wave-22 loop repairs (DX6)

Commission: Charlie Cheever's wave-22 fixes #5, #6 and #8 (2026-10-22).
Implementer: Codex in sb4-b22-dx6. This amends §10, the staging clock of
LLP 3000.000.000.002 §4, supplied sessions in LLP 3000.036.004 §15.4,
and the starter application of LLP 3000.013.002 §2. SEC7 owns security advice,
intent, campaigns, upload recipes and the password/roles recipe.
This proposal is subject to the requested fresh Muse and Codex reviews.

### Session input and compatibility

Seeded records preserve the actual signup session's kind alongside its
principal, token and numeric expiresAt. They can be passed directly as Session.
Online createClient accepts a legacy incomplete session literal as token-only
transport credentials: its token must be nonempty; partial principal, kind and
expiry metadata never establishes identity, refresh timing or authority. The
server resolves the bearer; session() is null and there is no refresh timer,
as for the existing token option. Complete shapes remain strictly validated
and privately snapshotted. Local/device session input remains complete-only.
E_SESSION lists every missing/invalid field without values and gives the exact
one-line online repair createClient({ url, token }). An ISO expiry in a complete
shape refuses instead of creating a NaN timer. Restored full sessions retain
the existing restore validation and server expiry semantics.

### Advances, maintenance and retained boundaries

ClockAdvance remains an object reporting now/fired/held; valueOf returns now
for arithmetic, ordering and new Date(result). This is JavaScript numeric
coercion, not strict-equality compatibility; typed callers use result.now.

For classified staging, advance(duration) preserves remaining ordinary session
lifetimes by default. The core advances valid full/read session created/expiry
fields, read caps and recoverable mint receipts by the same checked delta,
in the same transaction as existing worker-lease preservation, before publishing
the new process clock. Expired, capped, revoked or superseded authority cannot
become live. Selection uses the authority kernel and bounded pages; overflow
refuses atomically. Existing action delegated sessions and supervised service
families remain owned by the worker-lease path, without double shifting.
Historical admission timestamps are evidence, not an ongoing cap; application
deadlines/backoffs remain business time.

Preservation includes SDK custody, not just raw HTTP credentials. A staging
response for the exact request bearer can publish authoritative session expiry
and read cap beside the same locked business-time/revision snapshot, obtained
through a detached store-identity/liveness-checked authority read. No bearer,
new authority, mint, rotation or refresh is exposed. Production publishes no
such metadata. A failed detached timing read suppresses the clock sample,
including streaming-final samples, instead of publishing advanced business
time with stale credential metadata. A client reconciles only its current
bearer's matching request URL/header and monotonically increases its live
expiry/cap before clock listeners run. Local/device clients await durable
credential custody before publishing or persisting their business clock.
Online clients keep a volatile clock; their existing session listeners may
persist custody asynchronously. Authenticated restoration still validates
expired metadata with the staging probe; no online durability barrier is implied.
Heartbeat final frames carry the same matching-bearer timing or unavailable
evidence as ordinary response headers. Reconciliation and the corresponding
local/device clock publication are serialized: durable credential saves are awaited
before publishing or persisting the advanced clock. A failed save leaves the
clock unchanged, retains dirty credential timing for retry, and surfaces as a
transport failure; rotation/close fences also suppress stale clock publication.
App preparation retains its existing private credential staging: prepared local
clock offsets remain non-durable, including on discard/close, until the actual
App credential commit succeeds. Only then may clock persistence activate before
the scope is published. A later clock-save failure reports a signed-in warning
and retries through ordinary device work; it cannot roll back durable identity.
The existing per-invocation response cutoff (LLP 3000.013 §6 and §12.7) also
spans implicit scope retirement's local credential cleanup. A scope-owned
reference count retains capture before teardown and through each scheduled
local credential task, releasing on rollback or close without waiting for an
auth operation or remote revocation queue.
Capture occurs synchronously when the response decoder validates a body,
before caller Promise continuations can cross that cutoff.
An invocation retains a body validated while live through settlement; a
completed capture owner cannot reopen its cutoff through another begin or hold.
Retention preserves a decoded body, not a stale authentication classification.
An E_AUTH refusal remains scoped to the bearer revision actually dispatched:
rotation supersedes prior and intermediate retiring-bearer revisions, including
when the response was decoded before rotation or arrives after cancellation or
a retryable refresh failure. Recheck that authority when consuming retained
responses. A successor already dispatched during credential persistence remains
current; its own refusal may sign it out. Sign-out alone does not supersede a
same-bearer refusal, and sent outcomes and business refusals retain their ordinary
invocation custody. Retryable refresh failure still permits a same-id retry under
the current credential revision.
Rotated/logged-out credentials cannot be restored by a late response. Client
clocks are isolated, so one bearer's response cannot expire another before its
own timing is reconciled. Read startup verifies before scheduling expiry;
an expired restored metadata image requires a successful authenticated read-only
staging probe with matching viewer/scope and live timing. Production/offline
without this evidence keeps the existing expiry refusal. The local constructor
reconciles bootstrap timing before applying a restored device clock's expiry
check. Tests retain actual read clients and restore them across their old
absolute expiry, compare two independent bearers, and exercise explicit-expiry
and production controls. These headers are staging timing evidence only;
ordinary authority is still checked on every server admission. Real elapsed time still consumes
credentials. sessions:'expire' explicitly tests expiry with the existing
business clock. Dev retains its existing expiry behavior. Production rejects
all clock controls and never calls preservation. Restart keeps the persisted credential timestamps. Staging clock persistence
now follows §12; ordinary dev keeps its process-local offset.
This intentional staging-only simulated jump also shifts a read family's cap;
ordinary production read-cap immutability is unchanged.

advance(duration, {drain:false}) advances time and pauses background maintenance
before releasing the writer. This covers expiry sweeps, scheduled enqueue,
queued job chunks, asset collection, view recount and delivery maintenance,
so a mutation can exercise its own deadline check. Authorization and logical
row expiry still apply to ordinary queries/mutations. The pause lasts until
host.drain(), another draining advance, or process restart. drain() uses the
same authenticated endpoint with duration:'0ms', drain:true, advancing no time
and resuming normal coalesced maintenance. eachBoundary requires drain:true.
The HTTP report includes maintenancePaused; native CLI spells these controls
clock advance D --no-drain and clock drain. Failed/ambiguous requests are never
automatically replayed. A failed drain leaves its advanced time intact, its
report explicitly incomplete, and maintenance paused. Only a successful explicit
drain resumes it. drain() advances no time and safely continues existing queued
work/checkpoints; never repeat a lost advance to obtain that recovery.

A saved boundary is future-held only when it is strictly greater than the
boundary of the requested effective schedule window. Equality means that the
current window was already processed: report not-due, without consulting wall
time. Repeated advances inside that window and zero-time drains succeed without
allowHeld, including recovery of queued work after a failed drain completion.
Never enqueue the processed window again.
A draining advance whose saved future boundary blocks requested work refuses
E_CLOCK_HELD before clock, session or lease effects. The refusal names the
schedule, saved boundary, next unprocessed window and recovery duration to
reach the saved boundary from the unchanged process clock, not from the rejected
requested time. Relative recovery hints in accepted reports likewise use the
actual process clock, including when a disclosure floor is ahead of it.
A held report sets heldUntil to that saved boundary and
nextBoundary to the next unprocessed boundary. Catching up to heldUntil
reports not-due and never re-enqueues the processed window; reaching nextBoundary
fires once. Fresh staging storage and continuing with the original offset are
recovery choices; saved markers never rewind. allowHeld:true (CLI --allow-held) explicitly accepts a
retained boundary. Intentional drain:false pauses and dev --hold-schedules are
explicit holds. not-due/initialization remains a successful reported outcome.
Control authorization/classification is checked again under the actual writer.

### Upgrade advice and future formats

Advice is scoped to actual version transitions and each behavior's introduction
version. A fresh init/check is silent. Delivered stderr is acknowledged durably
for non-TTY callers too; baseline checks neither emit nor acknowledge advice.
The new upgrade helper retains warning stderr even under --quiet. Published
older quiet CLIs discard their check child's stderr without signalling this;
that historical capture cannot be distinguished from normal non-TTY delivery.
Advice from that old quiet path may therefore be acknowledged without display;
guide changes remains available. No ancestor
process inspection or script mutation is introduced.

The bounded candidate scan covers partial session literals and numeric advance
uses, explicit persistent startDev calls and data --json consumers. Explicit
nearby refusal assertions do not become dev-door warnings. Candidate paths,
never source or credentials, are printed; incomplete scans remain disclosed.
Future known-family store formats refuse with 'a newer Snapback (FORMAT)'.
Read-only SQL inspection validates the format before exposing rows and exits
nonzero on refusal. Already published old binaries cannot be repaired.

### Starter and proof rendering

Both list starters render through the packaged ReadState. Proven absence and
partial empty results retain distinct text, with existing freshness and
pagination semantics unchanged. Rename the docs' local ReadState example to
ReadNotice so it does not shadow the export. Password clients omit persona
selection and explicit web witness/generated API imports. The proof driver installs the
existing witness bridge before app creation; createApp calls an immutable Symbol observer only under explicit witness=web.
This is a public same-origin test channel, not a secret or an authentication
boundary. A witness URL alone installs no observer and exposes no App global.
The driver installs its nonconfigurable/nonwritable observer before application
scripts and locks/freezes the selected witness bridge; the first App remains
selected for that page. Same-App explicit API registration can enrich it;
a different explicit App refuses E_WITNESS_APP_SELECTION and requires a separate
page. Later automatic Apps cannot silently redirect the proof. Application JS
remains trusted to implement the application: same-origin code can forge its
own test observer outside a driven page, just as it can read web session storage.
The observer grants no new credentials or backend authority. Ordinary bundles
carry only the creation dispatch, not the witness implementation. No-API
registration captures only durable non-ephemeral mutation handles; direct
witness mutate still requires generated API.

Proof text locators match normalized entire descendant text, including composed
React Native spans and wrapping labels. Choose smallest matching visible,
non-editable elements; independent matches retain strict Playwright ambiguity.
Whitespace normalization collapses runs and trims both ends; case is preserved,
with no Unicode normalization or input value matching. Exact strings are case-sensitive;
explicit substring strings are case-insensitive and regex follows its pattern/flags.
String matching stays exact by default; substring is explicit, never a silent
fallback. A composed label cannot make an unrelated sibling count as success.
Text ranges outside overflow clips do not contribute evidence to a visible
ancestor. Layoutless wrappers may forward their visible descendants; a clipped
suffix cannot manufacture a complete status label.

### Required evidence and review

Unit/HTTP/browser tests cover safe legacy input, seeded account sessions,
non-TTY warning receipts, fresh init, refusal tests, future formats, numeric
coercion, saved-boundary no-effect refusal, maintenance pause/resume and session
preservation versus explicit expiry. Execute changed guide snippets and all
changed starters against the fresh packed tarball, including upgraded copies
of wave-21 025 acceptance and 002 prod:serve. Record three copied wave-22
frontend non-whitespace counts before/after, preserving app behavior.
Run complete crate tests, affected client and assigned Mac Studio tests,
qualify-linux consumer qualification (full test.sh is explicitly prohibited),
refs, docs budget and repo-root checks. Report exact results and limitations
in WORKSTREAM-REPORT.md; no version bump or push. Review readiness and factual
implementation status are recorded separately from author acceptance.
