## 12. Wave-24 loop repairs (DX7)

Commission: Charlie Cheever, 2026-10-22; implementer: Codex in sb4-b24-dx7.
This amends §11, LLP 3000.036 §22.8 and the ordinary rule-helper exclusion.
SEC8 owns security questionnaire/dispositions, presence intent, invariant test
coverage and lifecycle advice; DX7 only reports their existing verdicts.
Implementation and tests accompany this amendment; review readiness is distinct
from author acceptance.

### Durable staging time

A staging store owns its cumulative offset and latest explicitly checkpointed
business time. The additive typed metadata record also retains a pending
maintenance pause. Production ignores this record and rejects clock controls;
ordinary dev retains its process-local clock/reset contract. No release-format
change or filesystem sidecar is needed. Store adapters use their existing
metadata transaction and fencing, including Postgres.

Startup resumes at the maximum of wall plus saved offset, checkpointed time,
and the persisted time_audience_clock disclosure floor (LLP 3000.000.000.002
§4), plus the maximum retained commit stamp and schedule boundary even when a
checkpoint exists. A fresh store with no evidence starts at wall time. Unknown
metadata is preserved by ordinary old-binary adoptions; if an older binary or
manual restore removes it, recover time from these retained floors, with no
claim to recover a deleted pause. Explicit startup
offsets (serve --clock-offset and startServe({clockOffset})) are absolute
offsets from wall time, clamped to the resumed floor;
restarting never adds the same offset twice or rewinds saved boundaries.
The resulting effective offset is persisted at startup, preserving the saved
paused flag exactly. This is the maximum of five named evidence values: wall
plus offset, checkpointed now, time_audience_clock, recent commit maximum and
latest schedule boundary (plus an explicitly requested offset floor). Removing
all time evidence by restoring/recreating a store can return it to wall time;
this is the intentional independent-test reset path, never an in-place rewind.
Old binaries do not enforce the pause even when they preserve its record;
downgrade is not a way to run a paused proof under these guarantees. Malformed checkpoints
refuse startup rather than silently resetting. Downgrades to older binaries
ignore this additive record and retain their historical process-local behavior.

Each accepted staging advance checkpoints offset, time and paused=true in the
same transaction that preserves eligible sessions and worker leases, before
publishing the process clock or doing maintenance. E_CLOCK_HELD and input validation precede this transaction. A definite
storage abort rolls back all those effects. Postgres executes the existing
fenced writer transaction containing offset/time/pause and session/lease edits.
An uncertain COMMIT may already have committed: E_COMMIT_OUTCOME_UNKNOWN fences
the host from further admission/maintenance, then shuts it down. Restart reads
the durable checkpoint before serving; never replay a credential shift to
reconcile it. An uncertain pause-clear may already have cleared it, so only
definite failure guarantees retention of the durable pause. Fault injection
covers both write boundaries. A lost reply is still ambiguous and never automatically
replayed. drain:false, a failed drain and an interrupted drain retain the pause
across restart. A successful drain durably clears it only after due work has
finished. If that final write fails, report an incomplete drain and retain the
pause. Startup writes a checkpoint with zero delta and never preserves credentials.
Each advance computes its delta from the resumed effective clock and writes
credential timestamps and checkpoint in one commit: the checkpoint itself is
the durable completion evidence; there is no separate applied marker. A restart
does not shift already-preserved credentials again. Clock
status/advance reports say the offset belongs to this staging store. Coalescing,
eachBoundary, current-window equality and explicit allowHeld keep §11 semantics.

### Rendered and scrollable text

Mounted text inside overflow:auto/scroll ancestors counts even when outside
the scrollport: it can be reached by scrolling. The selector does not scroll
or change application layout. Overflow:hidden/clip remains a real clip, with
per-axis character geometry retaining only reachable characters. Starting at
each text rectangle, compose legal scroll shifts [current-max,current-min]
and each ancestor clip from inner to outer, independently for x and y; this
excludes unreachable negative-origin text and admits an inner scrollport
inside an outer hidden clip without scrolling the page. Zero-sized scrollports
remain clips. visibility:visible descendants may override visibility:hidden;
display:none and opacity:0 suppress every descendant. content-visibility:auto
counts when browser range geometry exists (unrendered skipped content supplies
no geometry); content-visibility:hidden excludes. Use computed CSS overflow
values, including the browser's visible-to-auto cross-axis conversion. Fixed
and sticky descendants use current geometry only, without hypothetical ancestor
scroll movement; the clip model is conservative for their special containing
blocks and does not claim they can be revealed by scrolling. display:none,
visibility:hidden/collapse, opacity:0 and content-visibility:hidden remain
excluded, as do editable/input/script text and layoutless text. This does not
claim occlusion detection or virtualized content that is not mounted. Existing
normalization, smallest matching element and strict ambiguity remain unchanged.

### Upgrade and gate presentation

Upgrade snapshots count security notes alongside compiler notes and report
pending review gates separately, with unavailable previous gate data stated
honestly for old snapshots. The gate status reflects acknowledgments without
writing them during reporting. A refuted invariant cannot be acknowledged:
check explicitly reports that --ack-security was not applied, leaves review
records untouched and explains that the policy/intent must be repaired.
The JSON field acknowledgmentNotApplied carries the same explanation. The
existing Report::refuted predicate determines refutations (LLP 3000.036.004);
ordinary advisory notes never fail check. Only check acknowledgment paths
write dispositions, and delta reporting only reads them. The CLI takes one
--ack-security ID per invocation; when refuted, that entire single-ID write is
suppressed and check exits nonzero. Old upgrade CLIs invoke the newly installed
check and cannot acknowledge/bypass its gates. The advance scan is bounded
heuristic migration advice, never a security gate; indirect aliases or arbitrary
use(t) dataflow can be missed. Mixed failures show
schema/device remediation before the gate repair/ack command, explicitly
subject to repairing any refutation first. Gate-only upgrade failures name
check --ack-security <ID> --reason '<spec rationale>'; other failures retain
schema/device remediation. An already published old CLI may still print its
old E_UPGRADE line; the new check child must carry the actionable explanation.
The release card records the 0.4.37 CLI's obsolete closed-door/startDev advice.
The numeric-advance candidate scan requires an actual advance call in the same
local expression/assignment as numeric consumption; unrelated operators or
new Date/assert calls elsewhere in a file do not qualify.

### Protected immutable payloads

A column-read-protected payload may be immutable. On insert its supplied value
is validated normally. On every update/existing-upsert, a supplied protected
immutable field is ignored without comparing it with stored data, for every
viewer, including its readers and the system. Its retained value and asset
placement remain unchanged; no upload ownership or placement check runs for
the ignored field because no asset is adopted (amending LLP 3000.036 §22.9).
An ignored newly uploaded asset remains unplaced under the existing uploader
ownership, accounting and collection lifetime; no extra adoption or extension
is created. Immutability constrains update/existing-upsert within one row
lifetime; delete is independently authorized by the table delete rule. It
does not add an undeletable identity policy or prohibit a newly authorized
insert after deletion. The operation still validates supplied input types/bounds; ignoring occurs
before merge/placement, including explicit null for an optional field. Invalid
operation input still fails using schema/site facts alone. An
existing upsert differing only in these fields succeeds with them retained;
absent-upsert still inserts. Compiler advice on an
explicit patch names that behavior using schema/site facts only. Device
prediction retains the old cell or its omission and never predicts the supplied
cell. Ordinary jobs/system updates follow the same rule; schema-evolution
backfills remain the explicit replacement path. Ordinary unprotected immutability retains
its existing contract. Cap/unique/index/authority/derived dependencies on
protected fields remain refused. This input-independent policy preserves
LLP 3000.036 §22.8's noninterference oracle and needs no new device protocol:
the same interpreter runs it on the server and device.

### Audience helpers and hidden probes

Expression-bodied helpers, or a block containing one return, may appear in
table/column rules when expansion is exactly the existing rule grammar.
Arguments are ordinary typed rule terms, substituted simultaneously without
caller capture. Only parameters, viewer and rule constants may be free in the
helper; a helper's focal dot cannot capture the caller's row. Nested helpers
are bounded to eight levels and 4096 visited syntax nodes per rule (including
arguments and each expanded body), refused as E_QUARRY_RULE_OPERATOR on
exhaustion. Direct and indirect cycles are detected on the expansion stack.
Parameters cannot shadow viewer or rule constants. exists is explicitly
allowed and pays the same probes after expansion/inversion as handwritten
rules; ordinary reads are excluded. Simultaneous substitution prevents capture;
cycles,
wrong arity/types, block effects/lets, arithmetic, clock and ordinary reads
refuse with E_QUARRY_RULE_OPERATOR and an actionable repair. No new runtime
instruction, probe semantics, authority or resource allowance is introduced.
Expansion precedes read-through, index inference, audience inversion and
security analysis so every consumer sees the same canonical predicate.

Program exists remains a caller-visible read; rule exists retains its existing
policy probe semantics. N_UNREADABLE_PROBE and N_LOOKUP_FILTERED name the exact
hiding read/read-through rule and source site, including exists directly in a
require. No runtime refusal confirms whether a hidden row physically exists.
Runnable guide recipes demonstrate protected immutable payloads, a role helper
and a hidden parent probe alongside a deliberately minimal readable projection.

store, drain:false restart and legacy saved boundaries; no double session
shift; production controls and failed-checkpoint atomicity; mounted below-fold
RN-web text and hidden/clip controls; gate-only and refuted upgrade diagnostics;
real and unrelated advance scan candidates; protected immutable equal/different
patches, upserts, assets and server/device observation equality; helper types,
recursion, capture and authority equivalence; hidden program exists source
advice. Every changed guide snippet executes against the fresh pack. Full
five-crate Rust tests, affected client pool, Mac Studio affected platform tests,
qualify-linux --consumer-only (test.sh is prohibited), published 0.4.35/0.4.37
old-CLI upgrades, refs/docs budget and repository-root checks are recorded in
WORKSTREAM-REPORT.md. No version bump, publication or push.

### 19.4 Operator inspection and retained identity

`sql --dormant --release DIR` and `sql --all-releases --release DIR` are
mutually exclusive read-only operator views under one pinned SQLite read
snapshot. The first contains retained whole/narrowed rows and retained cells;
the second includes active and retained images. Removed table names and
retained column names are queryable through generated logical application
relations; internal namespaces remain unavailable to submitted SQL. Factual
state/source metadata distinguish active rows, dormant rows and dormant
cells. A retained-cell record contains its id and retained cells, with
unprovided columns null. Conflicting images are separate labelled records,
never an arbitrary winning value. Inspection-mode reserved-name collisions
refuse instead of shadowing application columns. These modes bypass read
rules as existing owner-only SQL does; they add no HTTP/client namespace.
`all-releases` means every image still retained, not time-travel snapshots
or one invented version of every row per historical release.
`_snapback_columns` is the JSON array of actually provided application cells
on each inspection record, so a retained null is distinguishable from an
unprovided SQL NULL. This is provenance, not a missing-value substitution.

Equivalent retained executable source remains selectable across compiler
upgrades. Compiler reports or equivalent old/new packaging metadata do not
change source identity; actual source, native and worker behavior remain
identity-bearing and full control-request integrity still covers all files.
Ordinary refused deploys leave neither numbered candidates nor stages.
This is a source-bundle identity, not a frozen compiler-binary identity or
a proof of behavior equivalence between compiler versions. Activation
recompiles retained source with the selected runtime and revalidates every
data/authority constraint. Actual compiler semantic fixes can change
execution without minting a new source id; version negotiation and the
normal upgrade/publication qualification remain independent fences.
Backfill/reverse definition identity still includes its normalized typed
IR. Recompilation that changes that historical definition cannot reuse its
old receipts: immutable-name checks refuse and require an explicit new-name
bridge. Selecting the source id across an upgrade is supported; success of
an incompatible historical migration after an arbitrary semantic compiler
change is not promised. Removing the source-identity trap does not bypass
these admission checks.

### 19.5 Argument aliases and review evidence

`explanation: text 1..500 alias reason` declares one canonical argument and
one accepted old spelling on a Quarry query/mutation. Normalize the old
spelling to the canonical value before typed execution, including local
device execution. Both spellings supplied at once refuse E_INPUT even if
equal. Alias names must be unique and cannot collide with canonical args;
deprecated/aliased combinations refuse. Canonical default/null validation
semantics are unchanged. Generated TypeScript accepts either spelling and
rejects both; capability comparison recognizes the old shape accepted by
the alias. Compatibility advice for renamed same-typed inputs recommends
mapping with an alias. It never recommends accepting and dropping the old
value with `deprecated` as a rename repair.

Every changed guide fence/starter runs as written against a freshly packed
tarball. Qualification includes regression stores, actual old callers,
reverse/native/preimage conflicts, forward old-source writes, inspection,
hot/cold/check parity and copied wave-19 dormant lanes. Full test and
differential results and any external qualification limits belong in
WORKSTREAM-REPORT.md. Reviews are mutually blind Muse and fresh Codex
sessions on one immutable semantic revision, retained verbatim with
provenance. A READY verdict records review readiness, not author acceptance
or a publication receipt. No version bump or push is authorized.

These are design readiness verdicts, not an implementation test result, author acceptance or a publication receipt. ## 2. Language

A top-level declaration describes one table's historical input fields and its
simultaneous output patch:

```quarry
backfill bookings vehicleClass(weight: int, length: int):
  { vehicleClass: 'commercial' if weight > 3500 else 'van' if length >= 500 else 'car' }
```

Here length is stored in centimetres. Arguments are typed historical fields;
they remain in the declaration after the candidate drops those fields. The
compiler binds arguments against their explicit types, not today's table.
The runtime validates them against each interpreted historical row. Output
names belong to candidate authoritative columns and may equal input names:
read-old/write-new. Outputs of multiple declarations on a table must be
disjoint. Names are unique per table. Durable store history keeps one
normalized definition per table/name, including gaps; changing it refuses with a
bridge/new-name repair. A new name denotes a new transformation.

The expression body is an object patch. It uses Quarry's existing arithmetic,
comparisons, null handling, conditional expressions, immutable helper expansion,
and bounded deterministic scalar builtins. Supported text additions are
`before(text, delimiter)`, `after`, `beforeLast`, `afterLast`, `startsWith`,
and `allDigits`. Splits remove the first/last exact delimiter; absent delimiter
means whole text before, empty text after. An empty split delimiter refuses.
Digits means nonempty ASCII digits. Text is Unicode, comparisons and splits
are locale independent. Existing `lower`, `trim`, `slice`, `count`, `sha256`,
`first`, `last`, `at`, `min`, `max`, exact `divide`, calendar/civil transforms
and concatenation retain their normal checked semantics. No randomness,
viewer, clock, minted ids, writes, mutation helpers or loops are admitted.
Identity `id` is never an output. Only scalar outputs are admitted, including new references. Assets, lists,
engine-owned/captured/maintained and lifetime outputs refuse, including fields
owned by a historical source lifetime even when the candidate removes that rule.
Restored narrowed rows retain their individual output ownership, captures and
reference types. Columns added by later predecessor schemas contribute their
own types/defaults only when absent from that archived definition. Overwriting an
existing reference/asset is refused: derive a new reference column so the
existing dormant reference-lifetime ledger owns retirement/restoration.

Point reads use the ordinary `members[principal = owner] ! else NOT_FOUND`
spelling. They require a unique point. A missing asserted point refuses;
ordinary nullable point reads and `exists` retain their explicit semantics.
Historical existence predicates may traverse a metered source range, needed
for wave-17 cross-tower references; they return no row collection. A meter
cut is a refusal, never false absence or an incomplete historical value.
Before evaluating any patch, each unique lookup index validates the historical
population's cardinality with bounded privileged duplicate samples. A different
simultaneous patch making candidate keys unique cannot choose an arbitrary old
match. All reads run as the trusted activation system principal, bypassing application
read rules, against the assembled historical snapshot. Dormant namespaces
remain unnameable. Candidate read indexes are built over that source snapshot
only when their component fields exist there. No JavaScript or app migration
operation receives this authority. All declarations are self-contained: they
cannot read another declaration's outputs in the same activation.

## 3. Adoption and reversibility

The existing activation writer transaction owns this sequence:

1. Assemble retained source rows/cells and their source definitions using the
   existing restoration intent. This includes narrowed rows eligible to return:
   restore their full historical images before evaluation, deferring only the
   backfill output columns' candidate-type checks. Retained reference invalidation,
   source lifetime and audience checks still apply before restoration. Retire
   source-expired lifetimes before any backfill; a transform cannot extend an
   expiry or opening boundary.
2. When leaving a declaration, restore its predecessor-visible output fields
   from saved **output preimages**, separately from its argument inputs. Save
   interpreted old values, including literal defaults/nulls, and absence.
   Added output columns use ordinary dormant retention instead. A different
   current value from both recorded output and preimage refuses with a conflict;
   untouched same-column enum renames restore their exact original value.
   Membership is per row lifetime, with normalized declaration definitions kept
   beside evidence. Removing a whole table suspends its membership rather than
   feeding transformed values back as inputs. Re-entering that declaration reads
   its original overwritten inputs and preserves its current result; selecting
   original source instead reverses it even when the immediately previous schema
   lacked the table/declaration. Ordinary active deletion clears membership.
3. Enter declarations absent from the immediately active schema. Unchanged
   carry-forward and reopening run none for ordinary active rows: candidate-native
   writes are not historical rows. Newly restored narrowed rows receive carried
   declarations too, including a declaration introduced while they were dormant. On re-entry, restored cells/rows are eligible along with
   older active rows. A native v2 row whose absent historical inputs have a
   complete valid restored output image keeps that image. Older v1 rows lacking
   outputs are filled. Declarations must accompany skipped-release source;
   no unseen intermediate transformation is implicitly inferred.
4. Evaluate every entering patch against one assembled source snapshot, with
   ordinary per-row job meters and a paged source walk. Stage output/evidence
   in SQLite temp storage before applying any patch. Recompute the patch on
   re-entry, so changed lookup values count as changed dependencies even when
   declared arguments are unchanged. Compare both saved argument values and
   computed outputs. If unchanged, preserve edited added-column cells. If
   changed, recompute only when current outputs equal the saved result or the
   reversed preimage; otherwise refuse the conflict. Never overwrite a manual
   output edit because another input happened to change. A fully reversed
   same-column output edited during absence also refuses re-entry when its
   current interpreted value equals neither preimage nor saved result, even
   with unchanged arguments. Sparse defaults count by interpreted value.
5. Apply patches and evidence together. Run ordinary narrowing/reference
   closure, projection, complete type/asset validation, indexes, uniqueness,
   caps and maintained-data rebuilds. Transformed dependencies force rebuilds
   even when their declarations did not change. Publish programs, generation
   and release in the same commit. Any error rolls everything back.

Evidence is keyed by table number, row lifetime and the SHA-256 digest of the
normalized declaration/typed IR (no diagnostic locations or whitespace).
It records declared input values, each overwritten output preimage and computed
output. Ordinary authoritative deletion/expiry deletes that row's evidence in
its transaction; id reuse cannot inherit it. Row moves to dormant storage
preserve lifetime. Backfill definitions enter schema/activation identity;
changed rows also set activation population, forcing a fresh generation and
device reacquisition. Public/device schema projections omit all backfill
metadata; devices never execute migrations. A successful evidence installation
sets `snapback4-release-store-6`, transactionally. Unaware binaries refuse that
format even when an old source is selected. Failed installation retains the
previous format. Legacy stores without backfills remain unchanged. Prune previews
backfill preimages separately, with counts, bytes and bounded ids. Explicit
prune deletes their values and keeps only per-lifetime reversal-unavailable
identities. Leaving an overwritten column then refuses with a pruned-preimage
reason: select a bridge retaining present meaning or restore a backup. Active
row deletion clears these identities too. Pruning added-column evidence does
not prevent rollback when no old-visible field needs a preimage.

Dropping a declaration has explicit semantic meaning: leave it by restoring
preimages or refusing conflicts. Keep declarations in every later source
whose view should retain their meaning. Refusals include exact affected counts
and up to five bounded privileged samples. Missing inputs/lookups, output/type
errors, arithmetic/meter errors and uniqueness/cap conflicts refuse atomically;
a widened/optional non-unique bridge plus an authorized repair mutation is the
repair path. A post-backfill-edit conflict requires keeping the current/bridge
source, deciding a truthful old representation, then repairing it before
selecting old source. No automatic fallback turns a new enum value into an old
meaning. Native v2 rows with no preimage retain the parent's existing law:
forward selection may make incompatible present values dormant; rollback
requires representability and refuses with its existing reason. This amendment
does not change REL5's rollback or restoration selection policy.

`deploy --check` uses the existing private disk snapshot, deploy direction and
restoration intent; rollback previews use rollback direction. Later live writes
can change its plan. `backfills` in both cold/hot JSON receipts and exact preview
text reports `table name transformed N rows` or `table name reversed on N rows`.
These are transformed row visits, separate from the parent's disjoint dormant
rows/cells/index entries. They are not additive counts of distinct rows across
different transformations, and do not claim a frozen production population.

Consumed historical references must retain valid parent-lifetime evidence even
when the input column is dropped. Expired predecessor/restored images are retired
before any transform, including ordinary expiry without read-through. Pruning
keeps membership-only tombstones solely for surviving lifetimes; such a tombstone
refuses re-entry requiring lost values rather than treating the row as fresh.

Re-entry retains an adoption-cohort identity with each active row receipt.
All declarations first applied in one activation, including continuously carried
declarations on tables that were never removed, read one reconstructed source
snapshot, including point/range indexes; this overlays that cohort's complete
input evidence and output preimages (including original absence) on current
images. Distinct original cohorts are evaluated separately against their
respective historical overlays, so a declaration introduced later can read an
earlier declaration's already-transformed values. Computed and retained/native output values must fit candidate scalar types and
text minimum/format constraints before narrowing; invalid generated outputs
refuse the entire adoption with affected-row samples. Patches remain staged until
all cohorts succeed, and current row images are restored before applying them.
New declarations use the current assembled predecessor snapshot. Inactive
cohorts restore their own still-omitted input interpretations, independently of
new declarations in that activation, and catch up changed authored inputs. Declared historical arguments consume retained
cells and source definitions even when the candidate omits those columns;
retained parent-lifetime bindings still apply. A native-image exception requires a birth marker for this exact declaration,
a matching fingerprint of the original non-output inputs, and valid, complete outputs;
a new declaration never skips valid old inputs merely because an older
transformation left a retained output. Receipt reference inputs use private namespaced bindings in the existing
dormant parent-lifetime ledger, independently of the live input column. A
retargeted/dropped input cannot substitute its current binding for the saved
reference. Every unchanged saved argument consumed during active or inactive
re-entry validates its private lifetime binding before program evaluation.
Bindings use each restored narrowed row's actual archived reference type
(rather than the table-wide predecessor), even when an
argument is declared as generic `id`. Existing private bindings survive
re-entry with unchanged saved inputs even after the visible column becomes
generic `id`; schema type erasure cannot recapture the same parent id. Reconstruction validates any consumed saved binding before restoring
its value. Original absence is recorded separately from the predecessor's interpreted
value. A physically missing field consumes its source default when present,
otherwise null; receipts retain that interpretation explicitly, preventing
later defaults from changing argument or lookup/index evaluation. Expired sources retire when entering or leaving the
last declaration, including restored table images.

When transformed rows become dormant, candidate output reference types supply
parent bindings even when the predecessor had no such column. Existing invalid
bindings are preserved. If a restored row becomes dormant again, only its
explicitly backfilled authoritative cells update the retained image; original
source definition, other cells and authority provenance stay intact. Forward
selection after rollback reconstructs absent added outputs for that same
declaration before evaluating its arguments, rather than feeding it its own
retained result.

Input omission is stored independently from interpreted null/default values,
both for the current physical lifetime and for each receipt. Ordinary writes
compare interpreted old/new inputs; meaningful edits end omission provenance,
while unrelated writes and default materialization do not. Receipts reconstruct
their original interpretation of omitted fields across later default changes.

Rows inserted while a declaration is active receive a private native-birth
marker keyed by its normalized definition and a hash of their input image;
no original input values are kept in that marker. The same declaration preserves
complete native images with unchanged inputs, including absent optional inputs,
constant transforms and whole-table restoration. Changed native inputs, invalid
present inputs, lost native output images or conflicting other-transform history
refuse with a bridge reason. An inactive overlapping receipt is compatible only
when every overlapping current output equals its recorded preimage; an active
or pruned conflicting history still refuses. Same-column output values belong
to the candidate type, rather than the historical input type, for native rows.
A new declaration name does not inherit another
name's birth marker. Prune retains only native fingerprints for surviving
lifetimes, alongside current omission flags; receipt absence flags are forgotten
with historical values. Delete/reset remove every marker for the ended lifetime.

Source retirement uses the ordinary engine expiry/reference-action closure
under the complete assembled predecessor schema, before any patch runs or
removed table is archived. Only surviving post-action rows enter dormant storage. Restored source
indexes and cap occupancy are rebuilt first; writing-reference groups must fit
the source reservation's live bound. Cascade and set-null effects participate
in this same transaction; restrict or retained-child blockers refuse the entire
adoption. Retired source population forces a new generation so clients cannot
keep children removed by a cascade. Authoritative set-null updates corresponding archived cells/narrowed images
to null before clearing old column bindings, while receipt input bindings remain
independent. An expired
narrowed source is exposed only inside this uncommitted lifecycle phase and
cannot reach a backfill evaluation alive.

Development reset still empties every lifetime's data/history/native/omission
markers, then reconstructs code-derived declaration/input registries from its
retained schema before reseeding; the new identity's rows receive new birth
markers. No release HTTP reset door is added.

Evidence decode is preflighted against
the existing job heap ceiling. Text preview lists private preimages separately
from dormant application rows/cells/index entries.

### Per-column transformed views

A receipt records a `retained_outputs` set independently of its declaration's
active flag. When leaving a declaration, candidate-visible transformed outputs
reverse to their preimages. Outputs absent from the candidate remain transformed
in dormant storage; their set membership survives until reversal or re-entry.
Even an inactive declaration reverses those suspended fields when a later plain
schema exposes them. Whole-table suspension keeps the transformed view intact.

Re-entry preserves the saved preimage for each still-transformed output. A
fully reversed output instead captures its actual assembled source value,
including restored dormant cells and interpreted predecessor defaults, before
applying the patch. Input history and rollback preimages are separate: an input
may retain its original omitted/null interpretation while the latest rollback
view has a literal default. Ordinary writes do not erase omission provenance
when they edit a transformed output from that same original cohort.

An inverse restoring genuine absence records a transaction-local source mask.
Later evaluation cannot fill that absence from the suspended result or a default
belonging to the output schema. The mask is dropped on every exit. Earlier
recorded cohorts' staged patches are replayed into later recorded source views;
fresh declarations still share the untouched predecessor snapshot and cannot
read each other's newly staged outputs. Every decode remains heap-admitted.

Reference arguments copy any matching ordinary cell-lifetime bindings before
restoration consumes that ledger, including transformed reference cells absent
from an archived whole-row definition. Unchanged saved inputs retain existing
private bindings; inferred current bindings cannot overwrite their invalidation.
The stable format is `snapback4-release-store-6`, with schema capability
`release-backfills-v3`. Published 0.4.33's store format 3 upgrades on first use.
Unpublished experimental formats 4/5 and the older capabilities cannot silently read
this evidence layout. Package versions are not changed in this workstream.

//! A process-local clock for development and explicitly classified staging stores.
use snapback4_core::error::Refusal;
use std::time::{Duration, Instant};

// @ref LLP 3000.000.000.001#8-wave-18-developer-loop-amendment-dx4 — preserve expiry tests and tell callers how to recover.
pub(crate) const SESSION_NOTICE: &str = "Time travel also advances session expiry and unplaced-upload collection: seven-day password sessions may now be expired. Sign in again after the jump and upload unplaced bytes after it; never replay an ambiguous clock advance.";

pub(crate) const PRESERVED_SESSION_NOTICE: &str = "Authenticated development and staging sessions keep their remaining lifetime across this jump; sessions:expire explicitly tests credential expiry. Unplaced uploads still age. Never replay an ambiguous advance.";

const MAX_TIME: i64 = snapback4_core::value::I53_MAX;

pub fn input(message: impl Into<String>) -> Refusal { Refusal::new("E_CLOCK_INPUT", "clock", message).guide("clock") }
pub fn release() -> Refusal { Refusal::new("E_CLOCK_DEV_ONLY", "clock", "clock controls require dev or a deploy --staging store; production always uses wall time").guide("clock") }

/// Isolated time tests stay inside an assigned range, or request a free port.
pub(crate) fn isolated_command() -> String {
    isolated_range(std::env::var("PORT_LO").ok().as_deref(), std::env::var("PORT_HI").ok().as_deref())
}
fn isolated_range(lo: Option<&str>, hi: Option<&str>) -> String {
    match (lo.and_then(|n| n.parse::<u16>().ok()), hi.and_then(|n| n.parse::<u16>().ok())) {
        (Some(lo), Some(hi)) if lo > 0 && hi >= lo => format!("dev --memory --port {lo}-{hi} within the current range"),
        _ => "dev --memory --port 0".into(),
    }
}

pub fn duration(text: &str) -> Result<i64, Refusal> {
    if text.starts_with('-') { return Err(input("clock advance cannot move backwards; use a positive duration such as +2m")); }
    let text = text.strip_prefix('+').unwrap_or(text);
    let split = text.bytes().position(|b| !b.is_ascii_digit()).unwrap_or(text.len());
    let (number, unit) = text.split_at(split);
    let multiplier = match unit { "ms" => 1, "s" => 1_000, "m" => 60_000, "h" => 3_600_000, "d" => 86_400_000, "w" => 604_800_000, _ => 0 };
    number.parse::<i64>().ok().and_then(|n| n.checked_mul(multiplier)).filter(|n| *n > 0 && *n <= MAX_TIME)
        .ok_or_else(|| input("expected a positive whole duration with ms, s, m, h, d or w, such as +25h"))
}

pub fn offset(text: &str) -> Result<i64, Refusal> {
    if let Some(rest) = text.strip_prefix('-') { duration(rest).map(|n| -n) } else { duration(text) }
}

fn time(value: Option<i64>) -> Result<i64, Refusal> {
    value.filter(|n| (0..=MAX_TIME).contains(n)).ok_or_else(|| input("time must be Unix milliseconds in 0..9007199254740991; choose a smaller time or duration"))
}

pub fn run_now(text: Option<&String>) -> Result<i64, Refusal> {
    let now = snapback4_core::now_ms();
    match text {
        None => Ok(now),
        Some(text) if text.starts_with('+') => time(now.checked_add(duration(text)?)),
        Some(text) => time(text.parse().ok()),
    }
}

#[derive(Clone, Debug)]
pub struct Clock {
    dev: bool,
    base: i64,
    offset: i64,
    started: Instant,
    epoch: String,
    revision: u64,
    #[cfg(test)]
    frozen: bool,
}

impl Clock {
    pub fn new(dev: bool, offset: i64) -> Result<Self, Refusal> {
        if !dev && offset != 0 { return Err(release()); }
        Ok(Self { dev, offset, base: time(snapback4_core::now_ms().checked_add(offset))?, started: Instant::now(),
            epoch: snapback4_core::ids::mint(snapback4_core::now_ms(), &mut snapback4_core::ids::Mint::default()).map_err(|e| e.refusal())?, revision: 0, #[cfg(test)] frozen: false })
    }
    pub fn staging(offset: i64, floor: i64) -> Result<Self, Refusal> {
        let mut clock = Self::new(true, offset)?;
        let delta = floor.saturating_sub(clock.now()).max(0);
        if delta > 0 { clock.advance(delta)?; }
        Ok(clock)
    }
    #[cfg(test)]
    pub(crate) fn freeze(&mut self, now: i64) { self.base = now; self.frozen = true; }
    pub fn now(&self) -> i64 {
        #[cfg(test)] if self.frozen { return self.base; }
        if !self.dev { return snapback4_core::now_ms(); }
        self.base.saturating_add(self.started.elapsed().as_millis().min(MAX_TIME as u128) as i64).min(MAX_TIME)
    }
    /// Identifies samples taken before/after a restart or deliberate clock jump.
    pub fn offset(&self) -> i64 { self.offset }
    pub fn revision(&self) -> String { format!("{}:{}", self.epoch, self.revision) }
    pub fn presence_now(&self) -> i64 {
        // Production grace is elapsed real time, unaffected by wall-clock corrections.
        if self.dev { self.now() } else { self.started.elapsed().as_millis().min(MAX_TIME as u128) as i64 }
    }
    /// Back to wall time: the offset `--clock-offset` and every advance added
    /// is dropped (a development reset's `{"clock":"reset"}`).
    pub fn reset(&mut self) -> Result<i64, Refusal> {
        if !self.dev { return Err(release()); }
        self.revision += 1;
        self.offset = 0;
        self.base = snapback4_core::now_ms();
        self.started = Instant::now();
        #[cfg(test)] { self.frozen = false; }
        Ok(self.base)
    }
    pub fn advance(&mut self, milliseconds: i64) -> Result<i64, Refusal> {
        if !self.dev { return Err(release()); }
        if milliseconds <= 0 { return Err(input("clock advance cannot move backwards; use a positive duration such as +2m")); }
        let next = time(self.now().checked_add(milliseconds))?;
        let offset = self.offset.checked_add(milliseconds).ok_or_else(|| input("cumulative clock offset overflow"))?;
        self.revision += 1;
        self.offset = offset;
        self.base = next;
        self.started = Instant::now();
        Ok(next)
    }
}

/// The control CLI only talks to a loopback dev host. It does not open its store.
pub fn command(options: &crate::verbs::Options) -> i32 {
    let result = (|| -> Result<String, String> {
        let draining = options.positionals == ["drain"];
        if !draining && (options.positionals.len() != 2 || options.positionals[0] != "advance") {
            return Err(input(format!("usage: {}", crate::cli_flags::synopsis("clock"))).to_string());
        }
        let duration_text = if draining { "0ms" } else { options.positionals[1].as_str() };
        if !draining { duration(duration_text).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?; }
        let release_path = options.flags.get("release").map(|dir| std::path::PathBuf::from(dir).join("store.sqlite"));
        if release_path.is_some() && options.flags.contains_key("port") { return Err("clock --release does not accept --port".into()); }
        let (address, instance, token) = if let Some(path) = &release_path {
            let store = snapback4_core::store_port::AnyStore::open_read_only(path).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?;
            if store.deployment_environment().map_err(|e|e.to_string())?.as_deref() != Some("staging") { return Err(release().to_string()); }
            let address = crate::store_owner::inspector_address(path).ok_or("staging clock requires a running serve")?;
            let session = crate::serve::token::mint_live(path, "clock", 1).map_err(|(_,error)|error)?;
            (address,None,Some(session.token))
        } else {
            let (address,instance)=crate::verbs::dev_address(options)?;
            let token=crate::verbs::mint_for_dev(address,"operator",1)?;
            (address,instance,Some(token))
        };
        let mut body = serde_json::json!({"duration":duration_text,"eachBoundary":options.flags.contains_key("each-boundary"), "drain":!options.flags.contains_key("no-drain"), "allowHeld":options.flags.contains_key("allow-held")});
        if options.flags.contains_key("expire-sessions") { body["sessions"] = "expire".into(); }
        if draining && options.flags.contains_key("no-drain") { return Err(input("clock drain does not accept --no-drain").to_string()); }
        let body = body.to_string();
        // The common HTTP/1.0 exchange is EOF-delimited and bounds replies at
        // 64 MiB, enough for the preflight's complete explicit window report.
        let response = if release_path.is_some() {
            crate::verbs::run_forward::exchange_authorized(address,"POST","/dev/clock/advance",None,token.as_deref(),&body,Duration::from_secs(60))
        } else {
            crate::verbs::run_forward::exchange_dev_authorized(address,"/dev/clock/advance",instance.as_deref(),token.as_deref().unwrap(),&body,Duration::from_secs(60))
        };
        let (status, json) = response.map_err(|error| {
            let evidence = crate::verbs::run_forward::exchange(address, "GET", "/dev/status", None, "", Duration::from_secs(5)).ok()
                .map(|(_, status)| format!("now={}, skewMs={}", status["now"], status["skewMs"]))
                .unwrap_or_else(|| "clock status unavailable".into());
            format!("clock advance reply failed at {address}: {error}; time may already have advanced ({evidence}); inspect /dev/status and queued work before retrying; for an isolated dev pass --port N")
        })?;
        if let Some(notice) = json["sessionNotice"].as_str() { eprintln!("clock: {notice}"); }
        if let Some(held) = json["held"].as_array() {
            for schedule in held.iter().filter(|s| s["holdCode"] == "saved-boundary") {
                eprintln!("clock: schedule {}: {}", schedule["name"].as_str().unwrap_or("?"), schedule["reason"].as_str().unwrap_or("held"));
            }
        }
        if let Some(denied) = json.get("denied") {
            if json.get("now").is_some() {
                eprintln!("clock: incomplete advance report (time already moved): {}", serde_json::json!({"now":json["now"],"fired":json["fired"],"held":json["held"]}));
            }
            let clock = json["message"].as_str().map(|message| format!("; time remains advanced: {message}")).unwrap_or_default();
            return Err(format!("{}: {}{clock}\n  guide: clock", denied["code"].as_str().unwrap_or("E_CLOCK_INPUT"), denied["message"].as_str().unwrap_or("clock advance failed")));
        }
        if status != 200 { return Err(format!("dev refused clock advance: {json}")); }
        Ok(json.to_string())
    })();
    match result { Ok(body) => { println!("{body}"); 0 }, Err(error) => { eprintln!("{error}"); 1 } }
}

        }
    }
}

//! Bounded, read-only evidence from the store's own commit clock.
use snapback4_core::{Engine, RuntimeError};
use std::collections::BTreeSet;

const SKIPPED_PREVIEW: usize = 32;
const TOLERANCE_MS: i64 = 5_000;
const RECENT_COMMITS: usize = 64;
const TABLE_BYTES: usize = 4096;
const TABLE_NAMES: usize = 8;
const REPORT_ERROR_BYTES: usize = 1024;
// Reserve one MiB for the outer clock/work envelope inside the host encoder.
// The reply retains `schedules` and its fired/held partition.
const REPORT_BYTE_BUDGET: usize = (crate::serve::JSON_RESPONSE_CEILING - (1 << 20)) / 2;

// @ref LLP 3000.007#20.12 — inspect a fixed recent commit window, never application row values.
pub fn ahead(engine: &Engine, now: i64) -> Result<Option<String>, RuntimeError> {
    // Reverse primary-key traversal: no full scan, sort, row payloads or retained
    // historical stamp set. octet_length(column) uses SQLite record metadata;
    // unlike a text length/cast/substr, it need not load oversized payloads.
    let mut lead = 0;
    let mut tables = BTreeSet::new();
    let mut omitted = false;
    engine.store().visit_recent_commit_clock(now.saturating_add(TOLERANCE_MS), TABLE_BYTES as i64, RECENT_COMMITS as i64, |record| {
        let at = record.at;
        if at <= now.saturating_add(TOLERANCE_MS) { return Ok(()); }
        lead = lead.max(at.saturating_sub(now));
        let length = record.length.ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::Store("commit clock record is missing touched length".into()))?;
        if length > TABLE_BYTES as i64 { omitted = true; return Ok(()); }
        let bytes = record.touched.ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::Store("commit clock record is missing touched bytes".into()))?;
        match serde_json::from_slice::<Vec<String>>(&bytes) {
            Ok(names) => for name in names {
                if tables.contains(&name) { continue; }
                if tables.len() < TABLE_NAMES && name.len() <= 128 { tables.insert(name); }
                else { omitted = true; }
            },
            Err(_) => omitted = true,
        }
        Ok(())
    })?;
    if lead == 0 { return Ok(None); }
    let mut names = tables.into_iter().collect::<Vec<_>>();
    if omitted { names.push("additional tables omitted".into()); }
    if names.is_empty() { names.push("no table names recorded".into()); }
    Ok(Some(format!(
        "N_CLOCK_STORE_AHEAD: recent commit stamps lead the current clock by up to {lead}ms (latest {RECENT_COMMITS} commits; tables: {}); use a fresh store per offset test ({}), or restart with the same --clock-offset (or dev --resume-clock) to continue",
        names.join(", "), crate::dev_clock::isolated_command())))
}

/// UTC calendar text for Unix milliseconds: schedule boundaries are UTC.
pub fn utc(ms: i64) -> String {
    let (days, rem) = (ms.div_euclid(86_400_000), ms.rem_euclid(86_400_000));
    // Civil-from-days (proleptic Gregorian), exact for the I53 range.
    let z = days + 719_468;
    let era = z.div_euclid(146_097);
    let doe = z - era * 146_097;
    let yoe = (doe - doe / 1_460 + doe / 36_524 - doe / 146_096) / 365;
    let doy = doe - (365 * yoe + yoe / 4 - yoe / 100);
    let mp = (5 * doy + 2) / 153;
    let day = doy - (153 * mp + 2) / 5 + 1;
    let month = if mp < 10 { mp + 3 } else { mp - 9 };
    let year = yoe + era * 400 + i64::from(month <= 2);
    format!("{year:04}-{month:02}-{day:02}T{:02}:{:02}:{:02}Z", rem / 3_600_000, rem % 3_600_000 / 60_000, rem % 60_000 / 1_000)
}

/// A positive duration in the units `clock advance` accepts, rounded up to a second.
pub fn span(ms: i64) -> String {
    let seconds = (ms.max(0) + 999) / 1_000;
    let (d, h, m, s) = (seconds / 86_400, seconds % 86_400 / 3_600, seconds % 3_600 / 60, seconds % 60);
    let parts: Vec<String> = [(d, "d"), (h, "h"), (m, "m"), (s, "s")].into_iter().filter(|(n, _)| *n > 0).map(|(n, unit)| format!("{n}{unit}")).collect();
    if parts.is_empty() { "0s".into() } else { parts.join("") }
}

/// Each schedule's saved boundary, in declaration order.
pub fn saved_boundaries(engine: &Engine) -> Vec<Option<i64>> {
    engine.schema().schedules.iter()
        .map(|schedule| {
            let shape = engine.store().schedule_shape(&schedule.name).ok().flatten();
            if shape.as_ref().is_some_and(|shape| *shape != snapback4_core::value::digest(schedule)) { return None; }
            engine.store().schedule_boundary(&schedule.name).ok().flatten()
        })
        .collect()
}

/// Preflight is bounded before the clock or worker leases change.
// @ref LLP 3000.007#20.12.1 — only explicit execution enumerates all crossed windows; coalesced reports keep a bounded preview.
pub fn crossed_boundaries(engine: &Engine, before: &[Option<i64>], now: i64, held: bool, each_boundary: bool) -> Result<Vec<(usize, i64)>, snapback4_core::Refusal> {
    let mut boundaries = Vec::new();
    let mut report_bytes = 0usize;
    for (index, schedule) in engine.schema().schedules.iter().enumerate() {
        // JSON may escape every error byte; count that bound per schedule.
        report_bytes = report_bytes.saturating_add(2048 + 6 * (REPORT_ERROR_BYTES + 3) + 8 * (schedule.name.len() + schedule.target.len()));
        if report_bytes > REPORT_BYTE_BUDGET { return Err(crate::dev_clock::input("schedule report exceeds its byte budget; reduce the declared schedules")); }
        if held { continue; }
        let Some(old) = before.get(index).copied().flatten() else { continue; };
        let end = schedule.boundary(now);
        if old >= end { continue; }
        let periods = schedule.periods_between(old, end);
        if each_boundary && periods > (10_000 - boundaries.len()) as i64 {
            return Err(crate::dev_clock::input("--each-boundary crosses more than 10000 schedule windows; use a smaller duration or omit --each-boundary to coalesce"));
        }
        report_bytes = report_bytes.saturating_add((if each_boundary { periods as usize } else { (periods as usize).min(SKIPPED_PREVIEW + 1) }).saturating_mul(1024 + 8 * schedule.target.len()));
        if report_bytes > REPORT_BYTE_BUDGET { return Err(crate::dev_clock::input("schedule window report exceeds its byte budget; use a smaller duration")); }
        if !each_boundary { continue; }
        let mut boundary = schedule.next(old);
        while boundary <= end {
            boundaries.push((index, boundary));
            boundary = schedule.next(boundary);
        }
    }
    boundaries.sort_by_key(|(index, at)| (*at, *index));
    Ok(boundaries)
}

// @ref LLP 3000.000.000.001#11-wave-22-loop-repairs-dx6 — no silent successful advance behind retained schedule markers.
pub fn refuse_held(engine: &Engine, before: &[Option<i64>], current_clock: i64, now: i64) -> Result<(), snapback4_core::Refusal> {
    for (schedule, saved) in engine.schema().schedules.iter().zip(before) {
        // Equality is the already processed current window, not a hold. The
        // authoritative requested clock defines the window, never wall time.
        if let Some(saved) = saved.filter(|saved| *saved > schedule.boundary(now)) {
            return Err(snapback4_core::Refusal::new("E_CLOCK_HELD", "clock", format!(
                "schedule {} already processed boundary {}; requested clock {} is in an earlier window; next unprocessed boundary is {}. Nothing advanced. Advance {} to reach the saved window or use a fresh staging deployment; allowHeld:true explicitly accepts this hold. Never rewind saved boundaries",
                schedule.name, utc(saved), utc(now), utc(schedule.next(saved)), format!("{}ms", saved.saturating_sub(current_clock)))).guide("clock"));
        }
    }
    Ok(())
}

fn period(schedule: &snapback4_core::schema::Schedule) -> String {
    schedule.describe()
}

/// What each schedule did on one clock advance, and why one did not fire.
/// The core fires each schedule at most once per advance, for the latest
/// boundary, and holds it while its saved boundary is ahead of the clock.
// @ref LLP 3000#7-decisions-during-execution — missed boundaries fire once; this only explains them.
pub fn schedule_report(engine: &Engine, before: &[Option<i64>], fired: &[String], now: i64, current_clock: i64, held: bool, each_boundary: bool, port: Option<u16>) -> Vec<serde_json::Value> {
    let after = saved_boundaries(engine);
    // Read only bounded summaries: job args/cursors and full error payloads
    // are irrelevant to this report and must not be loaded then replicated.
    let runs = job_summaries(engine).unwrap_or_default();
    engine.schema().schedules.iter().enumerate().map(|(index, schedule)| {
        let (old, saved) = (before.get(index).copied().flatten(), after.get(index).copied().flatten());
        let current = schedule.boundary(now);
        let next = schedule.next(current);
        let mut entry = serde_json::json!({ "name": schedule.name, "target": schedule.target, "period": period(schedule), "fired": false, "firedWindows": [], "skippedBoundaries": [], "nextBoundary": utc(next) });
        if fired.contains(&schedule.name) {
            let end = saved.unwrap_or(current);
            entry["fired"] = true.into();
            let start = schedule.previous(end);
            let window = |boundary| serde_json::json!({"periodStart": schedule.previous(boundary), "periodEnd": boundary});
            let mut windows = Vec::new();
            if each_boundary {
                if let Some(old) = old {
                    let mut at = schedule.next(old);
                    while at <= end { windows.push(window(at)); at = schedule.next(at); }
                }
            } else { windows.push(window(end)); }
            if each_boundary {
                entry["reason"] = format!("fired {} windows: {}", windows.len(), windows.iter().map(|window| format!("{} to {}", utc(window["periodStart"].as_i64().unwrap()), utc(window["periodEnd"].as_i64().unwrap()))).collect::<Vec<_>>().join("; ")).into();
            }
            entry["firedWindows"] = windows.into();
            entry["periodStart"] = start.into();
            entry["periodEnd"] = end.into();
            if !each_boundary { entry["reason"] = format!("fired for the period {} to {}", utc(start), utc(end)).into(); }
            if let Some(run) = runs.get(&schedule.target) { entry["run"] = run.clone(); }
            // @ref LLP 3000.040#24-monthly-and-yearly-schedules — calendar periods count boundaries, not milliseconds.
            let skipped = if each_boundary { 0 } else { old.map(|old| schedule.periods_between(old, end) - 1).unwrap_or(0) };
            if skipped > 0 {
                entry["skippedPeriods"] = skipped.into();
                let mut windows = Vec::new();
                let mut at = schedule.next(old.unwrap());
                while at < end && windows.len() < SKIPPED_PREVIEW {
                    let mut item = window(at);
                    let mut args = serde_json::Map::new();
                    if let Some(program) = engine.program(&schedule.target) {
                        if program.args.contains_key("periodStart") { args.insert("periodStart".into(), schedule.previous(at).into()); }
                        if program.args.contains_key("periodEnd") { args.insert("periodEnd".into(), at.into()); }
                    }
                    item["command"] = format!("snapback4 run {} '{}'{}", schedule.target, serde_json::Value::Object(args), port.map(|port| format!(" --port {port}")).unwrap_or_default()).into();
                    windows.push(item); at = schedule.next(at);
                }
                entry["skippedBoundariesOmitted"] = (skipped - windows.len() as i64).into();
                entry["skippedBoundaries"] = windows.into();
                entry["reason"] = format!("fired once, for the latest period {} to {}; {skipped} earlier period{} this advance crossed (from {}) did not run: missed boundaries fire once. Run {} with periodStart/periodEnd for an earlier period",
                    utc(start), utc(end), if skipped == 1 { "" } else { "s" }, utc(old.unwrap_or(end)), schedule.target).into();
            }
        } else if held {
            entry["holdCode"] = "disabled".into();
            entry["reason"] = "schedules are held by explicit maintenance control; clock drain resumes paused work (a staging pause survives restart); dev --hold-schedules/--replay require restarting without the flag".into();
        } else if let Some(saved) = saved.filter(|saved| old.is_some() && *saved > current) {
            let due = schedule.next(saved);
            entry["holdCode"] = "saved-boundary".into();
            entry["savedBoundary"] = saved.into();
            entry["heldUntil"] = utc(saved).into();
            entry["nextBoundary"] = utc(due).into();
            entry["reason"] = format!("held by the store: boundary {} was already processed and this clock {} is in an earlier window (an explicitly selected clock is behind the saved boundary). One fire per boundary. Advance {} more to reach the saved window, or use a fresh staging deployment/store for an independent test; keep the same --clock-offset when continuing a test across serve restarts",
                utc(saved), utc(now), span(saved.saturating_sub(current_clock))).into();
        } else if old != saved {
            entry["holdCode"] = "initialized".into();
            entry["reason"] = format!("{} started at boundary {}: a schedule fires at the boundary after the first one it sees, next {} ({} from now)",
                if old.is_none() { "this schedule" } else { "this changed schedule" }, utc(saved.unwrap_or(current)), utc(next), span(next - now)).into();
        } else {
            entry["holdCode"] = "not-due".into();
            entry["reason"] = format!("next boundary {} ({} from now; schedule times are UTC)", utc(next), span(next - now)).into();
        }
        entry
    }).collect()
}

// @ref LLP 3000.000.000.001#10-wave-20-loop-repairs-dx5 — every declared schedule has one reported outcome.
pub fn outcomes(schedules: &[serde_json::Value]) -> (Vec<serde_json::Value>, Vec<serde_json::Value>) {
    schedules.iter().cloned().partition(|schedule| schedule["fired"] == true)
}

/// The report never loads full error/argument payloads from retained jobs.
fn job_summaries(engine: &Engine) -> Result<std::collections::BTreeMap<String, serde_json::Value>, RuntimeError> {
    let mut summaries = std::collections::BTreeMap::new();
    for summary in engine.store().job_summaries(REPORT_ERROR_BYTES)? {
        let name = summary.name;
        if summaries.contains_key(&name) { continue; }
        let mut error = summary.error;
        let truncated = summary.error_truncated;
        if let Some(text) = &mut error && truncated {
            let mut end = REPORT_ERROR_BYTES.min(text.len());
            while !text.is_char_boundary(end) { end -= 1; }
            text.truncate(end); text.push('…');
        }
        summaries.insert(name, serde_json::json!({ "id":summary.id, "state":summary.state,
            "rowsWritten":summary.rows_written, "error":error, "errorTruncated":truncated }));
    }
    Ok(summaries)
}

/// Startup lines for schedules the store holds: their saved boundary leads the clock.
pub fn held_schedules(engine: &Engine, now: i64) -> Vec<String> {
    engine.schema().schedules.iter().zip(saved_boundaries(engine)).filter_map(|(schedule, saved)| {
        let saved = saved.filter(|saved| *saved > schedule.boundary(now))?;
        let due = schedule.next(saved);
        Some(format!("clock     schedule {} is held until {} ({} from now): catch up to its saved boundary; next unprocessed window {}; `clock advance {}` or restart with dev --resume-clock",
            schedule.name, utc(saved), span(saved - now), utc(due), format!("{}ms", saved - now)))
    }).collect()
}

/// The dev clock offset that continues a store's own time: its newest
/// recent commit stamp (or saved schedule boundary) less wall time, never negative.
pub fn resume_offset(store: &snapback4_core::store_port::AnyStore, wall: i64) -> Result<i64, RuntimeError> {
    let commit = store.recent_commit_max_at(RECENT_COMMITS as i64)?;
    let boundary = store.latest_schedule_boundary()?;
    let latest = commit.into_iter().chain(boundary).max().unwrap_or(wall);
    // Whole seconds, rounded up, so restarting twice does not drift backwards.
    Ok((latest.saturating_sub(wall).max(0) + 999) / 1_000 * 1_000)
}

pub fn has_data(engine: &Engine) -> bool {
    engine.store().inspection_has_data().unwrap_or(false)
}

pub fn offset_line(offset: i64, running: bool) -> String {
    format!("clock     offset from wall time: {offset:+}ms{}", if running { " (development process; staging persists with its store)" } else { " (no running dev; inspecting against wall time)" })
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    pub(super) fn engine() -> Engine {
        let bound = snapback4_lang::compile(&[("schema.q".into(), "table alpha:\n  at: time\n  public\ntable beta:\n  at: time\n  public\n".into())]);
        assert!(bound.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:?}", bound.diagnostics);
        Engine::new_at(snapback4_core::store_port::AnyStore::memory().unwrap(), bound.schema,
            bound.programs.into_iter().map(|p| p.program), 100_000).unwrap()
    }
    fn seed(engine: &mut Engine, table: &str, commit_at: i64, row_at: i64) {
        engine.seed_at([(table.into(), snapback4_core::value::object([
            ("id", snapback4_core::Value::Id(snapback4_core::ids::mint(row_at, &mut snapback4_core::ids::Mint::default()).unwrap())),
            ("at", snapback4_core::Value::Int(row_at)),
        ]))], commit_at).unwrap();
    }
    #[test]
    fn clock_report_bounds_one_large_job_error_repeated_across_many_schedules() {
        let mut source = "table rows:\n  n: int\n  public 'fixture'\njob tick():\n  for row in rows:\n    update rows[row.id] {n:1}\n".to_owned();
        for n in 0..500 { source.push_str(&format!("schedule s{n} every 1m: tick\n")); }
        let bound = snapback4_lang::compile(&[("schema.q".into(), source)]);
        assert!(bound.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:?}", bound.diagnostics);
        let mut engine = Engine::new_at(snapback4_core::store_port::AnyStore::memory().unwrap(), bound.schema, bound.programs.into_iter().map(|p|p.program), 0).unwrap();
        engine.fire_schedules(0).unwrap();
        let before = saved_boundaries(&engine);
        assert_eq!(crossed_boundaries(&engine, &before, 60_000, false, true).unwrap().len(), 500);
        let fired = engine.fire_schedules(60_000).unwrap();
        let message = "🧪\n\\".repeat(30_000);
        (engine.store()).test_support_conn().execute("UPDATE _jobs SET error = ?1 WHERE id = (SELECT id FROM _jobs ORDER BY created_at DESC, id DESC LIMIT 1)", [&message]).unwrap();
        let report = schedule_report(&engine, &before, &fired, 60_000, 60_000, false, false, None);
        assert_eq!(report.len(), 500);
        assert!(report.iter().all(|s| s["run"]["errorTruncated"] == true));
        assert!(report.iter().all(|s| s["run"]["error"].as_str().unwrap().len() <= REPORT_ERROR_BYTES + 3));
        assert!(serde_json::to_vec(&report).unwrap().len() < 2 << 20);
    }
    #[test]
    fn clock_report_refuses_before_the_host_encoder_limit() {
        let mut source = "table rows:\n  n: int\n  public 'fixture'\njob tick():\n  for row in rows:\n    update rows[row.id] {n:1}\n".to_owned();
        for n in 0..3000 { source.push_str(&format!("schedule s{n} every 1m: tick\n")); }
        let bound = snapback4_lang::compile(&[("schema.q".into(), source)]);
        assert!(bound.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:?}", bound.diagnostics);
        let mut engine = Engine::new_at(snapback4_core::store_port::AnyStore::memory().unwrap(), bound.schema, bound.programs.into_iter().map(|p|p.program), 0).unwrap();
        engine.fire_schedules(0).unwrap();
        let before = saved_boundaries(&engine);
        let refusal = crossed_boundaries(&engine, &before, 60_000, false, true).unwrap_err();
        assert_eq!(refusal.code, "E_CLOCK_INPUT");
        assert!(refusal.message.contains("byte budget"));
        assert_eq!(before, saved_boundaries(&engine));
        assert!(REPORT_BYTE_BUDGET < crate::serve::JSON_RESPONSE_CEILING);
    }
    #[test]
    fn startup_hold_instruction_catches_up_without_repeating_the_saved_window() {
        let source = "table rows:\n  n: int\n  public 'fixture'\njob tick():\n  for row in rows:\n    update rows[row.id] {n:1}\nschedule hourly every 1h: tick\n";
        let bound = snapback4_lang::compile(&[("schema.q".into(), source.into())]);
        assert!(bound.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:?}", bound.diagnostics);
        let mut engine = Engine::new_at(snapback4_core::store_port::AnyStore::memory().unwrap(), bound.schema,
            bound.programs.into_iter().map(|p|p.program), 0).unwrap();
        engine.fire_schedules(0).unwrap();
        let saved = 5 * 3_600_000;
        engine.fire_schedules(saved).unwrap();
        let raw = 1_234;
        let lines = held_schedules(&engine, raw);
        assert_eq!(lines.len(), 1);
        let line = &lines[0];
        assert!(line.contains(&format!("held until {}", utc(saved))), "{line}");
        assert!(line.contains(&format!("next unprocessed window {}", utc(saved + 3_600_000))), "{line}");
        let duration = line.split_once("`clock advance ").unwrap().1.split_once('`').unwrap().0;
        let delta = crate::dev_clock::duration(duration).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(delta, saved - raw);
        let before = saved_boundaries(&engine);
        refuse_held(&engine, &before, raw, raw + delta).unwrap();
        assert!(engine.fire_schedules(raw + delta).unwrap().is_empty());
        assert!(held_schedules(&engine, raw + delta).is_empty());
        let report = schedule_report(&engine, &before, &[], raw + delta, raw + delta, false, false, None);
        assert_eq!(report[0]["holdCode"], "not-due");
        assert_eq!(engine.fire_schedules(saved + 3_600_000).unwrap(), vec!["hourly"]);
    }

    #[test]
    fn utc_text_and_spans_match_the_calendar() {
        assert_eq!(utc(0), "1970-01-01T00:00:00Z");
        assert_eq!(utc(951_782_400_000), "2000-02-29T00:00:00Z");
        assert_eq!(utc(-1), "1969-12-31T23:59:59Z");
        assert_eq!(utc(1_791_468_000_000), "2026-10-08T14:00:00Z");
        assert_eq!(span(12_633_000), "3h30m33s");
        assert_eq!(span(86_400_001), "1d1s");
        assert_eq!(span(0), "0s");
    }
    #[test]
    fn resume_offset_follows_the_newest_commit_and_is_never_negative() {
        let mut engine = engine();
        assert_eq!(resume_offset(engine.store(), 100_000).unwrap(), 0);
        seed(&mut engine, "alpha", 3_700_500, 1);
        assert_eq!(resume_offset(engine.store(), 100_000).unwrap(), 3_601_000);
        assert_eq!(resume_offset(engine.store(), 9_000_000).unwrap(), 0);
    }
    #[test]
    fn commit_tolerance_and_largest_lead_ignore_future_application_values() {
        let mut engine = engine();
        seed(&mut engine, "alpha", 100_000, 1_000_000);
        assert!(ahead(&engine, 100_000).unwrap().is_none());
        seed(&mut engine, "alpha", 105_000, 2_000_000);
        seed(&mut engine, "beta", 107_000, 3_000_000);
        let note = ahead(&engine, 100_000).unwrap().unwrap();
        assert!(note.contains("up to 7000ms (latest 64 commits; tables: beta)"), "{note}");
        let note = ahead(&engine, 99_999).unwrap().unwrap();
        assert!(note.contains("up to 7001ms (latest 64 commits; tables: alpha, beta)"), "{note}");
        assert!(ahead(&engine, 102_000).unwrap().is_none());
    }
    #[test]
    fn recent_window_does_not_claim_older_commits_or_read_row_payloads() {
        let mut engine = engine();
        seed(&mut engine, "alpha", 200_000, 200_000);
        for n in 0..RECENT_COMMITS { seed(&mut engine, "beta", 100_000, 300_000 + n as i64); }
        // Deliberately unreadable images: metadata inspection must not deserialize them.
        (engine.store()).test_support_conn().execute("UPDATE r SET data = 'not json'", []).unwrap();
        assert!(ahead(&engine, 100_000).unwrap().is_none());
        (engine.store()).test_support_conn().execute("UPDATE _commits SET at = 200000, touched = ?1 WHERE seq = (SELECT MAX(seq) FROM _commits)", ["x".repeat(TABLE_BYTES + 1)]).unwrap();
        let note = ahead(&engine, 100_000).unwrap().unwrap();
        assert!(note.contains("100000ms") && note.contains("additional tables omitted"), "{note}");
    }
}

// @ref LLP 3000.000.000.001#12-wave-24-loop-repairs-dx7 — staging owns time; legacy stores recover from retained evidence.
pub fn staging_clock(store: &snapback4_core::store_port::AnyStore, requested: i64, wall: i64) -> Result<snapback4_core::metadata::StagingClock, RuntimeError> {
    use snapback4_core::metadata::{DevMetadata, StagingClock};
    let saved = store.dev_value(DevMetadata::StagingClock)?
        .map(|text| serde_json::from_str::<StagingClock>(&text)
            .map_err(|_| RuntimeError::Input("invalid persisted staging clock; refusing to reset staging time".into())))
        .transpose()?;
    if let Some(saved) = &saved { saved.validate()?; }
    let floor = store.time_audience_clock_value()?.map(|text| text.parse::<i64>()
        .map_err(|_| RuntimeError::Input("invalid staging disclosure clock".into()))).transpose()?.unwrap_or(0);
    let legacy_floor = store.recent_commit_max_at(RECENT_COMMITS as i64)?.into_iter()
        .chain(store.latest_schedule_boundary()?).max().unwrap_or(wall);
    let recovered = if let Some(saved) = &saved { wall.checked_add(saved.offset).unwrap_or(i64::MAX).max(saved.now) }
        else { wall.saturating_add(resume_offset(store, wall)?) };
    let now = wall.checked_add(requested).unwrap_or(i64::MAX).max(recovered).max(floor).max(legacy_floor);
    let state = StagingClock { now, offset: now.saturating_sub(wall), paused: saved.is_some_and(|s| s.paused) };
    state.validate()?;
    Ok(state)
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod staging_tests {
    use super::*;
    use snapback4_core::metadata::{DevMetadata, StagingClock};
    #[test]
    fn staging_checkpoint_recovers_precise_time_pause_and_later_legacy_boundaries() {
        let bound = snapback4_lang::compile(&[("schema.q".into(), "table rows:\n  n: int\n  public 'fixture'\njob tick():\n  for row in rows:\n    update rows[row.id] {n:1}\nschedule hourly every 1h: tick\n".into())]);
        let mut engine = Engine::new_at(snapback4_core::store_port::AnyStore::memory().unwrap(), bound.schema, bound.programs.into_iter().map(|p|p.program),0).unwrap();
        engine.store().set_deployment_environment("staging").unwrap();
        let saved = StagingClock {offset:1000,now:2000,paused:true};
        engine.checkpoint_staging_clock_at(0,1000,false,&saved).unwrap();
        let resumed=staging_clock(engine.store(),0,1100).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(resumed.offset,1000);assert_eq!(resumed.now,2100);assert!(resumed.paused);
        let rollback=staging_clock(engine.store(),0,500).unwrap();assert_eq!(rollback.now,2000);
        engine.fire_schedules(0).unwrap();engine.fire_schedules(7_200_000).unwrap();
        let recovered=staging_clock(engine.store(),0,1100).unwrap();assert!(recovered.now>=7_200_000);assert!(recovered.paused);
        engine.store().set_dev_value(DevMetadata::StagingClock,"malformed").unwrap();
        assert!(staging_clock(engine.store(),0,1100).is_err());
    }
    #[test]
    fn failed_checkpoint_keeps_the_previous_time_and_pause_record() {
        let mut engine=super::tests::engine();
        engine.store().set_deployment_environment("staging").unwrap();
        let guest = snapback4_core::identity::guest(engine.store(), 100_000, &mut snapback4_core::ids::Mint::default()).unwrap();
        let expiry = guest.expires_at;
        let identity = engine.store().store_identity().unwrap().unwrap();
        let old=StagingClock {offset:10,now:100_010,paused:true};
        engine.checkpoint_staging_clock_at(0,100_000,false,&old).unwrap();
        engine.store().test_staging_clock_write_failure(true).unwrap();
        for paused in [true,false] {
            let next=StagingClock {offset:1000,now:101_000,paused};
            assert!(engine.checkpoint_staging_clock_at(if paused {1000}else{0},100_000,true,&next).is_err());
            assert_eq!(engine.staging_session_timing(&guest.token, 100_010, &identity).unwrap().unwrap().expires_at, expiry, "checkpoint failure rolls back credential preservation");
            assert_eq!(staging_clock(engine.store(),0,100_000).unwrap().now,old.now);
            assert!(staging_clock(engine.store(),0,100_000).unwrap().paused);
        }
        engine.store().test_staging_clock_write_failure(false).unwrap();
        let next = StagingClock {offset:1010,now:101_010,paused:true};
        engine.checkpoint_staging_clock_at(1000,100_010,true,&next).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(engine.staging_session_timing(&guest.token, 101_010, &identity).unwrap().unwrap().expires_at, expiry + 1000);
        engine.checkpoint_staging_clock_at(0,101_010,false,&next).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(engine.staging_session_timing(&guest.token, 101_010, &identity).unwrap().unwrap().expires_at, expiry + 1000, "startup does not shift a preserved credential twice");
    }
}
