// @ref LLP 3000.011.003.000.000#1 — direct children, file logs and bounded cleanup own proof orchestration.
import { spawn, type ChildProcess } from "node:child_process";
import { closeSync, mkdirSync, openSync, readFileSync, writeFileSync } from "node:fs";
import { join, resolve } from "node:path";
import { fileURLToPath } from "node:url";
import { randomUUID } from "node:crypto";
import { setTimeout as sleep } from "node:timers/promises";
import { stripVTControlCharacters } from "node:util";

const children = new Set<ChildProcess>();
const privateGroups = new WeakMap<ChildProcess, number>();
process.once("exit", () => { for (const child of children) child.kill("SIGKILL"); });
export function register(child: ChildProcess): void {
  children.add(child);
  child.once("exit", () => children.delete(child));
  child.on("error", () => children.delete(child));
}
export interface ProofOptions {
  cwd?: string;
  env?: NodeJS.ProcessEnv;
  args?: readonly string[];
  timeoutMs?: number;
  stopTimeoutMs?: number;
  logsDir?: string;
  /** Keep stdout in memory only (stderr remains diagnostic evidence). */
  secret?: boolean;
  /** Internal: a frontend keeper owns this private process group. */
  detached?: boolean;
  /** Internal: the frontend observes parent death through stdin EOF. */
  livenessPipe?: boolean;
  /** Internal: a keeper-backed group owns npm and other descendant trees. */
  processGroup?: boolean;
}
export interface ProofHost {
  url: string;
  pid: number;
  logs: { stdout: string; stderr: string };
  stop(): Promise<void>;
}
export function deadline(ms: number | undefined, fallback: number): number {
  const value = ms ?? fallback;
  if (!Number.isFinite(value) || value < 1) throw new RangeError("proof timeout must be positive and finite");
  return value;
}
/** @throws deadline expires; work rejection propagates. */
export async function bounded<T>(work: Promise<T>, ms: number, label: string): Promise<T> {
  let timer: ReturnType<typeof setTimeout> | undefined;
  try {
    return await Promise.race([work, new Promise<never>((_, reject) => {
      timer = setTimeout(() => reject(Error(`${label} timed out after ${ms} ms`)), ms);
    })]);
  } finally { clearTimeout(timer); }
}
/** Attempts every cleanup, even if a preceding step fails or stalls. */
/** @throws AggregateError after every cleanup was attempted; each step has a 10-second bound. */
export async function cleanup(...steps: Array<(() => unknown | Promise<unknown>) | undefined>): Promise<void> {
  const errors: unknown[] = [];
  for (const step of steps) if (step) {
    try { await bounded(Promise.resolve().then(step), 10_000, "proof cleanup"); }
    catch (error) { errors.push(error); }
  }
  if (errors.length) throw new AggregateError(errors, "proof cleanup failed");
}
/** @throws invalid handle/deadline, host stop failure or TERM/KILL reap deadline. */
export async function stopChild(child: ChildProcess | Pick<ProofHost, "url" | "stop">, graceMs = 5000): Promise<void> {
  deadline(graceMs, 5000);
  if (child && "url" in child && typeof child.url === "string" && typeof child.stop === "function") {
    await bounded(Promise.resolve().then(() => child.stop()), graceMs + 10_000, "host stop"); return;
  }
  if (!child || !("kill" in child) || typeof child.kill !== "function" || typeof child.once !== "function"
      || !(child.exitCode === null || typeof child.exitCode === "number")
      || !(child.signalCode === null || typeof child.signalCode === "string")) {
    throw new TypeError("stopChild needs a ChildProcess or { url, stop }");
  }
  if (child.exitCode !== null || child.signalCode !== null) { await groupGone(child); return; }
  let done!: () => void;
  const exited = new Promise<void>(resolve => { done = resolve; });
  child.once("exit", done);
  child.once("error", done);
  try {
    child.kill("SIGTERM");
    try { await bounded(exited, graceMs, "SIGTERM"); }
    catch {
      child.kill("SIGKILL");
      await bounded(exited, 5000, "SIGKILL reap");
    }
  } finally { child.removeListener("exit", done); child.removeListener("error", done); }
  await groupGone(child);
}
async function groupGone(child: ChildProcess): Promise<void> {
  const pid = privateGroups.get(child);
  if (!pid || process.platform === "win32") return;
  await bounded((async () => {
    for (;;) {
      try { process.kill(-pid, 0); }
      catch (error) { if ((error as NodeJS.ErrnoException).code === "ESRCH") return; throw error; }
      await sleep(10);
    }
  })(), 5000, "proof process-group cleanup");
}
/** @throws partial, invalid or reversed PORT_LO/PORT_HI pair. */
export function portRange(env: NodeJS.ProcessEnv = process.env): string {
  const low = env.PORT_LO, high = env.PORT_HI;
  if (low === undefined && high === undefined) return "0";
  if (!low || !high || !/^\d+$/.test(low) || !/^\d+$/.test(high)
      || Number(low) < 1 || Number(high) > 65535 || Number(low) > Number(high)) {
    throw Error("PORT_LO and PORT_HI must both specify an inclusive range, 1 <= low <= high <= 65535");
  }
  return `${Number(low)}-${Number(high)}`;
}
/** App-local evidence survives harness cleanup of its temporary root. */
/** @throws None; returns the resolved explicit or cwd/logs path. */
export function logsDir(options: Pick<ProofOptions, "cwd" | "logsDir"> = {}): string {
  return resolve(options.cwd ?? ".", options.logsDir ?? "logs");
}
export function logged(command: string, args: readonly string[], options: ProofOptions = {}) {
  const cwd = resolve(options.cwd ?? "."), id = randomUUID();
  const directory = logsDir(options); mkdirSync(directory, { recursive: true, mode: 0o700 });
  const logs = { stdout: join(directory, `${id}.stdout.log`), stderr: join(directory, `${id}.stderr.log`) };
  if (options.secret) writeFileSync(logs.stdout, "stdout withheld: secret command (returned only to caller)\n", { flag: "wx", mode: 0o600 });
  const out = options.secret ? undefined : openSync(logs.stdout, "wx", 0o600), err = openSync(logs.stderr, "wx", 0o600);
  const grouped = options.processGroup === true && process.platform !== "win32";
  const executable = grouped ? process.execPath : command;
  const executableArgs = grouped ? [resolve(fileURLToPath(new URL("./proof-command.js", import.meta.url))), command, ...args] : [...args];
  let child: ChildProcess;
  try { child = spawn(executable, executableArgs, { cwd, detached: grouped || (options.detached ?? false), env: { ...(options.env ?? process.env), SNAPBACK4_PROOF_LAUNCH: id }, stdio: [grouped || options.livenessPipe ? "pipe" : "ignore", options.secret ? "pipe" : out!, err, ...(grouped ? ["ipc" as const] : [])] }); }
  finally { if (out !== undefined) closeSync(out); closeSync(err); }
  if (grouped && child.pid) privateGroups.set(child, child.pid);
  // Secret stdout never reaches a file, even briefly. Callers still receive it.
  let stdout = "";
  child.stdout?.setEncoding("utf8");
  child.stdout?.on("data", chunk => { stdout += chunk; });
  register(child);
  child.stdin?.on("error", () => {});
  let error: Error | undefined;
  let actualPid = child.pid;
  let actualCode: number | null = null;
  let actualSignal: NodeJS.Signals | null = null;
  let startedResolve!: () => void;
  const started = new Promise<void>(resolve => { startedResolve = resolve; });
  if (!grouped) startedResolve();
  child.on("message", message => {
    const event = message as {type?:string;pid?:number;code?:number|null;signal?:NodeJS.Signals|null;error?:string};
    if (event.type === "started" && typeof event.pid === "number") { actualPid = event.pid; startedResolve(); }
    if (event.type === "result") { actualCode = event.code ?? null; actualSignal = event.signal ?? null; if (event.error) error = Error(event.error); }
  });
  child.on("error", value => { error = value; });
  const closed = new Promise<void>((resolve, reject) => child.once("close", () => groupGone(child).then(resolve,reject)));
  return { child, logs, id, closed, started, pid: () => actualPid, code: () => grouped ? actualCode : child.exitCode, signal: () => grouped ? actualSignal : child.signalCode, stdout: () => options.secret ? stdout : logText(logs.stdout), error: () => error };
}
export const logText = (path: string): string => readFileSync(path, "utf8");
export async function ready(run: ReturnType<typeof logged>, parse: (line: string) => string | undefined, timeoutMs: number): Promise<string> {
  const end = performance.now() + timeoutMs;
  let outAt = 0, errAt = 0;
  while (performance.now() < end) {
    if (run.error()) throw run.error();
    if (run.child.exitCode !== null || run.child.signalCode !== null) {
      throw Error(`proof child exited before ready (${run.child.exitCode ?? run.child.signalCode}); ${run.logs.stderr}\n${logText(run.logs.stderr).slice(-4000)}`);
    }
    for (const [path, offset, stream] of [[run.logs.stdout, outAt, "out"], [run.logs.stderr, errAt, "err"]] as const) {
      const text = logText(path);
      const complete = text.lastIndexOf("\n") + 1;
      for (const line of text.slice(offset, complete).split("\n")) {
        const url = parse(stripVTControlCharacters(line).trim());
        if (url) return url;
      }
      if (stream === "out") outAt = complete; else errAt = complete;
    }
    await sleep(25);
  }
  throw Error(`proof child ready timed out after ${timeoutMs} ms; logs: ${run.logs.stdout}, ${run.logs.stderr}`);
}

// A private process-group leader for proof commands. Its keeper observes
// parent EOF and kills the complete group, including npm-created descendants.
// @ref LLP 3000.000.000.002#10-wave-25-day-two-friction-dx8 — proof cleanup owns wrapper-created descendants and caller death.
import { spawn } from "node:child_process";
import { fileURLToPath } from "node:url";

const [command, ...args] = process.argv.slice(2);
if (!command || process.platform === "win32") throw Error("invalid proof command wrapper invocation");
process.stdin.resume();
let guard: ReturnType<typeof spawn> | undefined;
const abandon = () => guard?.stdin?.destroy();
process.stdin.once("end", abandon);
process.stdin.once("error", abandon);

guard = spawn(process.execPath, [fileURLToPath(new URL("./proof-guard.js", import.meta.url))], { stdio: ["pipe", "inherit", "inherit", "ipc"] });
await new Promise<void>((resolve, reject) => {
  guard!.once("message", message => message === "ready" ? resolve() : reject(Error("invalid proof command keeper")));
  guard!.once("error", reject);
  guard!.once("exit", () => reject(Error("proof command keeper exited early")));
});
guard.once("exit", () => process.exit(1));
process.once("exit", abandon);

const child = spawn(command, args, { cwd: process.cwd(), env: process.env, stdio: ["ignore", "inherit", "inherit"] });
process.send?.({ type: "started", pid: child.pid });
for (const signal of ["SIGTERM", "SIGINT"] as const) process.once(signal, () => child.kill(signal));
child.once("error", error => {
  process.send?.({ type: "result", code: null, signal: null, error: error.message }, () => process.exit(1));
});
child.once("close", (code, signal) => {
  process.send?.({ type: "result", code, signal }, () => process.exit(code ?? (signal ? 1 : 0)));
});

//! Capture the selected deployed bytes, generated client and contemporary fixtures.
// @ref LLP 3000.011.003.000.000#14-wave-15-saved-deployments-and-case-evidence-day5 — source and fixture evidence is immutable; identical-source refresh may repair derived artifacts.
use super::{Options, Project};
use std::{collections::BTreeMap, path::PathBuf};

pub(super) struct SavedVersion { path: PathBuf, files: Vec<(PathBuf, Vec<u8>)>, complete: bool, refresh: bool }
impl Drop for SavedVersion {
    fn drop(&mut self) { if !self.complete && !self.refresh { let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(&self.path); } }
}
impl SavedVersion {
    pub(super) fn prepare(project: &Project, sources: &[(String,String)], bound: &snapback4_lang::Bound, options: &Options, deployment: &std::path::Path) -> Result<Option<Self>,String> {
        let Some(name) = options.flags.get("save-version") else {return Ok(None);};
        if sources.is_empty() || sources.iter().all(|(_,text)|text.trim().is_empty()) {return Err("not saved yet: a saved version needs nonempty Quarry source".into());}
        if options.flags.contains_key("check") {return Err("--save-version captures a successful deployment; omit --check to save, or omit --save-version for a read-only preview".into());}
        if project.root.canonicalize().map_err(|e|e.to_string())?.starts_with(deployment.canonicalize().map_err(|e|e.to_string())?) {
            return Err("cannot save a version inside deployment custody; run deploy from the application's source directory".into());
        }
        let suffix = name.strip_prefix("versions/v").ok_or("--save-version needs a new project-relative versions/vN directory")?;
        if suffix.is_empty() || suffix.starts_with('0') || !suffix.bytes().all(|b|b.is_ascii_digit()) {return Err("--save-version needs versions/vN with a positive numeric N".into());}
        let parent=project.root.join("versions");
        if std::fs::symlink_metadata(&parent).is_ok_and(|m|m.file_type().is_symlink()) {return Err("--save-version refuses a symlinked versions directory".into());}
        std::fs::create_dir_all(&parent).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?;
        let path=project.root.join(name);
        let refresh = match std::fs::symlink_metadata(&path) {
            Ok(meta) if meta.file_type().is_symlink() || !meta.is_dir() => return Err(format!("cannot refresh {name}: saved version must be a real directory")),
            Ok(_) => {
                let selected=sources.iter().cloned().collect::<BTreeMap<_,_>>();
                let existing=crate::client_compatibility::sources(&path).map_err(|e|format!("cannot refresh {name}: {e}"))?.into_iter().collect::<BTreeMap<_,_>>();
                if existing != selected {return Err(format!("cannot refresh {name}: selected source differs from the saved source; choose a new versions/vN"));}
                true
            }
            Err(error) if error.kind()==std::io::ErrorKind::NotFound => {std::fs::create_dir(&path).map_err(|e|format!("cannot save {name}: {e}"))?;false}
            Err(error) => return Err(format!("cannot inspect {name}: {error}")),
        };
        let mut files = if refresh {Vec::new()} else {sources.iter().map(|(name,text)|(PathBuf::from(name),text.as_bytes().to_vec())).collect()};
        files.push((PathBuf::from("generated/api.ts"),snapback4_lang::emit::typescript(bound).into_bytes()));
        files.push((PathBuf::from("generated/package.json"),crate::project::GENERATED_PACKAGE.as_bytes().to_vec()));
        let mut saved=Self {path,files,complete:false,refresh};
        let fixture = match options.flags.get("schema").map(PathBuf::from) {
            None=>project.root.join("fixtures"),
            Some(source) if source.is_dir()=>source.join("fixtures"),
            Some(source) if source.file_name().is_some_and(|n|n=="schema.q")=>source.parent().unwrap().join("fixtures"),
            Some(source)=>source.with_file_name(format!("{}-fixtures",source.file_stem().unwrap_or_default().to_string_lossy())),
        };
        if fixture.is_dir() && !refresh {
            let mut entries=std::fs::read_dir(&fixture).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?.collect::<Result<Vec<_>,_>>().map_err(|e|e.to_string())?;
            entries.sort_by_key(|e|e.file_name());
            for entry in entries {
                if entry.path().extension().is_some_and(|e|e=="jsonl") || entry.file_name()=="test.sweep.json" {
                    if !entry.file_type().map_err(|e|e.to_string())?.is_file() {return Err("saved fixtures must be regular files".into());}
                    saved.files.push((PathBuf::from("fixtures").join(entry.file_name()),std::fs::read(entry.path()).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?));
                }
            }
        }
        if !refresh {saved.files.push((PathBuf::from("fixtures/.gitkeep"),Vec::new()));}
        Ok(Some(saved))
    }
    pub(super) fn finish(&mut self, release: u64, source_hash: &str) -> Result<(),String> {
        self.files.push((PathBuf::from("release.json"),format!("{}\n",serde_json::json!({"release":release,"sourceHash":source_hash,"cliVersion":env!("CARGO_PKG_VERSION")})).into_bytes()));
        for (name,bytes) in &self.files {
            let path=self.path.join(name);
            std::fs::create_dir_all(path.parent().unwrap()).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?;
            if self.refresh {
                let temporary=path.with_extension(format!("refresh-{}",std::process::id()));
                std::fs::write(&temporary,bytes).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?;
                std::fs::rename(&temporary,&path).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?;
            } else {
                use std::io::Write;
                let mut file=std::fs::OpenOptions::new().write(true).create_new(true).open(path).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?;
                file.write_all(bytes).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?;
            }
        }
        self.complete=true;
        eprintln!("{} release {release} sources, generated client, contemporary fixtures and release.json at {}",if self.refresh {"refreshed"} else {"saved"},self.path.display());
        Ok(())
    }
}

fn stage_downfills(tx:&Tx<'_>,next:&Schema,previous:&Schema,sources:&BTreeMap<String,Table>,now:i64,report:&mut super::adoption_report::AdoptionReport)->Result<bool,RuntimeError>{
    let next_keys:BTreeSet<String>=next.downfills.iter().map(Backfill::down_key).collect();
    let leaving:Vec<_>=previous.downfills.iter().filter(|f|!next_keys.contains(&f.down_key())&&next.table(&f.table).is_some_and(|t|f.outputs.iter().any(|c|t.columns.contains_key(c)))).collect();
    if leaving.is_empty(){return Ok(false)}
    let mut source=previous.clone();source.backfills.clear();source.downfills.clear();
    let mut rebuilt_reads=BTreeSet::new();
    tx.conn().execute_batch("CREATE TEMP TABLE _downfill_pending(t INTEGER NOT NULL,id TEXT NOT NULL,definition TEXT NOT NULL,patch TEXT NOT NULL,evidence TEXT NOT NULL,PRIMARY KEY(t,id,definition)) WITHOUT ROWID;")?;
    for fill in leaving {
        fill.admit_down(previous).map_err(refusal)?;
        let Some(target)=next.table(&fill.table) else {continue};
        let target_outputs:BTreeSet<_>=fill.outputs.iter().filter(|c|target.columns.contains_key(*c)).cloned().collect();
        if target_outputs.is_empty(){continue}
        if fill.name.starts_with("$rename:down:") {
            let source_kind=fill.inputs.values().next();let target_kind=target_outputs.iter().next().and_then(|c|target.columns.get(c));
            if source_kind!=target_kind {return Err(refusal(format!("column rename on {} must use identical old and new cell types",fill.table)))}
        }
        for c in &target_outputs {
            let kind=&target.columns[c];
            if protected(kind) || target.engine_field(c) || target.sequences.contains_key(c) || target.maintained.contains_key(c) || target.expire.as_ref()==Some(c) || target.not_before.as_ref()==Some(c) {
                return Err(refusal(format!("down {} {} output {c} must be an authoritative scalar without reference or lifetime ownership",fill.table,fill.name)))
            }
        }
        let t=tx.num(&fill.table)?;let key=fill.down_key();let mut bad=super::adopt_refusals::Samples::default();let mut count=0;let mut preimages=0u64;let mut preimage_ids=Vec::new();
        walk_images(tx,t,|id,row,_in_r|{
            let prior=evidence(tx,t,id,&key)?;
            let eligible=!absence(tx,t,id,&format!("$native:{key}"))?.is_empty();
            if prior.is_none() && !eligible{return Ok(())}
            if let Some(prior)=&prior {if !prior.active {
                let fields=row.as_object()?;
                if prior.after.iter().any(|(c,v)|target_outputs.contains(c)&&fields.get(c)!=Some(v)) {
                    bad.add(id,"projected cells changed before their native receipt was restored; repair under a bridge");
                }
                return Ok(())
            }}
            let fields=row.as_object()?;
            // Only a forward receipt that actually owns this output supplies
            // a predecessor preimage. Unrelated transformation history on the
            // same lifetime must not suppress a disjoint reverse projection.
            let mut forward_outputs=BTreeSet::new();
            let mut q=tx.conn().prepare("SELECT octet_length(d.definition),d.definition FROM _backfill_evidence e JOIN _backfill_definitions d ON d.digest=e.definition WHERE e.t=?1 AND e.id=?2 ORDER BY e.definition")?;
            let mut receipts=q.query(params![t,id])?;
            while let Some(r)=receipts.next()? {
                let mut admission=Meter::new(Ceilings::job(),Cut::Abort);admission.heap((r.get::<_,i64>(0)?.max(0) as u64).saturating_mul(64).saturating_add(8192))?;
                let definition:Backfill=decode(&r.get::<_,String>(1)?)?;
                if let Some(receipt)=evidence(tx,t,id,&definition.key())? {
                    // A declared down owns the view of an edited in-place output.
                    // Unedited outputs still restore their exact predecessor.
                    forward_outputs.extend(definition.outputs.into_iter().filter(|c|
                        !receipt.authored.contains(c) && (fields.get(c)==receipt.after.get(c) || fields.get(c)==receipt.before.get(c))));
                }
            }
            drop(receipts);drop(q);
            // An entering inactive receipt restores exact native cells. Those
            // preimages outrank a different leaving declaration's substitution.
            for entering in next.downfills.iter().filter(|f|f.table==fill.table) {
                if let Some(e)=evidence(tx,t,id,&entering.down_key())? {if !e.active {forward_outputs.extend(e.reversed.into_keys());}}
            }
            let mut pruned_outputs=BTreeSet::new();
            let mut q=tx.conn().prepare("SELECT octet_length(d.definition),d.definition FROM _backfill_pruned p JOIN _backfill_definitions d ON d.digest=p.definition WHERE p.t=?1 AND p.id=?2 ORDER BY p.definition")?;
            let mut receipts=q.query(params![t,id])?;
            while let Some(r)=receipts.next()? {
                let mut admission=Meter::new(Ceilings::job(),Cut::Abort);admission.heap((r.get::<_,i64>(0)?.max(0) as u64).saturating_mul(64).saturating_add(8192))?;
                pruned_outputs.extend(decode::<Backfill>(&r.get::<_,String>(1)?)?.outputs);
            }
            drop(receipts);drop(q);
            if target_outputs.iter().any(|c|pruned_outputs.contains(c)) {bad.add(id,"forward predecessor preimage was explicitly pruned");return Ok(())}
            let mut projected_outputs=BTreeSet::new();
            for c in &target_outputs {
                if forward_outputs.contains(c){continue}
                // An active receipt means this cell was supplied by this down
                // declaration on the preceding rollback.  Its archived copy
                // is therefore a prior down result, not a predecessor
                // preimage, and the next cycle must issue a fresh receipt so
                // edits under the older release remain conflict checked.
                if prior.as_ref().is_some_and(|e|e.active && e.reversed.contains_key(c)) {
                    projected_outputs.insert(c.clone());
                    continue;
                }
                // A non-null retained predecessor value is an exact preimage.
                // Legacy rollback loops could manufacture optional null cells;
                // the explicit down declaration is the author's substitution
                // policy for that otherwise ambiguous evidence-free case.

                if !retained || fill.name.starts_with("$rename:down:") {projected_outputs.insert(c.clone());}
            }
            if projected_outputs.len()<target_outputs.len(){preimages+=1;if preimage_ids.len()<5{preimage_ids.push(id.to_owned());}}
            if projected_outputs.is_empty(){return Ok(())}
            if prior.is_none() && tx.conn().query_row("SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM _backfill_pruned WHERE t=?1 AND id=?2 AND definition=?3)",params![t,id,key],|r|r.get::<_,bool>(0))? {
                bad.add(id,"reverse projection evidence was explicitly pruned");return Ok(())
            }
            let native_source=row_source(tx,t,id,sources.get(&fill.table))?;
            if let Some(source)=native_source.as_deref() {if let Some(c)=projected_outputs.iter().find(|c|source.columns.get(*c).is_some_and(protected)||source_owned(source,c)) {
                bad.add(id,&format!("native preimage {}.{c} requires reference, asset or lifetime custody; keep a typed bridge",fill.table));return Ok(())
            }}
            let mut args=BTreeMap::new();
            for (c,kind) in &fill.inputs {
                let value=source_value(tx,t,id,&row,c,native_source.as_deref().or(Some(&previous.tables[&fill.table])),&mut Meter::new(Ceilings::job(),Cut::Abort))?;
                if crate::engine::validate::validate_column(&fill.table,c,kind,value.as_ref()).is_err(){bad.add(id,&format!("current reverse input {}.{c} is absent or invalid",fill.table));return Ok(())}
                args.insert(c.clone(),value.unwrap_or(Value::Null));
            }
            let inputs=Value::Row(args.clone().into());let mut context=migration_context(tx,now)?;context.args=args;
            for (c,kind) in &fill.inputs {
                let kind=native_source.as_deref().and_then(|s|s.columns.get(c)).filter(|k|protected(k)).unwrap_or(kind);
                let value=inputs.field(c)?;let unchanged=prior.as_ref().is_none_or(|e|e.inputs.field(c).ok()==Some(value));
                capture_input_refs(tx,t,id,c,&input_ref(&key,c),kind,value,unchanged)?;
                if input_lifetime_lost(tx,t,id,&key,c)? {bad.add(id,&format!("reverse input {c} lost its parent lifetime; use a typed bridge"));return Ok(())}
            }
            let mut reads=BTreeSet::new();collect_reads(&fill.program.body,&mut reads);
            let needed=reads.difference(&rebuilt_reads).cloned().collect::<BTreeSet<_>>();
            if !needed.is_empty(){rebuild_reads(tx,&source,&needed)?;tx.clear_activation_scan();tx.set_meta("activation_population","true")?;rebuilt_reads.extend(needed);}
            let mut runner=Runner::new(tx,&source,context,Meter::new(Ceilings::job(),Cut::Abort),0);
            let patch=match runner.run(&fill.program){Ok(Value::Row(f)|Value::Map(f))=>f.into_iter().collect::<BTreeMap<_,_>>(),Ok(_)=>{bad.add(id,"down projection did not return an object");return Ok(())},Err(e)=>{bad.add(id,&e.to_string());return Ok(())}};
            if runner.read_cut().is_some(){bad.add(id,"reverse lookup did not complete; reduce its bounded work");return Ok(())}
            if patch.keys().cloned().collect::<BTreeSet<_>>()!=fill.outputs{return Err(refusal("down projection returned undeclared output fields"))}
            let patch:BTreeMap<_,_>=patch.into_iter().filter(|(c,_)|projected_outputs.contains(c)).collect();
            let reversed=projected_outputs.iter().map(|c|{
                // Each rollback owns this cycle's current physical preimage,
                // including newer writes and absence since the last restoration.
                let value=fields.get(c).cloned();
                (c.clone(),value)
            }).collect();
            let e=Evidence{computed:BTreeMap::new(),authored:BTreeSet::new(),reversed,retained:BTreeSet::new(),absent:BTreeSet::new(),epoch:tx.meta("generation")?.and_then(|s|s.parse::<i64>().ok()).unwrap_or(0).saturating_add(1),active:false,inputs,before:BTreeMap::new(),after:patch.clone()};
            tx.conn().execute("INSERT INTO temp._downfill_pending VALUES(?1,?2,?3,?4,?5)",params![t,id,key,canonical_json(&patch),canonical_json(&e)])?;count+=1;Ok(())
        })?;
        if bad.count>0{return Err(refusal(format!("down {} {}: {}",fill.table,fill.name,bad.describe())))}

        if count>0{report.backfills.push(format!("{} {} projected {count} {} down",fill.table,fill.name,if count==1{"row"}else{"rows"}));}
    }
    Ok(true)
}

import { identityRequest } from "./identity-request.ts";
import { randomUUID, randomBytes } from "node:crypto";
import type { ServeHost } from "./proof.ts";

export interface StagingAccount {
  kind: "user"; role: string; email: string; password: string; principal: string; token: string; expiresAt: number;
}
export type StagingSessions = Record<string, StagingAccount[]>;
export type ProvisionedAccount = StagingAccount;
export type ProvisionedSessions = StagingSessions;
export interface SeedAccountsOptions {
  roles: Record<string, number>;
  provision: { readonly name: string };
  /** Service seat authorized by the provisioning mutation. Default operator. */
  service?: string;
  args?: (account: StagingAccount) => Record<string, unknown>;
  /** Whole setup deadline, including rate-limit waits. Default 5 minutes. */
  timeoutMs?: number;
}

// @ref LLP 3000.000.000.001#2-real-staging-accounts — real credentials and ordinary operator mutations, never system seeding or auth bypass.
/** @throws E_STAGING_INPUT for invalid plans; E_STAGING for production/dev hosts; E_STAGING_TIMEOUT, E_STAGING_SIGNUP or E_STAGING_PROVISION for incomplete setup. Partial accounts/grants may exist; inspect or discard the staging store before retrying. */
async function accounts(host: Pick<ServeHost, "url" | "operatorToken">, options: SeedAccountsOptions, environment: "staging" | "production"): Promise<StagingSessions> {
  // @ref LLP 3000.000.000.001#8-wave-18-developer-loop-amendment-dx4 — custom argument keys override defaults; the seat grants no extra authority.
  const service = options.service ?? "operator";
  const roles = Object.entries(options.roles), timeout = options.timeoutMs ?? 300_000;
  if (!/^[A-Za-z0-9_-]{1,64}$/.test(service) || !Number.isSafeInteger(timeout) || timeout < 1 || !roles.length || roles.some(([role, n]) => !/^[A-Za-z][A-Za-z0-9_-]{0,63}$/.test(role) || !Number.isSafeInteger(n) || n < 1)
      || roles.reduce((sum, [, n]) => sum + n, 0) > 100 || !/^[A-Za-z][A-Za-z0-9_]*(?:\.[A-Za-z][A-Za-z0-9_]*)*$/.test(options.provision?.name ?? "")) {
    throw Error("E_STAGING_INPUT: provide 1..100 accounts across named roles, a mutation handle and a positive timeoutMs");
  }
  const end = performance.now() + timeout;
  const remaining = () => {
    const ms = Math.ceil(end - performance.now());
    if (ms <= 0) throw Error("E_STAGING_TIMEOUT: setup incomplete; partial accounts/grants may exist; inspect or discard this staging deployment");
    return ms;
  };
  const request = (path: string, init?: RequestInit) => fetch(host.url + path, { ...init, signal: AbortSignal.timeout(Math.min(remaining(), 30_000)) });
  const config = await request("/auth/config");
  const identity = await config.json();
  if (!config.ok || identity.environment !== environment || identity.development !== false || !identity.password) {
    throw Error(environment === "staging"
      ? "E_STAGING: account seeding requires serve on a fresh deploy --staging with password identity; production and dev refuse before creating users"
      : "E_PRODUCTION_ACCOUNTS: account provisioning requires serve on a production deploy with password identity; staging and dev refuse before creating users");
  }
  if (environment === "production") {
    const response = await request("/schema"), schema = await response.json();
    const named = (value: unknown): boolean => value === `service:${service}` || (value !== null && typeof value === "object" && Object.values(value).some(named));
    if (!response.ok || !named(schema)) process.stderr.write(`snapback4: WARNING minted service:${service} seat matches no schema-authorized identity; provisionAccounts(${options.provision.name}) will likely refuse.\n`);
  }
  const operator = await host.operatorToken(service);
  const sessions: StagingSessions = Object.create(null);
  for (const [role, count] of roles) {
    sessions[role] = [];
    for (let i = 0; i < count; i++) {
      const email = `staging-${role}-${randomUUID()}@example.test`, password = randomBytes(24).toString("base64url");
      const {json: reply} = await identityRequest(host.url + "/auth/signup", {
        method:"POST", headers:{"content-type":"application/json"}, body:JSON.stringify({email,password}),
      }, fetch, {timeoutMs:remaining()}).catch(error => {
        if ((error as {code?: string})?.code === "E_IDENTITY_TIMEOUT") throw Error("E_STAGING_TIMEOUT: signup deadline expired; partial accounts/grants may exist; inspect or discard this staging deployment");
        remaining(); throw error;
      });
      if ((reply.denied as {code?:string} | undefined)?.code === "E_RATE_LIMIT") {
        throw Error("E_STAGING_TIMEOUT: identity retry budget exhausted; increase timeoutMs or inspect partial accounts/grants before retrying");
      }
      const session = reply.session as {principal?:string;kind?:string;token?:string;expiresAt?:number} | undefined;
      if (typeof session?.principal !== "string" || typeof session.token !== "string" || typeof session.expiresAt !== "number" || session.kind !== "user") {
        throw Error(`E_STAGING_SIGNUP: signup refused (${(reply.denied as {code?:string} | undefined)?.code ?? "invalid reply"}); partial accounts/grants may exist`);
      }
      const account: StagingAccount = { kind: session.kind, role, email, password, principal: session.principal, token: session.token, expiresAt: session.expiresAt };
      const args = { principal: account.principal, role, ...options.args?.(account) };
      const response = await request(`/m/${options.provision.name}`, { method: "POST", headers: { "content-type": "application/json", authorization: `Bearer ${operator}` }, body: JSON.stringify({ id: randomUUID(), args }) });
      const result = await response.json();
      if (!response.ok || result.state !== "sent") throw Error(`E_STAGING_PROVISION: ${options.provision.name} refused (${result.why?.code ?? result.denied?.code ?? "invalid reply"}); partial accounts/grants may exist`);
      sessions[role]!.push(account);
    }
  }
  return sessions;
}

// @ref LLP 3000.000.000.001#2-real-staging-accounts — real credentials and ordinary operator mutations, never system seeding or auth bypass.
/** @throws E_STAGING_INPUT for invalid plans; E_STAGING for production/dev hosts; E_STAGING_TIMEOUT, E_STAGING_SIGNUP or E_STAGING_PROVISION for incomplete setup. */
export function seedAccounts(host: Pick<ServeHost, "url" | "operatorToken">, options: SeedAccountsOptions): Promise<StagingSessions> {
  return accounts(host, options, "staging");
}

/** Create real production password accounts and provision each one through the
 * same ordinary service-authorized mutation contract as seedAccounts. */
// @ref LLP 3000.000.000.002#10-wave-25-day-two-friction-dx8 — production setup shares the plan without acquiring fixture authority.
export function provisionAccounts(host: Pick<ServeHost, "url" | "operatorToken">, options: SeedAccountsOptions): Promise<ProvisionedSessions> {
  return accounts(host, options, "production");
}

// @ref LLP 3000.000.000.002#4-staging-clock — staging grants clock control, never fixture or persona authority.
pub(super) fn staging(shared: &Shared) -> Result<bool, snapback4_core::Refusal> {
    shared.engine.call(|engine, _| engine.store().deployment_environment().ok().flatten().as_deref() == Some("staging"))
}
fn clock_service(request: &Request, shared: &Shared) -> Result<Option<String>, snapback4_core::Refusal> {
    // @ref LLP 3000.000.000.002#10-wave-25-day-two-friction-dx8 — real-user dev clock authority is independent of fixture doors.
    if shared.personas {
        match super::session_identity::principal_of(request,shared) {
            Ok((principal,kind,snapback4_core::identity::SessionScope::Full)) if kind == "service" && principal == "service:operator" => return Ok(Some(principal)),
            _ if shared.dev_doors() && crate::auth::bearer_token(request).is_err() => return Ok(None),
            _ => return Err(snapback4_core::Refusal::new("E_AUTH", "auth", "development clock control requires a full service:operator token").guide("clock")),
        }
    }
    if !staging(shared)? { return Err(crate::dev_clock::release()); }
    match super::session_identity::principal_of(request,shared) {
        Ok((principal,kind,snapback4_core::identity::SessionScope::Full)) if kind == "service" && principal.starts_with("service:") => Ok(Some(principal)),
        _ => Err(snapback4_core::Refusal::new("E_AUTH", "auth", "staging clock control requires a full service token").guide("clock")),
    }
}
fn checkpoint_clock(engine: &mut snapback4_core::Engine, delta: i64, before: i64, preserve: bool, checkpoint: &snapback4_core::metadata::StagingClock) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    let result = engine.checkpoint_staging_clock_at(delta, before, preserve, checkpoint);
    #[cfg(feature = "postgres")]
    if matches!(result, Err(RuntimeError::CommitOutcomeUnknown(_))) { crate::lifecycle::uncertain_clock_commit(); }
    result
}
fn incomplete_report(cause: &snapback4_core::Refusal) -> snapback4_core::Refusal {
    snapback4_core::Refusal::new("E_CLOCK_REPORT_INCOMPLETE", "clock",
        format!("clock already advanced; report incomplete ({cause}); inspect clock or use clock drain (0ms); do not replay the advance"))
        .guide("clock")
}

pub(super) fn advance(request: Request, shared: &Shared, body: &str) {
    let service = match clock_service(&request, shared) {
        Ok(service) => service,
        Err(why) => return respond(request,403,json!({"denied":refusal_json(&why)})),
    };
    let payload = match serde_json::from_str::<serde_json::Value>(body) {
        Ok(payload) => payload,
        Err(_) => return respond(request, 400, json!({"denied":refusal_json(&crate::dev_clock::input("clock advance takes {\"duration\":\"+2m\"}"))})),
    };
    let each_boundary = match payload.get("eachBoundary") {
        None => false,
        Some(serde_json::Value::Bool(value)) => *value,
        Some(_) => return respond(request, 400, json!({"denied":refusal_json(&crate::dev_clock::input("eachBoundary must be a boolean"))})),
    };
    let boolean = |key: &str, default: bool| -> Result<bool, snapback4_core::Refusal> {
        match payload.get(key) {
            None => Ok(default), Some(serde_json::Value::Bool(value)) => Ok(*value),
            Some(_) => Err(crate::dev_clock::input(format!("{key} must be a boolean"))),
        }
    };
    let (drain, allow_held) = match boolean("drain", true).and_then(|drain| Ok((drain, boolean("allowHeld", false)?))) {
        Ok(options) => options,
        Err(error) => return respond(request, 400, json!({"denied":refusal_json(&error)})),
    };
    if each_boundary && !drain { return respond(request, 400, json!({"denied":refusal_json(&crate::dev_clock::input("eachBoundary requires drain:true"))})); }
    let preserve_sessions = match payload.get("sessions").and_then(|s| s.as_str()) {
        None if payload.get("sessions").is_none() => service.is_some(),
        Some("preserve") if service.is_some() => true,
        Some("expire") => false,
        _ => return respond(request, 400, json!({"denied":refusal_json(&crate::dev_clock::input("sessions must be preserve (authenticated development or staging only) or expire"))})),
    };
    let duration = payload.get("duration").and_then(|value| value.as_str());

        .and_then(|duration| if duration == "0ms" && drain { Ok(0) } else { crate::dev_clock::duration(duration) }) {
        Ok(milliseconds) => milliseconds,

/** @throws None at construction; locator observation can refuse ambiguous or invalid selectors. */
// @ref LLP 3000.000.000.001#11-wave-22-loop-repairs-dx6 — composed text preserves exactness and ambiguity.
export function renderedText(page: Page, text: string | RegExp, options: { within?: Locator; exact?: boolean } | null = {}): Locator {
  const normalized = options ?? {};
  const root = normalized.within ?? page;
  const match = (exact: boolean) => registeredLocator(root.locator(`${renderedSelector}=${JSON.stringify({
    source: typeof text === "string" ? text : text.source,
    flags: typeof text === "string" ? "" : text.flags,
    regex: typeof text !== "string", exact,
  })}`));
  const locator = match(normalized.exact ?? true);
  if (typeof text === "string" && normalized.exact !== false) substringLocators.set(locator, { locator: match(false), scoped: normalized.within !== undefined });
  return locator;
}

export interface ExhaustPagesOptions {
  /** Refuse rather than loop forever when a backend returns bad continuations. */
  maxPages?: number;
  /** Names the read in a refusal without exposing its rows or cursor. */
  label?: string;
}
type ProofPage<Row> =
  | { readonly data: readonly Row[]; readonly next?: Cursor | null; readonly complete?: boolean; readonly cut?: string | null }
  | { readonly denied: Refusal }
  | { readonly loading: true };
/** Read every page by passing each opaque `next` back unchanged. The callback
 * receives null first. Refusals, loading, malformed/repeated cursors and a
 * capped or incomplete page fail instead of returning a partial snapshot. */
/** @throws E_PROOF_PAGE for a refused, loading, malformed, repeated, incomplete or over-limit traversal; callback errors propagate. */
// @ref LLP 3000.000.000.002#10-wave-25-day-two-friction-dx8 — proof traversal must never promote capped or partial reads to complete evidence.
export async function exhaustPages<Row>(readPage: (cursor: Cursor | null) => Promise<ProofPage<Row>>, options: ExhaustPagesOptions = {}): Promise<Row[]> {
  const maxPages = options.maxPages ?? 1_000, label = options.label ?? "paged read";
  if (!Number.isSafeInteger(maxPages) || maxPages < 1) throw new Error(`E_PROOF_PAGE: ${label}: maxPages must be a positive safe integer`);
  const rows: Row[] = [], seen = new Set<string>();
  let cursor: Cursor | null = null;
  for (let pageNumber = 1; pageNumber <= maxPages; pageNumber++) {
    const page = await readPage(cursor);
    if (!page || typeof page !== "object") throw new Error(`E_PROOF_PAGE: ${label}: page ${pageNumber} returned no read envelope`);
    if ("denied" in page) throw new Error(`E_PROOF_PAGE: ${label}: page ${pageNumber} refused ${page.denied.code}: ${page.denied.message}`);
    if ("loading" in page) throw new Error(`E_PROOF_PAGE: ${label}: page ${pageNumber} is still loading`);
    if (!Array.isArray(page.data)) throw new Error(`E_PROOF_PAGE: ${label}: page ${pageNumber} data is not an array`);
    const cut = page.cut ?? null;
    if (page.complete === false || cut !== null) throw new Error(`E_PROOF_PAGE: ${label}: page ${pageNumber} is incomplete${cut === null ? "" : ` (${cut} cut)`}`);
    rows.push(...page.data);
    const next = page.next ?? null;
    if (next === null) return rows;
    if (typeof next !== "string" || next.length === 0) throw new Error(`E_PROOF_PAGE: ${label}: page ${pageNumber} returned a malformed cursor`);
    if (seen.has(next)) throw new Error(`E_PROOF_PAGE: ${label}: page ${pageNumber} returned a repeated cursor`);
    seen.add(next);
    cursor = next as Cursor;
  }
  throw new Error(`E_PROOF_PAGE: ${label}: exceeded maxPages ${maxPages}`);
}

/** @throws page closed or browser activation failed. */
export async function frontPage(page: Page): Promise<Page> { await page.bringToFront(); return page; }

//! Staging clock preservation: one shared authority decision on both stores.
use crate::{RuntimeError, authority::{self, AuthorityTransaction, SessionScope}};
use super::Engine;
#[cfg(test)]
use super::effects::OutboxTransaction;

pub(crate) const PAGE: usize = 128;

pub(crate) struct ClockSession {
    pub digest: String,
    pub created_at: i64,
}

pub(crate) struct ClockMint {
    pub invocation: String,
    pub intent: String,
    pub principal: String,
    pub family: String,
    pub generation: i64,
    pub expires_at: i64,
}

/// Bounded facts and exact timestamp writes, under the existing writer fence.
/// Worker-marked families are excluded from this enumeration: their timestamps
/// remain owned by the existing worker-lease primitive.
pub(crate) trait SessionClockTransaction: AuthorityTransaction {
    fn clock_sessions(&self, read: bool, after: Option<&str>) -> Result<Vec<ClockSession>, RuntimeError>;
    fn set_clock_session(&self, digest: &str, scope: SessionScope, created_at: i64, expires_at: i64, cap: Option<i64>) -> Result<(), RuntimeError>;
    fn clock_mints(&self, after: Option<(&str, &str)>) -> Result<Vec<ClockMint>, RuntimeError>;
    fn set_clock_mint(&self, invocation: &str, intent: &str, expires_at: i64) -> Result<(), RuntimeError>;
}

fn shifted(value: i64, delta: i64) -> Result<i64, RuntimeError> {
    value.checked_add(delta).ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::Input("staging clock advance overflows a session timestamp; use a smaller advance".into()))
}

fn sessions(tx: &impl SessionClockTransaction, delta: i64, before: i64, apply: bool) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    for read in [false, true] {
        let mut after = None;
        loop {
            let page = tx.clock_sessions(read, after.as_deref())?;
            if page.is_empty() { break; }
            for candidate in &page {
                let Some(session) = authority::live_session(tx, &candidate.digest, before)? else { continue; };
                // Delegated action credentials remain capped by their claim and
                // product deadline. The worker primitive owns their lease shift.
                if session.kind == "action" || (session.scope == SessionScope::Read) != read { continue; }
                let created_at = shifted(candidate.created_at, delta)?;
                let expires_at = shifted(session.expires_at, delta)?;
                let cap = session.cap_expires_at.map(|cap| shifted(cap, delta)).transpose()?;
                if apply { tx.set_clock_session(&candidate.digest, session.scope, created_at, expires_at, cap)?; }
            }
            after = page.last().map(|s| s.digest.clone());
        }
    }
    Ok(())
}

fn mints(tx: &impl SessionClockTransaction, delta: i64, before: i64, apply: bool) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    let mut after: Option<(String, String)> = None;
    loop {
        let page = tx.clock_mints(after.as_ref().map(|(invocation, intent)| (invocation.as_str(), intent.as_str())))?;
        if page.is_empty() { break; }
        for mint in &page {
            if mint.expires_at <= before || authority::family_authority_expiry(tx, &mint.principal, &mint.family, mint.generation, before)?.is_none() { continue; }
            let expires_at = shifted(mint.expires_at, delta)?;
            if apply { tx.set_clock_mint(&mint.invocation, &mint.intent, expires_at)?; }
        }
        after = page.last().map(|m| (m.invocation.clone(), m.intent.clone()));
    }
    Ok(())
}

// @ref LLP 3000.000.000.001#11-wave-22-loop-repairs-dx6 — staging can preserve live credential lifetime while moving application time.
pub(crate) fn preserve(tx: &impl SessionClockTransaction, delta: i64, before: i64) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    if delta < 0 { return Err(RuntimeError::Input("staging clock advance must be non-negative".into())); }
    // Validate the complete change with bounded pages before the first write.
    // The writer transaction gives both passes the same authority snapshot;
    // shifts never change token keys, scope, family or revocation generations.
    sessions(tx, delta, before, false)?;
    mints(tx, delta, before, false)?;
    mints(tx, delta, before, true)?;
    sessions(tx, delta, before, true)
}

/// Server-authoritative lifetime of the exact live bearer, without token material.
pub struct SessionTiming {
    pub expires_at: i64,
    pub cap_expires_at: Option<i64>,
}

impl Engine {
    /// Read timing from the same detached authority snapshot as store identity.
    /// HTTP invokes this only for an immutable classified staging host.
    pub fn staging_session_timing(&self, token: &str, now: i64, expected_store: &str) -> Result<Option<SessionTiming>, RuntimeError> {
        Ok(crate::identity::resolve_session_record_snapshot(&self.store, &crate::identity::digest(token), now, None, Some(expected_store))?
            .map(|session| SessionTiming { expires_at: session.expires_at, cap_expires_at: session.cap_expires_at }))
    }
}

export type ReadCut = "meter" | "words" | null;

/**
 * A read. `complete` is false beyond a sync horizon or at an online-only site.
 * `cut` names a disclosed meter or word-limit cut. Non-uniform server reads
 * withhold it: null means "not disclosed here", not "not cut".
 * An empty meter-cut page is partial: retry narrower, or continue if `next` exists.
 * `fresh` is transport truth: the value reflects the server now, as far as the
 * change feed can tell. When it does not, `since` is the wall-clock time (ms) of
 * the newest server state it does reflect, so a screen can say "as of three minutes ago".
 * Observations discard responses older than observed commits in the same store and
 * permanently refuse on store replacement or regression.
 * `connected[p]` is the exception. No commit announces that `connected[p]` changed, so an answer of a query that
 * may read it is a sample: it keeps the `fresh` it arrived with (true for a server's whole answer, false when
 * a local answer composes retained rows) until a newer answer replaces it. A background re-read about
 * 5 seconds after each reply settles does that while refreshes succeed; failed refreshes or a suspended
 * client leave the sample on screen, with the same flag, and no bound on its age.
 * A mounted same-key observation retains its answer during plain revalidation,
 * marked `revalidating: true`, until replacement, with no timeout. Transport
 * failure marks `fresh: false`; retry exhaustion also keeps the answer. Local
 * commits, durable/ephemeral change evidence and /sync publications start one
 * non-renewable second at the replacement attempt; capacity queue wait pauses
 * this budget. Online change
 * evidence can be revocation, so its second starts on receipt and includes queue
 * wait. Failure or timeout withdraws; a later accepted answer can still publish.
 * Applied local scope loss withdraws dependent audience groups immediately,
 * including staged removals; older replica answers wait for publication.
 * `keep: false` withdraws immediately; `keep: true` adds a 30 s subscriber-free
 * linger. Gate actions on `isReady(read) && !read.revalidating` when they
 * require a completed re-read.
 * Every option withdraws on expiry, a refusal, a viewer or credential change,
 * store reset, adoption or close. Retention never grants authority.
 * @ref LLP 3000.023#10 — all change evidence bounds retention.
 * Before the first answer use `loading`; a refusal carries its message in `denied`.
 */
// @ref LLP 3000.031#1.1 — existence describes the disclosed value, never hidden rows.
export interface ReadyMetadata {
  readonly complete: boolean;
  readonly cut: ReadCut;
  readonly fresh: boolean;
  readonly revalidating?: true;
  readonly since?: number;
  readonly next?: Cursor | null;
  readonly meter?: ReadMeter;
}
// @ref LLP 3000.031#3 — named arms preserve generic covariance.
// Every arm retains T; absent additionally proves null or an empty array.
interface Present<out T> extends ReadyMetadata {
  readonly existence: "present";
  readonly data: T & ({} | undefined);
}
interface Partial<out T> extends ReadyMetadata {
  readonly existence: "partial";
  readonly data: T;
}
interface Absent<out T> extends ReadyMetadata {
  readonly existence: "absent";
  readonly data: T & (null | []);
  readonly complete: true;
  readonly cut: null;
}
export type Ready<T> = Present<T> | Partial<T> | Absent<T>;
export type Read<T> =
  | Ready<T>
  | { readonly loading: true }
  | { readonly denied: Refusal };
/** A one-shot's final answer; ready can still be partial or stale. */
export type SettledRead<T> = Exclude<Read<T>, { readonly loading: true }>;

/** Engine work for this server answer under dev; never returned by serve. */
export interface ReadMeter {
  /** Precise reason, including non-uniform work that public `Read.cut` withholds. */
  readonly cut: ReadCut;
  readonly examined: number;
  readonly indexEntries: number;
  readonly probes: number;

/// Ask the current retained owner to stop through its private same-owner
/// control socket, then wait until the store lock proves cleanup completed.
pub fn stop_release(options: &Options) -> i32 {
    let result = (|| {
        if crate::store_config::StoreConfig::resolve(options)?.is_some() {
            return Err("E_STORE_UNSUPPORTED: stop is outside the admitted store profile".into());
        }
        let dir = existing_deployment(&directory(options)?)?;
        match crate::release_control::request(&dir, crate::release_control::Command::Stop) {
            Ok(_) => {
                crate::store_owner::wait_released(&dir.join("store.sqlite"), std::time::Duration::from_secs(10))?;
                println!("store stopped: {}", dir.display());
                Ok(())
            }
            Err(control) => match crate::store_owner::wait_released(&dir.join("store.sqlite"), std::time::Duration::ZERO) {
                Ok(()) => {
                    println!("store already stopped: {}", dir.display());
                    Ok(())
                }
                Err(_) => Err(control),
            },
        }
    })();
    match result {
        Ok(()) => 0,
        Err(error) => { eprintln!("stop: {error}"); 1 }
    }
}

pub(crate) fn release_bind(options: &Options) -> Result<std::net::IpAddr, String> {
    let bind = options
        .flags
        .get("bind")
        .map(String::as_str)
        .unwrap_or("127.0.0.1");
    let address: std::net::IpAddr = bind
        .parse()
        .map_err(|_| "--bind must be a literal IPv4 or IPv6 address".to_owned())?;
    if !address.is_loopback() {
        if !options.flags.contains_key("i-understand-cleartext") {
            return Err("non-loopback HTTP sends passwords and bearer tokens in cleartext; use a loopback listener behind a TLS proxy, or explicitly pass --i-understand-cleartext".into());
        }
        eprintln!(
            "WARNING: listening on {address} over CLEARTEXT HTTP. Passwords, bearer tokens, schema and data have no transport protection. Expose only through a TLS proxy on a trusted private hop; see snapback4/README.md."
        );
    }
    Ok(address)
}

## 8. Wave-21 release evolution

Implementer: Codex for Charlie Cheever (EVOL), 2026-10-21; target 0.4.37.
This amendment supersedes the insert-only input policy in §3. Ordinary
writes while the exact declaration is active refresh existing native input
fingerprints. They do not create native birth evidence for migrated rows.
Active forward receipts refresh only actually edited, currently authored
non-output inputs and their reference lifetime bindings. Overwritten historical
inputs, original output preimages, omission interpretations on unrelated
writes, inactive receipts and immutable definitions retain their custody.
An older-release edit does not receive this refresh. At re-entry a changed
input is compatible when deterministic recomputation over the assembled source
produces the exact stored output; an incompatible edit without accepted native evidence still refuses.
Valid native outputs written under the owning declaration remain authored
values, even when they differ from the historical transform. Refresh replaces
the fingerprint, rather than accumulating old accepted images.

Rename destination conflicts consult only physically present or retained cells.
An absent optional destination is absent; interpreting it as null or a default
cannot manufacture a conflict. Explicit present null is still a real cell.

SEC6 owns rule-changing deploy review items, write intent, projection privacy
and client session validation; EVOL does not change those contracts.


A table declares `table observations renamed from behaviors:`. It moves the
same numeric table identity and row/reference lifetimes, never copies rows or
merges independent histories. Both names cannot appear together in a selected
schema. The durable name relation permits old-source rollback and older-source
writes to return under the newer name. Existing independent destination storage
refuses atomically. Indexes, retained type definitions, asset homes and migration
receipts follow that identity; literal values and operation names do not change.
Store format 8 and the release-table-renames-v1 capability fence the new metadata.

`rollback --check --bridge DIR` now writes the selected target's complete source,
loosening only differing compatible value domains to their union with current
source and retaining new authoritative tables with their current rules. It lists
changes and direct target read/write operations on affected tables. It never changes immutable
source or activates automatically. The generated bridge is a new deployable
release, subject to ordinary compilation/adoption. Incompatible nominal types,
new business logic, current guards absent from v1, and old UI assumptions about
null require explicit review; diagnostics and the app-specific checklist remain
visible. A retained new table does not claim a frozen v1 operation exposes it.
No reverse projection fabricates an asset, reference, list element or missing
table. Scalar `down` remains the answer for a truthful scalar substitution;
loosened bridges cover nullable files/images, empty image lists and optional
money without invented placeholders.

Preview causes are recorded by the actual narrowed-population closure: a column
and incompatible value category, or a typed reference to a hidden parent. Counts
may overlap across causes and are schema populations before read rules. New-table
rows have a separate cause. Foresight consults declared reverse writers rather
than calling their old output absent. SEC6 owns review-item resurfacing.

Asset columns (`file`, image/video and asset lists) use `renamed from` as an
identity move, rather than computed scalar forward/down substitutions. The
active and retained physical cells and the ledger's one home move together;
selecting old source changes the public name back, preserving current values.
Both aliases cannot be active together; a conflicting physical/retained cell
refuses atomically. Reference/captured/engine field renames remain excluded.
Older runtimes refuse store format 8; device projections omit migration metadata.

Cross-table historical existence and unique point reads are first-class
backfill inputs under §2's assembled-source, metered system authority. A guide
example must include the source index and explicit row input rather than an
application reconciliation loop. Outputs remain disjoint scalar patches and
all declarations observe the same pre-patch snapshot.

`fileText(file)` is a migration-only deterministic input, accepted in backfill
and down expressions, including through pure helpers. It reads the immutable
retained file named by the ledger, as UTF-8. It never reads an application path
or URL, unverified bytes, arbitrary ids, or a device's filesystem. The runtime
requires an internal migration context and charges the job heap and step meter
before reading at most the ledger byte length plus one. Absent bytes, released
assets, length disagreement, invalid UTF-8 or meter exhaustion refuse the whole
adoption. Preview reads the same original immutable asset root while writing
only its private database snapshot. The feature creates no user-callable
migration operation and does not make arbitrary file contents readable to users.
`nonemptyLines(text)` counts trimmed nonempty lines, including repeated entries
separately; CRLF and whitespace-only lines have their ordinary text meaning.
It is a pure scalar transform; an empty textual list counts zero. A generated
scalar is reversible through the existing output-preimage/evidence custody;
fileText never mutates the bytes or their home. Ordinary new-order byte validation
is outside this release-migration primitive; it receives no invented enforcement
claim from a historical backfill.


### EVOL review clarifications

“Exact declaration” means its normalized typed-IR digest, not a release number.
A declaration can be carried in later source. “Currently authored” means a
column present in that selected schema whose interpreted value actually changes
in an application write; hidden inputs and overwritten migration arguments are
excluded. “Older-release edit” means a write while this exact declaration is
absent. Replacing accepted non-output inputs intentionally forgets their previous
live values: this is live data, not a history of every application write. It
never forgets an overwritten output's reversal preimage. For migrated rows the
parent's dependency/output comparison still applies, with exact recomputation
agreement added for both transformed and fully reversed cells. Native rows with
matching refreshed non-output fingerprints and valid complete outputs preserve
their authored images. A fingerprint mismatch needs full recomputation agreement
or refuses; it never authorizes overwriting a different authored image.

A helper inherits the migration context transitively when expanded. Calling a
helper containing fileText outside a backfill/down refuses during compilation;
raw IR also refuses at execution without the unforgeable internal context/root.
Failure in a forward migration, down, or exact preview aborts that entire
transaction. Preview has the identical job precharge and file validation.
File-read failures never print raw bytes. Output scalars are deliberately
authored data under their destination rules; an author copying file text into
a readable text column must choose its audience explicitly, as for any backfill.
No automatic output or diagnostic exposes the input bytes. Asserted missing
points refuse the whole operation. Nullable points preserve §2's ordinary
nullable semantics only through the author's explicit expression; no missing
point becomes an invented preimage. The database snapshot pins ledger and point
reads; asset bytes are immutable under their ledger id, read at exact length.

Rename conflicts compare a destination cell not owned by the moving alias in
the same surviving row lifetime. They do not compare the source cell against
itself or consult dead row lifetimes. Table moves keep their numeric identity;
independent destination identities cannot be merged. Asset renames never revive
a released asset. A selected schema still validates every moved asset against
the ledger/type. Reference, captured and engine-field renames are diagnosed
before activation, rather than silently losing their bindings.
## 9. Edited migrated outputs (ROLL)

Commissioned for 0.4.39, 2026-10-22. LLP 3000.000.000 §20 and its review
clarifications govern default rollback views and the closed safety boundary.
An in-place output edited under its declaring source may use a declared down
on its current assembled image, or keep the exact value in a target domain
which already admits it. A failing old type/constraint still refuses; no
per-row widening or invented scalar is allowed. Unedited outputs keep exact
preimage precedence, with a receipt saying when that old view is stale until
forward. Real forward conflicts include competing values and bridge repair.

Receipts distinguish the original preimage, authored newer image, saved
computation, selected old physical image and presence. Equal explicit inputs
alone cannot authorize retaining an edited output: fresh point/existence
recomputation must also agree with the saved computation, or exactly equal
the actual authored image. Changed projected cells, parents, pruned evidence
and meter cuts still refuse atomically. Editing never creates native-birth
status. Format 9 fences this retained law, including after selecting old source;
format 8 remains readable and upgrades transactionally on successful adoption.
Repeated cycles, partially retired outputs and table-name changes preserve
that evidence. The implementation adds focused regressions for those paths.

## 20. Visible rollback views (ROLL)

Implementer: Codex for Charlie Cheever, 2026-10-22; commissioned for 0.4.39.
This amendment replaces opt-in bridging for declared domain widenings and
relaxed unique keys. Existing retention, metering, authority, immutable source,
and atomic activation laws continue to apply. SEC7 owns ordinary check gates
and ranking; ROLL owns deploy presentation and release selection.

### 20.1 Prepared source and selection

Deploy compares every selectable ordinary predecessor, even without sampled
widened rows. A widening without an applicable authored down requires a compiled
rollback view. All needed views must prepare successfully or deploy refuses.
View admission compares structural source domains (including text minima and
formats), nominal aliases, unique keys and caps; it never parses human advice
strings to decide whether a view is needed. A fitting authored scalar down
removes its covered value-domain risk; extraction/renames retain their typed
identity laws.
The compatible join is LLP 3000.000.000.002 §8: optional union, integer and text
bound union with identical format, enum union, recursively joined bounded lists,
and asset size-bound union with identical nominal kind, MIME and once policy.
Nullability and list cardinality join separately. This differs from extraction,
which requires full type equality, including size, optionality and cardinality.
References join only across explicit nominal identity renames. A mixed change
cannot enlarge any current shared-column domain with predecessor-only values:
current read/write predicates may rely on their schema-level impossibility.
The ordinary union view refuses even if that column is not the widening trigger.
A closed alternative may retain the complete current authored source closure
(schema, defaults, programs, helpers, jobs and authority), rather than synthesize
a union. It requires every predecessor table and column under the same spelling,
every predecessor operation name/kind, and current argument domains accepting
every predecessor input under the same closed compatibility rule below. It
retains the exact current domains and values, with no resurrection, translation
or old-only writes. Missing fields/names or incompatible inputs refuse. Current
result semantics are disclosed for old-UI review; current extra operations
remain current-authorized. Native/worker bundle qualification remains mandatory.
Otherwise author a compatible source and truthful migration before deploying. Enum order
and nominal rename spelling do not change this domain comparison. Domain
includes nullability, recursive list-element/cardinality bounds, text formats,
integer bounds, asset kind/size/MIME/once constraints; unsupported or incompatible
domain changes refuse rather than truncate or coerce. No conversion,
zero-money fallback, implicit business default or arbitrary row choice is inferred.

The view retains predecessor operation names and argument contracts, including
existing authored operation aliases under their compilation law (same-module,
no alias chains). Every exported predecessor alias counts as an old name; a
current alias resolves to its actual authored target for input, guard, helper
and authority comparison. Renaming an export never drops its current guards. A current
require can be copied only with identical argument and entire transitive local
binding/helper meanings, lookup predicates and control-flow paths. A changed
unmodeled closure or guard depending on writes refuses. Canonical expression
comparison ignores source locations, never semantics. When a relaxed unique key invalidates an old lookup, or current guards require
an added field on a shared table, retain that field and its authored current
backfill. An affected operation whose current guard/workflow cannot be transported
unchanged may likewise reuse its current authored endpoint under the same closed
input-compatibility rule. Every current added field is retained on a shared table, even without a guard
mention; required fields retain their authored default/backfill and every old
write path must supply them through a compatible current authored endpoint or
refuse compilation. No missing-required-field write is permitted. All helpers in a reused workflow, including write,
return and system-scope dependencies, need identical transitive meanings. An already-authored current endpoint of the same name/kind whose
arguments accept every predecessor input may replace the affected old endpoint;
the initial comparison permits identical argument types, optional widening and
integer-bound widening and enum-superset widening, never narrowing or new required arguments. Retained
current backfill identity keeps its output custody active. This supplies authored
writes for the added required fields instead of inventing defaults; the generator never invents first-row
selection. The replacement's point/limited selection must retain the language's
deterministic total ordering, including its stable id tie-break; a source outside
that deterministic language fragment refuses. Missing or incompatible authored
endpoints refuse compilation.

A generated visible view retains the current forward-migration declaration set
for shared output columns. It does not resurrect a retired predecessor transform
whose outputs already remain current columns in the compiled union. Historical
source and receipts remain intact. This preserves current native values and
migration custody instead of reinterpreting them with a retired input domain.
Transforms needed for fields removed from current source remain subject to the
ordinary truthful-projection and complete-adoption checks.

A generated visible view omits a predecessor-only historical down declaration
when all its outputs remain current columns whose generated domains admit the
current native values. Its immutable original declaration and receipt evidence
remain historical; the view does not reenter that retired projection or restore
stale preimages over native values. Downs needed for removed fields remain.
Exact original-source selection retains its projection/conflict behavior. Writer
adoption still enforces full domains, lifetimes and zero-unacknowledged hiding.

export interface ScheduleOutcome {
  name: string;
  target: string;
  period: string;
  fired: boolean;
  reason: string;
  holdCode?: "disabled" | "saved-boundary" | "initialized" | "not-due" | "maintenance-failed";
  savedBoundary?: number;
  nextBoundary: string;
  heldUntil?: string;
  periodStart?: number;
  periodEnd?: number;
  firedWindows: { periodStart: number; periodEnd: number }[];
  skippedPeriods?: number;
  skippedBoundaries: { periodStart: number; periodEnd: number; command: string }[];
  skippedBoundariesOmitted?: number;
  run?: { id: string; state: string; rowsWritten: number; error: string | null; errorTruncated: boolean };
}
export interface ClockAdvance { now: number; fired: ScheduleOutcome[]; held: ScheduleOutcome[]; maintenancePaused: boolean; valueOf(): number }
export interface AdvanceOptions { eachBoundary?: boolean; drain?: boolean; allowHeld?: boolean; sessions?: "preserve" | "expire" }
export interface ServeHost extends ProofHost {
  /** Authoritative staging time; requires a staging store and local operator authority. */
  now(): Promise<number>;
  /** Advance staging time once and drain due work. A lost reply must not be retried. */
  advance(duration: string, options?: AdvanceOptions): Promise<ClockAdvance>;
  /** Resume and drain maintenance without advancing time. */
  drain(options?: Pick<AdvanceOptions, "eachBoundary" | "allowHeld">): Promise<ClockAdvance>;
  operatorToken(service?: string, days?: number): Promise<string>;
  seedAccounts(options: SeedAccountsOptions): Promise<StagingSessions>;
  provisionAccounts(options: SeedAccountsOptions): Promise<StagingSessions>;
  sessions?: StagingSessions;
}
export interface ServeOptions extends ProofOptions { seedUsers?: SeedAccountsOptions; provisionUsers?: SeedAccountsOptions; clockOffset?: string }
// @ref LLP 3000.000.000.001#2-real-staging-accounts — operator authority stays in the existing local store proof; bulk setup refuses production.
/** Starts a release. stop() awaits native exit and kernel store release.
 * @throws E_PROOF_USAGE for missing deployment directory; child exit, readiness deadline or served identity mismatch. stop() rejects bounded cleanup failure. */
export async function startServe(deployDir: string, options: ServeOptions | readonly string[] = {}): Promise<ServeHost> {
  if (typeof deployDir !== "string" || !deployDir.trim()) throw Object.assign(new TypeError("E_PROOF_USAGE: startServe(deployDir, options) requires a deployment directory as its first argument"), { code: "E_PROOF_USAGE" });
  const opts: ServeOptions = Array.isArray(options) ? { args: options } : options as ServeOptions;
  const directory = resolve(opts.cwd ?? ".", deployDir);
  if (opts.clockOffset !== undefined && (typeof opts.clockOffset !== "string" || !/^[+-]?\d+(ms|s|m|h|d|w)$/.test(opts.clockOffset))) throw new TypeError("E_PROOF_USAGE: clockOffset is a signed duration such as +25h");
  if (opts.clockOffset !== undefined && opts.args?.some(arg => arg === "--clock-offset" || arg.startsWith("--clock-offset="))) throw new TypeError("E_PROOF_USAGE: pass clockOffset once");
  const host = await nativeHost("serve", [directory], { ...opts, args: [...(opts.args ?? []), ...(opts.clockOffset === undefined ? [] : ["--clock-offset", opts.clockOffset])] });
  const clockCall = async (body?: object): Promise<ClockAdvance> => {
    const config = await fetchIdentity(`${host.url}/auth/config`, deadline(opts.timeoutMs, 10_000));
    if (!config.response.ok || config.body.environment !== "staging") throw Error("E_CLOCK_DEV_ONLY: clock controls require a deploy --staging store");
    const clockToken = served.operatorToken("clock");
    const response = await fetch(`${host.url}/dev/clock/${body ? "advance" : "status"}`, { method: body ? "POST" : "GET", headers: { Authorization: `Bearer ${await clockToken}`, "Content-Type": "application/json" }, ...(body ? { body: JSON.stringify(body) } : {}), signal: AbortSignal.timeout(deadline(opts.timeoutMs, 60_000)) });
    const result = await response.json() as Partial<ClockAdvance> & { sessionNotice?: string; denied?: { code?: string; message?: string } };
    if (result.sessionNotice) process.stderr.write(`snapback4: ${result.sessionNotice}\n`);
    if (!response.ok || result.denied || !Number.isSafeInteger(result.now)) throw Object.assign(Error(`staging clock refused or reply unavailable; inspect clock and queued work before retrying: ${result.denied?.code ?? response.status} ${result.denied?.message ?? ""}`), { report: result });
    if (body && (!Array.isArray(result.fired) || !Array.isArray(result.held))) throw Error("staging clock reply has no schedule report; inspect clock and queued work before retrying");
    for (const schedule of result.held ?? []) if (schedule.holdCode === "saved-boundary") process.stderr.write(`snapback4: schedule ${schedule.name}: ${schedule.reason}\n`);
    const report: ClockAdvance = { now: result.now!, fired: result.fired ?? [], held: result.held ?? [], maintenancePaused: result.maintenancePaused ?? false, valueOf() { return this.now; } };
    return report;
  };
  // Exit can precede inherited watchdog flock release. The private local
  // barrier observes the same canonical ownership sidecar without writing data.
  let stopping: Promise<void> | undefined;
  const stop = () => stopping ??= (async () => {
    await host.stop();
    const result = await cli(["serve", directory, "--wait-stopped"], {...opts, timeoutMs:6000});
    if (result.code !== 0) throw Error(`proof serve store release failed: ${result.stderr}`);
  })().catch(error => { stopping = undefined; throw error; });
  const served: ServeHost = { ...host, stop,
    now: async () => (await clockCall()).now,
    advance: (duration, options = {}) => {
      if (typeof duration !== "string" || (!/^\+?\d+(ms|s|m|h|d|w)$/.test(duration) || BigInt(duration.replace(/\D/g, "")) === 0n)) throw new TypeError("E_PROOF_USAGE: advance requires a positive whole duration");
      return clockCall({ duration, ...options });
    },
    drain: (options = {}) => clockCall({ duration: "0ms", drain: true, ...options }),
    operatorToken: async (service = "operator", days = 1) => {
      if (!/^[A-Za-z0-9_-]{1,64}$/.test(service) || !Number.isSafeInteger(days) || days < 1 || days > 3650) throw Error("proof operatorToken: invalid service or days (1..3650)");
      const result = await cli(["token", "--service", service, "--days", String(days), "--release", directory, "--quiet"], opts);
      if (result.code !== 0) throw Error(`proof operator token refused: ${result.stderr}`);
      const token = result.stdout.trim();
      if (!/^[A-Za-z0-9_-]{43}$/.test(token)) throw Error("proof operator token malformed");
      return token;
    },
    seedAccounts: accountOptions => seedAccounts(served, accountOptions),
    provisionAccounts: accountOptions => provisionAccounts(served, accountOptions),
  };
  try {
    if (opts.seedUsers && opts.provisionUsers) throw new TypeError("E_PROOF_USAGE: choose seedUsers for staging or provisionUsers for production");
    if (opts.seedUsers) served.sessions = await served.seedAccounts(opts.seedUsers);
    if (opts.provisionUsers) served.sessions = await served.provisionAccounts(opts.provisionUsers);
    return served;
  }
  catch (error) { await served.stop(); throw error; }
}

  const env = { ...process.env, ...options.env }, ports = portRange(env), timeout = deadline(options.timeoutMs, 30_000);
