- `snapback/generated/api.ts` is built by `check` and `dev`, never committed;
  the app's typecheck needs a `check` first (the suite does one).
- Point rule probes and `exists` require a unique key. A fully pinned superset
  of a required unique key is unique too (for example an explicit role index
  containing the unique conversation/principal pair). Nullable unique key
  arguments need non-null proof. Other existence checks use bounded populations
  in code. Named equalities covering a total unique key check extra columns on
  that one row without an additional index.
- The packaged CLI uses `LIBSQLITE3_FLAGS=-USQLITE_ENABLE_MEMORY_MANAGEMENT` during both publication tests and its production build (LLP 3000 §7). A plain production Cargo build retains the dependency's ordinary SQLite policy; use the same build input when comparing with the package. The variable is unnecessary at runtime.
- When creating a stage, `scripts/publish.mjs` runs the suite and refuses on red, builds the production binary, packs `snapback4-darwin-arm64` and verifies the installed digest, then publishes both packages. Bump `version` in `packages/snapback4/package.json` (and its `optionalDependencies`), `packages/snapback4-darwin-arm64/package.json`,
  `packages/snapback4-linux-x64/package.json` and `Cargo.toml` together; a test refuses a mismatch.
- Publication retains exact qualified tarballs under `target/publication/`. If a
  stage exists, use `node scripts/publish.mjs --resume` to validate its recorded
  suite qualification, requalify those exact bytes through the installed-consumer
  proof, and finish missing registry artifacts; an existing version must match
  its staged integrity. This also applies after `--dry-run`. An ordinary invocation
  refuses an existing stage so changed source cannot silently publish old bytes.
  Bump the version to build a new pair; preserve a partially published stage.
- Cross-host publication (LLP 3000.021 §3.3): prepare the common client once
  from clean release source, qualify that tarball on Linux, and import the
  Linux tarball plus its receipt on the Mac. Source commit, client SRI and
  Linux SRI must agree. Linux prefers working Docker/Podman, otherwise uses checksum-pinned proot
  with clean Ubuntu 24.04/Alpine root filesystems in a user cache, and refuses
  `NPM_TOKEN` or `NODE_AUTH_TOKEN`, even when empty. It never signs or packs
  the client. The Mac never rebuilds or executes Linux; `--resume` rechecks
  its copied receipt. No receipt packs a Darwin-only client; an existing
  two-platform preparation is refused on that path. Packed dependencies must
  match the staged set. Uploads use verified private snapshots, with each
  digest checked again immediately before publish.
  Commands and current qualification limits:
  [`reports/20260927-lx-linux-package/REPORT.md`](./reports/20260927-lx-linux-package/REPORT.md).
- The guide is tested text. Every ```quarry block in `crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide.rs` and in `packages/snapback4/README.md` is compiled by `crates/snapback4-lang/tests/guide.rs` with no diagnostic and no note (the language card is also run against the engine); every refusal code the sources emit must be in `guide::CODES`; every `guide("..")` a diagnostic carries must name a card. Add a form to the language card when you add it to the parser, fence README Quarry as ```quarry, and never fence Quarry as plain text to dodge the test.
- Native jobs (LLP 3000.005) need `rustup target add wasm32-unknown-unknown`; the suite's fixture build fails loudly without it. The guest SDK `crates/snapback4-native` is a path dependency until it is published; an app's `snapback/native/Cargo.toml` names it by path.

Quarry store testing (LLP 3000.035 §5.1): `SNAPBACK4_EVAL_POSTGRES` is an
explicit evaluation connection. The factory owns one physical schema per worker,
restores its rows and bookkeeping, installs the compiled fixture, then opens the
ordinary engine. Mutation attempts restore a seeded snapshot; shrinking reuses
that same factory. `Database::connect_fresh()` is an opt-in test mode. Fresh and
restored outcomes must serialize to identical bytes. The local wire observer
retains SQLSTATE, message, detail and SQL statement before the public query API
converts a driver error to a refusal; it never changes the traffic.

Reads sweep every public boundary. Mutations compare every discovered position
twice on SQLite, and compare up to eight seeded positions per dimension on
Postgres (including first/last), plus the case's original budget. Sampling
counters are in `store_stats`; each selected position runs prefetch on and off.
Physical prefetch counters are separate from the exact public outcome record.
The widened generator (`src/widen.rs`) emits prefetch-reachable reads: pages
projecting `tags[.tag]` and `tags[name = .label]` (a `Get` and a unique
`Lookup` whose keys vary across rows) and a loop reading `tags[row.tag]`, so
`store_stats` reports marked reads, transferred rows, cases with a transfer
and `prefetch_consumption_witness_probes` (fewer database calls with prefetch
on: cached answers were consumed). A planted cached-hit-to-miss fault is caught
by the random corpus (LLP 3000.035 §5.1; the closed
[coverage ticket](../issues/closed/20261007-quarry-store-oracle-prefetch-coverage-gap.md)).
Heap is charged identically on every store and the device (LLP 3000.007
§20.24.5); any disagreement is a ticket candidate, never normalized into a pass.
The cluster regression asserts it finds no signature and refuses any new one.

`run-stores --seconds N` stops each worker between cases after N seconds; a case
and any shrinking already underway finish. `cases` remains the requested count,
`generated` is actual completed generation, and `time_limited_workers` reports
the early stop. Slowest-case timing includes generation, comparisons, shrinking
and artifact saving. It does not silently claim the requested million. The scheduled
runner rejects incomplete reports; its own `--hours` option instead chooses a
case count from a 256-case calibration of both campaigns, with 20% headroom and
a million-case cap. This is a sizing estimate, not a hard deadline.

Generator widening (`src/widen.rs`, LLP 3000.035 §5.1): from a stream
independent of the base generator (`generate::base`; a seed it leaves alone is
the base case byte for byte), cases gain prefetch-reachable reads, mutation
blocks (lets, `require`, `if`, several writes to `key` and `k2`, reads after
writes), a prelude of mutations `prep0..` run in order on the same store before
`run` (`Case::steps`, sometimes as the other viewer; `observe` then reads both
keys), and expiry (`expire at .until`, a clock advancing from `seeded_at`
through each step to `clock`, sweeps, `until: now + ttl`). Every oracle runs the
prelude (`execute::world`) and compares its outcomes (`prelude-*` fields;
`store-postgres-prelude`); a device acquires at the seed clock and catches up by
one sync round after the server's prelude. The prelude is emitted first so no
rewrite moves its sites. `lean::generate` narrows half the cases outside the
model (projections, expiry) back into its fragment; the rest stay named
unsupported (`shape`, `loop`, `table-expiry`). The report's `coverage` counts
each new feature (`block/*`, `prelude/*`, `projection/*`, `expire/*`, `clock/*`).

Quarry device testing (`src/devices.rs`, `src/device_writes.rs`, LLP 3000.035
§5.1 oracle 4): each case runs on a server engine and on a device of each store
(native SQLite at a file path, and memory), both brought up through the client's
own path: the `/schema` backend as JSON text, then `local.ts::syncRound` (capture
`sync_state`, `/sync` snapshot pages built as `serve_sync.rs` builds them,
`observe_store`, `apply` through `call_text`, catch-up stream). Never seed a
device store directly. Reads the device publishes without the server (`complete`
with `unknown` false, or a known refusal) compare the whole outcome record
except `seq` (and server-only `delivery`); every other read counts under a named
reason (`online-only-*`). Low ceilings reach the device only through `call`'s
`spent` ledger (`default - n`), compared at the server's boundary positions.
Writes go through `admit`: a device refusal must equal the server's, a
predicted row must equal the committed one (less its `pending` marker), and
after `settle` plus a sync round the device must answer `observe` as the server
does. `run-devices` accepts `--seconds`. Known findings are retained under
`cases/found/device-*` with a `DIAGNOSIS.md`; `tests/devices.rs` replays them
and `tests/smoke.rs` pins the fixed-seed signature set.

The Lean oracle (`run-lean`, LLP 3000.035 §5.2): `snapback4_lang::emit::lean`
translates each generated program's surface tree (never the binder's IR) to
the model's case JSON; `quarry-oracle` (`lean/Oracle.lean`, one process per
worker) answers one tagged outcome per line; the engine runs on SQLite at
default ceilings. Compared: kind, refusal code, data (tagged: an id is not a
text), `complete`, `next` presence, each side's own continuation, and a
mutation's `observe` reread. Not compared: meter fields and sites. Programs
outside the model's fragment are named unsupported reasons, never passes.
`lean::generate` adds Lean-only variants (per-effect rules, a hidden probe
target, nullable/id orders, residual filters) aimed at `lean/OPEN.md`. A
disagreement is a Rust defect, a model defect (only when a document
contradicts the model: fix the model, rerun `lean/check.sh`), or an open
point (record the engine's behaviour in `OPEN.md`); never change the model to
match the engine without a document. Findings live in
`cases/found/lean--*` with `DIAGNOSIS.md`; `tests/lean.rs` replays them and
pins the open signature set. Lean-dependent tests skip loudly without the
built model; `SNAPBACK4_REQUIRE_LEAN=1` makes that a failure.

Quarry scheduled testing: prepare a separate clean clone whose current branch
can fast-forward to `origin/main`. The runner fetches/fast-forwards it and starts
its own socket-only evaluation cluster with `fsync=off`,
`synchronous_commit=off`, `full_page_writes=off`, `shared_buffers=1GB`, and
`max_connections=200`. It builds the Lean model's `quarry-oracle` first

**Revised:** 2026-10-22 (K0-Q fix round 1: reserve view-once consumption before byte delivery under bounded committer admission); 2026-10-13 (FLAKE: isolate real-timer client fixtures and keep quote layout pooled); ## Summary

Snapback 4 is one of two parallel candidates for the successor to Snapback 2,
and the sibling of Snapback 3, not its successor. It builds
[LLP 2000.000](./2000.000-keep-the-core-flip-two-substrates.rfc.md) as
written: the Snapback 2 core kept verbatim, two substrates flipped up front —
resources metered at runtime instead of priced statically, and a client that
replicates the viewer's declared partition instead of caching receipted
results — proved first on the hub fixture, with a deterministic simulator as
the primary harness. Snapback 3 ([LLP 2000](./2000-snapback3.rfc.md), led by
Codex / GPT-6 Astra) keeps the same two questions open and discovers their
answers in its first slice. Snapback 2 ([LLP 1000](./1000-snapback2.explainer.md))
continues as the incumbent and the control. All three are judged by one
pre-registered comparison on one clock.

This document records the author's decisions of 2026-09-12 that created the
track, restates the bet on one page so a reader can orient without the brief,
fixes the bakeoff protocol, draws the fence, and plans the first four weeks.
The design argument and its evidence stay in LLP 2000.000. Only running code
will bind (`rules/RULES.md`).

## 1. Decisions (author, 2026-09-12)

On 2026-09-12 Charlie Cheever asked whether Claude Fable 5.1 should lead a
Snapback 4 while Codex / GPT-6 Astra leads Snapback 3 and Snapback 2
continues, "and see which one turns out best." The lead answered yes with
conditions; the author accepted them ("Sounds good") and asked for this
document. The decisions below are therefore the lead's proposal as accepted
by the author. Where the author has not separately ruled, the text says so.

1. **Three tracks, one comparison.** Snapback 2 continues as the incumbent
   and the control. Snapback 3 builds LLP 2000; Snapback 4 builds LLP
   2000.000. This is a bakeoff of *bets* — discover the substrates in the
   slice versus decide them first — not of models. The result will say more
   about the bets than about the leads, and §3 is written so that it can.
2. **The name is Snapback 4.** Siblings, not a sequence: "4" does not mean
   "after 3." A separate number gives the track its own corpus root,
   directory, package identity and data directory with no rename when the
   bakeoff resolves, and files never move in LLP. If Snapback 4 loses, it is
   a number that never shipped. "Snapback 3b" was considered and rejected:
   it reads as a variant of 3, and this is a different bet.
3. **Snapback 4 builds LLP 2000.000 as written.** Its §14 open questions are
   the lead's to decide inside the track, recorded in §7 as they are
   decided. The author rules on the bar, the comparison and promotion (§3).
4. **One owner on the core.** The lead writes the engine, the store and the
   client contract. Codex lanes build the fixture generator, the reference
   applications, the device witnesses and the periphery. Blind reviews from
   two model families are reserved for the two disclosure-sensitive
   mechanisms: partition inversion of read rules (LLP 2000.000 §7.1) and
   the meter's cut behaviour on non-uniform scans (LLP 2000.000 §6). This
   departs from the standing rule that Codex codes and Claude orchestrates,
   deliberately, for the core only.
5. **The clock is four weeks** from the first commit under `snapback4/` to
   the first slice running on the hub fixture. At the clock the author kills
   the track, merges its substrates into a survivor, or continues it. There
   is no third month without a ruling.
6. **Shared and not shared.** Shared with Snapback 3: the LLP corpus, the
   Clique harness and scorers, the fixtures, the briefs, and the simulator
   once either track has one. Not shared: code, except the Snapback 2 pieces
   both are expected to copy (LLP 2000.000 §5.1).
7. **Snapback 2 keeps shipping and freezes new substrate work for the
   duration.** Fix-forward, the loop bar, acquisition and papercuts continue;
   no new certificate axes, client custody mechanisms or ceiling changes.
   This is accepted here as a condition of the bakeoff. Making it binding on
   concurrent Snapback 2 sessions needs one line in `rules/NOT-DOING.md`'s
   Moves ledger, which this document does not write.
8. **Reading load.** This root, updated in place, and one append-only
   journal, `snapback4/JOURNAL.md`. Sub-LLPs only for a decision that must
   outlive the journal. No panels beyond decision 4's two reviews.

const fetchIdentity = async (url: string, timeoutMs: number) => {
  const response = await fetch(url, { signal: AbortSignal.timeout(timeoutMs) });
  return { response, body: await response.json() as Record<string, unknown> };
};
async function nativeHost(verb: "dev" | "serve", prefix: string[], options: ProofOptions): Promise<ProofHost & { personas?: string[] }> {
  const env = { ...process.env, ...options.env }, id = randomUUID();
  const ports = portRange(env), timeout = deadline(options.timeoutMs, 30_000);
  const args = [...(options.args ?? [])];
  if (!args.includes("--port")) args.push("--port", ports);
  const run = logged(resolveBinary(env), [verb, ...prefix, ...args], { ...options, processGroup: true, env: { ...env, SNAPBACK4_PROOF_ID: id, SNAPBACK4_SUPERVISOR_PID: String(process.pid) } });
  try {
    await run.started;
    const url = await ready(run, line => {
      const match = line.match(/^SNAPBACK4_READY verb=(dev|serve) generation=\d+ url=(http:\/\/[^ ]+) pid=(\d+)(?: |$)/);
      if (!match) return;
      if (match[1] !== verb || Number(match[3]) !== run.pid()) throw Error("proof ready identity mismatch");
      return match[2];
    }, timeout);
    const { response, body } = await fetchIdentity(`${url}/auth/config`, timeout);
    if (!response.ok || body.proof_id !== id) throw Error("proof served identity mismatch");
    if (verb === "dev" && body.devDoors === false) process.stderr.write("snapback4: password-only development doors CLOSED (/seed, /dev/*, personas). For shared previews and probe:serve run snapback4 deploy staging --staging, then startServe(\"staging\"); see snapback4 guide staging. Local fixtures require dev --unsafe-dev-doors.\n");
    let personas: string[] = [];
    if (verb === "dev" && body.devDoors !== false) {
      const status = await fetchIdentity(`${url}/dev/status`, timeout);
      if (status.body.pid !== run.pid()) throw Error("proof dev PID mismatch");
      if (!Array.isArray(status.body.personas) || !status.body.personas.every(name => typeof name === "string")) throw Error("proof dev personas missing");
      personas = status.body.personas;
    }
    return { url, ...(verb === "dev" ? { personas } : {}), pid: run.pid()!, logs: run.logs, stop: () => stopChild(run.child, deadline(options.stopTimeoutMs, 5000)) };
  } catch (error) { await stopChild(run.child, deadline(options.stopTimeoutMs, 5000)); throw error; }
}
export interface DevHost extends ProofHost {
  personas: string[];
  /** Read the current authoritative development time, including all clock advances. */
  now(): Promise<number>;
  advance(duration: string, options?: AdvanceOptions): Promise<ClockAdvance>;
  drain(options?: Pick<AdvanceOptions, "eachBoundary" | "allowHeld">): Promise<ClockAdvance>;
  operatorToken(service?: string, days?: number): Promise<string>;
}
/** The array form preserves the former startDev(args) recipe. */
/** @throws invalid port range or conflicting memory flags; child exits, readiness deadline, PID/served identity mismatch, or unavailable dev clock. */
export function startDev(options: ProofOptions | readonly string[] = {}): Promise<DevHost> {
  const opts: ProofOptions = Array.isArray(options) ? { args: options } : options as ProofOptions;
  const args = [...(opts.args ?? [])];
  const persistent = args.some(arg => arg === "--store" || arg.startsWith("--store=") || arg === "--no-memory");
  if (persistent && args.includes("--memory")) throw new TypeError("proof startDev: --memory conflicts with --store/--no-memory");
  const env = { ...process.env, ...options.env }, ports = portRange(env), timeout = deadline(options.timeoutMs, 30_000);
  const cwd = resolve(options.cwd ?? ".");
  const directory = resolve(cwd, dir);
  // Vite is resolved from the app, never the proof package's development dependencies.
  const vite = kind === "vite" ? createRequire(resolve(directory, "package.json")).resolve("vite") : "";
  const run = logged(process.execPath, [fileURLToPath(new URL("./proof-host.js", import.meta.url)), kind, directory, ports, vite], { ...options, env, detached: process.platform !== "win32", livenessPipe: true });
  try {
    const url = await ready(run, line => {
      const match = line.match(/^SNAPBACK4_PROOF_READY (\{.*\})$/);
      if (!match) return;
      const data = JSON.parse(match[1]!);
      if (data.pid !== run.child.pid || data.kind !== kind) throw Error("proof frontend PID mismatch");
      return data.url as string;
    }, timeout);
    const response = await fetch(url, { signal: AbortSignal.timeout(timeout) });
    if (response.headers.get("x-snapback4-proof") !== String(run.child.pid) + ":" + run.id) throw Error("proof frontend identity mismatch");
    await response.body?.cancel();
    return { url, pid: run.child.pid!, logs: run.logs, stop: () => stopChild(run.child, deadline(options.stopTimeoutMs, 5000)) };
  } catch (error) { await stopChild(run.child, deadline(options.stopTimeoutMs, 5000)); throw error; }
}
/** @throws missing export/index or build script precondition; exhausted ports, readiness deadline or served identity mismatch. */
export function startStatic(dir: string, options: ProofOptions = {}): Promise<ProofHost> { return frontend("static", dir, options); }
/** @throws missing Vite dependency/configuration; exhausted ports, readiness deadline or served identity mismatch. */
export function startVite(dir: string, options: ProofOptions = {}): Promise<ProofHost> { return frontend("vite", dir, options); }

export interface CliResult { code: number | null; signal: NodeJS.Signals | null; stdout: string; stderr: string; logs: ProofHost["logs"] }
/** Waits for close (stdio drained), never merges the two streams. Nonzero is returned for refusal assertions. */
/** @throws spawn error or command deadline; nonzero exit is returned in code, not thrown. */
export function cli(args: readonly string[], options: ProofOptions = {}): Promise<CliResult> {
  return command(process.execPath, [fileURLToPath(new URL("../bin/snapback4.js", import.meta.url)), ...args], { ...options, secret: options.secret || args[0] === "token" });
}
/** A bounded directly spawned build/tool command, with the same file logs. */
/** @throws spawn error or command deadline; nonzero exit is returned in code, not thrown. */
export async function command(binary: string, args: readonly string[], options: ProofOptions = {}): Promise<CliResult> {
  const run = logged(binary, args, { ...options, processGroup: true, env: { ...process.env, ...options.env } });
  try {
    await bounded(run.closed, deadline(options.timeoutMs, 120_000), "proof cli close");
    if (run.error()) throw run.error();
    return { code: run.code(), signal: run.signal(), stdout: run.stdout(), stderr: logText(run.logs.stderr), logs: run.logs };
  } catch (error) { await stopChild(run.child, deadline(options.stopTimeoutMs, 5000)); throw error; }
  finally { run.child.stdout?.destroy(); } // Secret capture must not pin the driver after a deadline.
}
async function successful(args: string[], options: ProofOptions) {
  const result = await cli(args, options);
  if (result.code !== 0) {
    const stdout = result.stdout.trimEnd();
    const tail = stdout.length > 4000 ? `…${stdout.slice(-4000)}` : stdout;
    throw Error(`proof CLI failed (${result.code}): ${result.stderr}; stdout tail: ${tail || "(empty)"}; logs: ${result.logs.stderr}`);
  }
  return result;
}
/** @throws missing explicit saved source, CLI failure, spawn error or deadline. */
export function deploy(dir: string, schema: string, options: ProofOptions = {}) {
  if (typeof schema !== "string" || !schema) throw Error(`proof deploy requires an explicit saved schema file or directory; got ${JSON.stringify(schema) ?? String(schema)}: did npm run day2:proof pass versions/v1?`);
  return successful(["deploy", dir, "--schema", schema, ...(options.args ?? [])], options);
}
/** @throws invalid explicit release target, CLI failure, spawn error or deadline. */
export function rollback(dir: string, to: number | "latest", options: ProofOptions = {}) {
  if (to !== "latest" && (!Number.isSafeInteger(to) || to < 1)) throw Error(`proof rollback requires an explicit immutable release target; got ${JSON.stringify(to) ?? String(to)}`);
  return successful(["rollback", dir, "--to", String(to), ...(options.args ?? [])], options);
}
// @ref LLP 3000.011.003.000.000#11-wave-14-truthful-starter-custody-day4 — saved-source proof results expose authored cases separately from query sweeps.
export interface TestSourceOptions extends ProofOptions { fixtureOnly?: boolean }
export interface TestSourceResult extends CliResult { casesRun: number; casesSkipped: number }
/** @throws missing source, CLI failure, invalid business-case summary or zero cases without fixtureOnly. */
export function testSource(source: string, options: TestSourceOptions = {}): Promise<TestSourceResult> {
  if (typeof source !== "string" || !source) throw Error(`proof testSource requires an explicit saved source file or directory; got ${JSON.stringify(source) ?? String(source)}: pass versions/v1`);
  const { fixtureOnly = false, ...proofOptions } = options;
  return successful(["test", "--source", source, ...(fixtureOnly ? ["--fixture-only"] : []), ...(proofOptions.args ?? [])], proofOptions).then(result => {
    const summaries = [...result.stdout.matchAll(/^SNAPBACK4_TEST_CASES (\{.*\})$/gm)];
    if (summaries.length !== 1) throw Error(`proof testSource received no unique business-case summary; stdout tail: ${result.stdout.trimEnd().slice(-4000) || "(empty)"}; logs: ${result.logs.stdout}`);
    let cases: { run: number; skipped: number };
    try { cases = JSON.parse(summaries[0]![1]!) as { run: number; skipped: number }; }
    catch { throw Error(`proof testSource received a malformed business-case summary: ${summaries[0]![0]}; logs: ${result.logs.stdout}`); }
    if (!Number.isSafeInteger(cases.run) || cases.run < 0 || !Number.isSafeInteger(cases.skipped) || cases.skipped < 0) {
      throw Error(`proof testSource received invalid business-case counts: ${summaries[0]![0]}; logs: ${result.logs.stdout}`);
    }
    if (cases.run === 0 && !fixtureOnly) {
      throw Error(`proof testSource ran no business cases (${cases.skipped} skipped); add test.cases.jsonl for this saved version, or pass { fixtureOnly: true } when fixture loading alone is the intended proof; stdout tail: ${result.stdout.trimEnd().slice(-4000) || "(empty)"}`);
    }
    return { ...result, casesRun: cases.run, casesSkipped: cases.skipped };
  });
}

//! @ref LLP 3000.000.000.000#11-old-client-operation-contracts-rel5 — compare old callers with the candidate, separately from data adoption.
use crate::{Bound, BoundProgram, types::Ty};
use snapback4_core::ir::{ArgType as A, ProgramKind};
use std::collections::BTreeMap;

/// Conservative input-domain inclusion: every value accepted by old is accepted by new.
pub fn accepts(old: &A, new: &A) -> bool {
    accepts_in(old,new,None,None)
}

fn accepts_in(old: &A, new: &A, old_bound: Option<&Bound>, new_bound: Option<&Bound>) -> bool {
    if old==new {return true;}
    match (old,new) {
        (A::Optional(a),A::Optional(b))=>accepts_in(a,b,old_bound,new_bound),
        (A::Optional(_),_)=>false,
        (a,A::Optional(b))=>accepts_in(a,b,old_bound,new_bound),
        (A::Ref{table:old},A::Ref{table:new})=>new_bound.is_some_and(|bound|bound.schema.table_renames.get(new)==Some(old))
            && old_bound.is_some_and(|bound|bound.schema.table(old).is_some()),
        (A::Text{min:a,max:b,format:af},A::Text{min:c,max:d,format:bf})=>c<=a && d>=b && (bf.is_none() || af==bf),
        (A::IntRange{..},A::Int)=>true,
        (A::IntRange{min:a,max:b},A::IntRange{min:c,max:d})=>c<=a && d>=b,
        (A::Enum{variants:a},A::Enum{variants:b})=>a.iter().all(|v|b.contains(v)),
        (A::Bytes{max:a},A::Bytes{max:b}) | (A::Image{max:a},A::Image{max:b}) |
        (A::Video{max:a},A::Video{max:b})=>b>=a,
        (A::Json{max_bytes:a},A::Json{max_bytes:b})=>b>=a,
        (A::File{max:a,types:at},A::File{max:b,types:bt})=>b>=a && snapback4_core::assets::FILE_TYPES.iter().all(|t|!snapback4_core::assets::file_accepts(at,t)||snapback4_core::assets::file_accepts(bt,t)),
        (A::List{..}|A::ListRange{..},A::List{..}|A::ListRange{..})=> {
            let parts=|t:&A|match t {A::List{of,max}=>(0,*max,of.clone()),A::ListRange{of,min,max}=>(*min,*max,of.clone()),_=>unreachable!()};
            let (a,b,at)=parts(old);let(c,d,bt)=parts(new);
            c<=a && d>=b && match(at,bt){(_,None)=>true,(Some(a),Some(b))=>accepts_in(&a,&b,old_bound,new_bound),_=>false}
        }
        _=>false,
    }
}

#[derive(Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
enum ResultChange { Same, Additive, Changed }

fn combine(fields: impl IntoIterator<Item=ResultChange>, extra: bool) -> ResultChange {
    let mut additive=extra;
    for field in fields {
        match field {ResultChange::Changed=>return ResultChange::Changed,ResultChange::Additive=>additive=true,ResultChange::Same=>{}}
    }
    if additive {ResultChange::Additive} else {ResultChange::Same}
}

fn table_fields(name: &str, bound: &Bound) -> Option<BTreeMap<String,String>> {
    let schema=bound.schema.public_projection();
    let table=schema.tables.get(name)?;
    Some(table.columns.iter().filter(|(n,_)|!table.engine_field(n)).map(|(n,t)|(n.clone(),format!("{t:?};private={};constraints={:?}",table.read_optional(n),table.constraints.iter().filter(|c|matches!(c,snapback4_core::schema::Constraint::Text{field,..} if field==n)).collect::<Vec<_>>()))).collect())
}

fn map_change<T>(old: &BTreeMap<String,T>, new: &BTreeMap<String,T>, compare: impl Fn(&T,&T)->ResultChange) -> ResultChange {
    combine(old.iter().map(|(name,old)|new.get(name).map(|new|compare(old,new)).unwrap_or(ResultChange::Changed)),new.len()>old.len())
}

fn result_change(old: &Ty, new: &Ty, old_bound: &Bound, new_bound: &Bound) -> ResultChange {
    if result(old,old_bound)==result(new,new_bound) {return ResultChange::Same;}
    match (old,new) {
        (Ty::Optional(a),Ty::Optional(b))|(Ty::Omittable(a),Ty::Omittable(b))
        |(Ty::List(a,_),Ty::List(b,_))|(Ty::Collection(a,_),Ty::Collection(b,_))=>result_change(a,b,old_bound,new_bound),
        (Ty::Object(a)|Ty::RowShape(a)|Ty::RowObject(a),Ty::Object(b)|Ty::RowShape(b)|Ty::RowObject(b))=>map_change(a,b,|a,b|result_change(a,b,old_bound,new_bound)),
        (Ty::Row(a)|Ty::WriteRow(a)|Ty::Page(a,_),Ty::Row(b)|Ty::WriteRow(b)|Ty::Page(b,_)) if a==b=>{
            let (Some(a),Some(b))=(table_fields(a,old_bound),table_fields(b,new_bound)) else{return ResultChange::Changed;};
            combine(a.iter().map(|(name,kind)|if b.get(name)==Some(kind){ResultChange::Same}else{ResultChange::Changed}),b.len()>a.len())
        }
        _=>ResultChange::Changed,
    }
}

fn action_result_change(old: Option<&BTreeMap<String,A>>, new: Option<&BTreeMap<String,A>>) -> ResultChange {
    match (old,new) {
        (None,None)=>ResultChange::Same,
        (Some(old),Some(new))=>combine(old.iter().map(|(name,kind)|if new.get(name)==Some(kind){ResultChange::Same}else{ResultChange::Changed}),new.len()>old.len()),
        _=>ResultChange::Changed,
    }
}

pub fn is_note(line: &str) -> bool { line.contains(": RESULT NOTE:") }

    let mut lines=Vec::new();
    for before in old.programs.iter().filter(|p|p.program.kind!=ProgramKind::Job) {
        let name=&before.program.name;
        let prefix=format!("old-client compatibility ({label}), operation {name}");
        let Some(after)=new.programs.iter().find(|p|p.program.name==*name) else {
            lines.push(format!("{prefix}: INPUT BREAK: removed operation; old calls get E_OP; keep `alias {name} = replacement` for a same-module query/mutation, or retain a wrapper"));continue;
        };
        if before.program.kind!=after.program.kind {lines.push(format!("{prefix}: INPUT BREAK: operation kind changed; retain the old endpoint kind"));continue;}
        let compatible_rename = |new_name: &str, new_kind: &A| before.program.args.iter().find(|(old_name, old_kind)|
            !after.program.args.contains_key(*old_name) && accepts_in(old_kind,new_kind,Some(old),Some(new)) &&
            after.program.arg_aliases.get(*old_name).is_none_or(|canonical|canonical==new_name));
        for (arg,kind) in &after.program.args {
            if before.program.args.contains_key(arg) { continue; }
            if let Some((old_arg,old_kind))=before.program.args.iter().find(|(old,_)|after.program.arg_aliases.get(*old)==Some(arg)) {
                if !accepts_in(old_kind,kind,Some(old),Some(new)) {lines.push(format!("{prefix}: INPUT BREAK: aliased argument {old_arg} → {arg} narrows the old input domain ({old_kind:?} → {kind:?}); retain the old input domain or a compatible wrapper"));}
            } else if !matches!(kind,A::Optional(_)) {
                if let Some((old,_))=compatible_rename(arg,kind) {
                    lines.push(format!("{prefix}: INPUT BREAK: required argument {old} appears renamed to {arg}; old calls get E_INPUT; declare `{arg}: <new type> alias {old}` if it is the same value, or retain a compatible wrapper"));
                } else {
                    lines.push(format!("{prefix}: INPUT BREAK: clients calling without new required argument {arg} get E_INPUT; give {arg} a fitting `default <literal>` or retain a compatible wrapper"));
                }
            }
        }
        for (arg,kind) in &before.program.args {
            match after.program.args.get(arg) {
                None if after.program.arg_aliases.contains_key(arg)=>{
                    let next=&after.program.args[&after.program.arg_aliases[arg]];
                    if !accepts_in(kind,next,Some(old),Some(new)){lines.push(format!("{prefix}: INPUT BREAK: aliased argument {arg} retyped or narrowed ({kind:?} → {next:?}); retain its old input domain"));}
                },
                None if after.program.args.iter().any(|(new,next)|compatible_rename(new,next).is_some_and(|(old,_)|old==arg))=>{},
                None=>lines.push(format!("{prefix}: INPUT BREAK: removed argument {arg}; old calls sending it get E_INPUT; retain `{arg}: <old type> deprecated` on a query/mutation")),
                Some(next) if !accepts_in(kind,next,Some(old),Some(new))=>lines.push(format!("{prefix}: INPUT BREAK: argument {arg} retyped or narrowed ({kind:?} → {next:?}); old values may get E_INPUT; retain the old input domain or a compatible wrapper")),
                _=>{}
            }
        }
        for (alias,canonical) in &before.program.arg_aliases {
            let old_kind=&before.program.args[canonical];
            let next=after.program.args.get(alias).or_else(||after.program.arg_aliases.get(alias).and_then(|name|after.program.args.get(name)));
            match next {
                None=>lines.push(format!("{prefix}: INPUT BREAK: removed argument alias {alias}; old calls sending it get E_INPUT; retain its alias or a compatible wrapper")),
                Some(kind) if !accepts_in(old_kind,kind,Some(old),Some(new))=>lines.push(format!("{prefix}: INPUT BREAK: argument alias {alias} retyped or narrowed ({old_kind:?} → {kind:?}); retain its old input domain")),
                _=>{}
            }
        }
        let mut positions:BTreeMap<String,String>=BTreeMap::new();
        for canonical in before.program.args.keys() {
            for spelling in std::iter::once(canonical).chain(before.program.arg_aliases.iter().filter_map(|(alias,target)|(target==canonical).then_some(alias))) {
                let target=after.program.args.contains_key(spelling).then_some(spelling).or_else(||after.program.arg_aliases.get(spelling));
                if let Some(target)=target {
                    if let Some(prior)=positions.insert(target.clone(),canonical.clone())&&prior!=*canonical {
                        lines.push(format!("{prefix}: INPUT BREAK: independent arguments {prior} and {canonical} now share {target}; old calls containing both positions get E_INPUT; retain separate arguments or a compatible wrapper"));
                    }
                }
            }
        }
        for (table,before_table) in old.schema.visible_tables() {
            let Some(after_table)=new.schema.table(table) else { continue; };
            if !snapback4_core::ir::read_sites(&before.program).iter().any(|site|site.table==*table)
                && !snapback4_core::ir::write_tables(&before.program).iter().any(|(name,_)|name==table) { continue; }
            for field in before_table.columns.keys().filter(|f|!before_table.read_optional(f)&&after_table.read_optional(f)) {
                lines.push(format!("{prefix}: RESULT REVIEW: {table}.{field} is now audience-optional; handle an omitted row property or a null explicit scalar before adopting the new column read rule"));
            }
        }
        let action_change=action_result_change(old.schema.action_specs.get(name).and_then(|s|s.returns.as_ref()),new.schema.action_specs.get(name).and_then(|s|s.returns.as_ref()));
        let typed_change=result_change(&before.result,&after.result,old,new);
        let change=combine([action_change,typed_change],false);
        match change {
            ResultChange::Additive=>lines.push(format!("{prefix}: RESULT NOTE: result shape added fields while preserving prior fields and domains; old clients may ignore the additions")),
            ResultChange::Changed=>lines.push(format!("{prefix}: RESULT REVIEW: result shape/value domain changed; preserve the old projection/domain or introduce a new operation before retiring old clients")),
            ResultChange::Same=>{},
        }
        let old_domains=crate::result_domains::signature(before,old);
        let new_domains=crate::result_domains::signature(after,new);
        // A domain introduced only for a newly returned field is covered by
        // the additive result note. Existing or removed domains still need review.
        let changed:Vec<_>=old_domains.keys().collect::<std::collections::BTreeSet<_>>().into_iter().filter(|key|old_domains.get(*key)!=new_domains.get(*key)).cloned().collect();
        if !changed.is_empty() {lines.push(format!("{prefix}: RESULT REVIEW: declared source domains consumed by this operation changed: {}; results or refusals may change even when the TypeScript type stays number/string; preserve the old domains or verify old-client handling",changed.join(", ")));}
        if refusals(before)!=refusals(after) {lines.push(format!("{prefix}: BEHAVIOR REVIEW: refusal codes/messages changed ({:?} → {:?}); verify intended denials separately from input breaks",refusals(before),refusals(after)));}
    }
    lines
}

// One resolver for the CLI shim and the Node device opener.
import { existsSync } from "node:fs";
import { createRequire } from "node:module";
import { dirname, join, resolve } from "node:path";
import process from "node:process";
import { fileURLToPath } from "node:url";

export function resolveBinary(env: Record<string, string | undefined> = process.env): string {
  const here = dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url));
  const require = createRequire(import.meta.url);
  // @ref LLP 3000.021#3.3 — the installed platform package precedes checkout fallbacks
  const platform = `snapback4-${process.platform}-${process.arch}`;
  const candidates: string[] = [];
  if (env.SNAPBACK4_BIN) candidates.push(resolve(env.SNAPBACK4_BIN));
  try { candidates.push(join(dirname(require.resolve(`${platform}/package.json`)), "snapback4")); } catch { /* not installed for this platform */ }
  candidates.push(resolve(here, "../../../target/release/snapback4"), resolve(here, "../../../target/debug/snapback4"));
  const binary = candidates.find((path) => existsSync(path));
  if (!binary) throw new Error(`snapback4: no binary for ${process.platform}-${process.arch}. Packages exist for darwin-arm64 and linux-x64; elsewhere, build with \`cargo build --release -p snapback4\` and set SNAPBACK4_BIN.`);
  return binary;
}

   102	export function portRange(env: NodeJS.ProcessEnv = process.env): string {
   103	  const low = env.PORT_LO, high = env.PORT_HI;
   104	  if (low === undefined && high === undefined) return "0";
   105	  if (!low || !high || !/^\d+$/.test(low) || !/^\d+$/.test(high)

   107	    throw Error("PORT_LO and PORT_HI must both specify an inclusive range, 1 <= low <= high <= 65535");
   108	  }
   109	  return `${Number(low)}-${Number(high)}`;

   152	export async function ready(run: ReturnType<typeof logged>, parse: (line: string) => string | undefined, timeoutMs: number): Promise<string> {
   153	  const end = performance.now() + timeoutMs;
   154	  let outAt = 0, errAt = 0;
   155	  while (performance.now() < end) {
   156	    if (run.error()) throw run.error();
   157	    if (run.child.exitCode !== null || run.child.signalCode !== null) {
   158	      throw Error(`proof child exited before ready (${run.child.exitCode ?? run.child.signalCode}); ${run.logs.stderr}\n${logText(run.logs.stderr).slice(-4000)}`);
   159	    }
   160	    for (const [path, offset, stream] of [[run.logs.stdout, outAt, "out"], [run.logs.stderr, errAt, "err"]] as const) {
   161	      const text = logText(path);
   162	      const complete = text.lastIndexOf("\n") + 1;

   357	export interface ExhaustPagesOptions {
   358	  /** Refuse rather than loop forever when a backend returns bad continuations. */
   359	  maxPages?: number;
   360	  /** Names the read in a refusal without exposing its rows or cursor. */
   361	  label?: string;
   362	}
   363	type ProofPage<Row> =
   364	  | { readonly data: readonly Row[]; readonly next?: Cursor | null; readonly complete?: boolean; readonly cut?: string | null }
   365	  | { readonly denied: Refusal }
   366	  | { readonly loading: true };
   367	/** Read every page by passing each opaque `next` back unchanged. The callback
   368	 * receives null first. Refusals, loading, malformed/repeated cursors and a
   369	 * capped or incomplete page fail instead of returning a partial snapshot. */
   370	/** @throws E_PROOF_PAGE for a refused, loading, malformed, repeated, incomplete or over-limit traversal; callback errors propagate. */
   371	// @ref LLP 3000.000.000.002#10-wave-25-day-two-friction-dx8 — proof traversal must never promote capped or partial reads to complete evidence.
   372	export async function exhaustPages<Row>(readPage: (cursor: Cursor | null) => Promise<ProofPage<Row>>, options: ExhaustPagesOptions = {}): Promise<Row[]> {
   373	  const maxPages = options.maxPages ?? 1_000, label = options.label ?? "paged read";
   374	  if (!Number.isSafeInteger(maxPages) || maxPages < 1) throw new Error(`E_PROOF_PAGE: ${label}: maxPages must be a positive safe integer`);
   375	  const rows: Row[] = [], seen = new Set<string>();
   376	  let cursor: Cursor | null = null;
   377	  for (let pageNumber = 1; pageNumber <= maxPages; pageNumber++) {
   378	    const page = await readPage(cursor);
   379	    if (!page || typeof page !== "object") throw new Error(`E_PROOF_PAGE: ${label}: page ${pageNumber} returned no read envelope`);
   380	    if ("denied" in page) throw new Error(`E_PROOF_PAGE: ${label}: page ${pageNumber} refused ${page.denied.code}: ${page.denied.message}`);
   381	    if ("loading" in page) throw new Error(`E_PROOF_PAGE: ${label}: page ${pageNumber} is still loading`);
   382	    if (!Array.isArray(page.data)) throw new Error(`E_PROOF_PAGE: ${label}: page ${pageNumber} data is not an array`);
   383	    const cut = page.cut ?? null;
   384	    if (page.complete === false || cut !== null) throw new Error(`E_PROOF_PAGE: ${label}: page ${pageNumber} is incomplete${cut === null ? "" : ` (${cut} cut)`}`);
   385	    rows.push(...page.data);
   386	    const next = page.next ?? null;
   387	    if (next === null) return rows;
   388	    if (typeof next !== "string" || next.length === 0) throw new Error(`E_PROOF_PAGE: ${label}: page ${pageNumber} returned a malformed cursor`);
   389	    if (seen.has(next)) throw new Error(`E_PROOF_PAGE: ${label}: page ${pageNumber} returned a repeated cursor`);
   390	    seen.add(next);
   391	    cursor = next as Cursor;
   392	  }
   393	  throw new Error(`E_PROOF_PAGE: ${label}: exceeded maxPages ${maxPages}`);
   394	}

   396	/** @throws page closed or browser activation failed. */
   397	export async function frontPage(page: Page): Promise<Page> { await page.bringToFront(); return page; }

codex
NOT READY

The supplied amendment hash matches §10 at frozen commit `78c08eaa47e6cfa8ab25aedee30a9940c4188cd9`. Three material concerns remain:

1. **Paging accepts absent completeness evidence.** [proof.ts:364](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-b25-dx8/snapback4/packages/snapback4/src/proof.ts:364) makes `complete` and `cut` optional; the implementation rejects only explicit incompleteness. Counterexample: `exhaustPages(async () => ({data: firstPage, next: null}))` succeeds without any coverage evidence. An in-memory probe confirmed this. Require affirmative completeness metadata and reject missing or malformed evidence. Clarify that exhausting a declared paginated query does not prove an arbitrary capped query covers the entire population.

2. **Process failure handling is not fully bounded.**

   - [proof.ts:31](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-b25-dx8/snapback4/packages/snapback4/src/proof.ts:31) awaits `run.started` before entering readiness timeout handling. An existing but non-executable `SNAPBACK4_BIN` passes binary resolution, then fails spawning without supplying a numeric started PID. Nothing settles `started`, so the requested timeout never applies.
   - [proof-process.ts:93](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-b25-dx8/snapback4/packages/snapback4/src/proof-process.ts:93) races infinite cleanup polling against five seconds without cancelling the poll. A group remaining visible—for example because of unreaped members—causes rejection while referenced timers continue indefinitely. A mocked persistent-group probe confirmed polling continues after timeout.

   Reject startup on error/early close, include the startup handshake within the deadline, and make cleanup polling terminate when its deadline expires.

3. **Provisioning contradicts the promised staging support.** [proof-accounts.ts:40](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-b25-dx8/snapback4/packages/snapback4/src/proof-accounts.ts:40) requires one exact environment, and `provisionAccounts` selects production. Counterexample: a valid password-authenticated staging host receives `E_PRODUCTION_ACCOUNTS` before provisioning begins; an in-memory probe confirmed this. Accept both production and staging through ordinary signup and authorized mutations, while retaining development refusal, and align the guide and regression coverage.

The generated-rename exception retains type/protected-cell checks and transactional receipt staging. Development clock authorization checks the exact operator seat and revalidates authority under the writer.

These findings do not depend on pending fan-out extraction or full qualification. No files were modified.
